O Indra, lover of horses, do not unyoke your horses from the chariot while far from our place of sacrifice. Come graciously with them, facing us. O Indra, possessor of food in the form of Soma, be satisfied in this place of sacrifice by drinking Soma.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Lover of horses, Indra, loosen not your steeds while far from us, but come (with them) to our presence : enjoyer of the offering, be exhilarated at this sacrifice.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

हरिप्रिय (*haripriya*)—Hari means the two horses of Indra. Because Indra is fond of them, Indra is here addressed by the word hari-priya.
अस्मत् मा आरे वि मुमुचः (*asmat mā āre vi mumucaḥ*)—O Indra, do not unyoke your horses from the chariot far from us. That is, keep your horses yoked to the chariot until you reach our neighbourhood. Seated in the chariot, unyoke the horses after you have come near us. Do not stop the journey midway by unyoking the horses before then. So Indra is entreated.
#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒र्वाञ्चं॑ त्वा सु॒खे रथे॒ वह॑तामिंद्र के॒शिना॑ ।  
> घृ॒तस्नू॑ ब॒र्हिरा॒सदे॑ ॥ ९ ॥

> *arvāñcaṃ tvā sukhe rathe vahatāmiṃdra keśinā |*  
> *ghṛtasnū barhirāsade ‖ 9 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अर्वाञ्छं । त्वा । सुखे । रथे । वहतां । इंद्र । केशिना ।  
> घृतस्नू इति घृतस्नू । बर्हिः । आसदे ॥ ९ ॥

> *arvāñchaṃ | tvā | sukhe | rathe | vahatāṃ | iṃdra | keśinā |*  
> *ghṛtasnū iti ghṛtasnū | barhiḥ | āsade ‖ 9 ‖*

### Printed p. 36

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र घृतस्नू श्रमजनितजलप्रस्रवणयुक्तौ केशिना केशिनौ स्कंधप्रदेशे
> लंबमानकेशयुक्तौ हरी आसद आसदनीयं बर्हिरभिलक्ष्य सुखे सुखकरे रथेऽर्वांच मस्तदभिमुखं त्वा त्वां वहतां ॥ अर्वांचं । अंचतेः क्विन्युगिदचामिति
> नुम् । वहतां । वहेर्लोटि रूपं । तसो लसार्वधातुकस्वरे कृते धातुस्वरः ।
> आसदे । सदेः कृत्यार्थे केन्प्रत्ययः । नित्सरः । कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, let your two horses, घृतस्नू (*ghṛtasnū*), which have a flow of water produced by exertion, and केशिना (*keśinā*), which have hair hanging down on the shoulder region, bring you (वहतां (*vahatāṃ*)) facing us (अर्वांचं (*arvāṃcaṃ*)), in the comfortable chariot, toward the grass-seat (बर्हिः (*barhiḥ*)) on which one is to sit (आसदे (*āsade*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- धृतस्नू (*dhṛtasnū*)—your horses with bodies shining like ghee (or covered with sweat) **NOTE**
- केशिना (*keśinā*)—with attractive hair on the neck
- आसदे (*āsade*)—fit for sitting
- बर्हिः (*barhiḥ*)—toward the seat of darbha grass
- सुखे (*sukhe*)—comfortable
- रथे (*rathe*)—in the chariot
- अर्वांचं (*arvāṃcaṃ*)—facing us
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- वहतां (*vahatāṃ*)—let them bring

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, let your horses, with bodies shining like ghee (or covered with sweat) and with attractive hair on the neck, bring you facing us in the comfortable chariot toward the darbha-grass seat fit for sitting.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
May your long-maned and sleek-coated steeds bring you down in your easy chariot to sit upon the sacred grass.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

धृतस्नू (*dhṛtasnū*)—श्रमजनितजलप्रस्रवणयुक्तौ (*śramajanitajalaprasravaṇayuktau*). Since the word dhṛta is read among the names of water (Ni. 2-24), dhṛta means water, the water of exertion, that is, sweat. Because horses run fast over a long distance, their bodies are soaked with sweat. In the same way here, because Indra's horses have brought Indra from far away to our place of sacrifice, their bodies are drenched with sweat. That condition is described here by the word धृतस्नू (*dhṛtasnū*). **NOTE**
केशिना (*keśinā*)—केशिनौ । स्कंधप्रदेशे लंबमानकेशयुक्तौ हरी । (*keśinau | skaṃdhapradeśe laṃbamānakeśayuktau harī |*) It is natural for horses to have long hair on the neck. In the same way, Indra's horses are described here as having long hair on the neck.

### Printed p. 37
## Sūkta 42 (Maṇḍala 3)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उप नः सुतमिति नवर्चं चतुर्थं सूक्तं वैश्वामित्रं गायत्रमैंद्रं । उप
> न इत्यनुक्रमणिका । पूर्वसूक्तेन सहोक्तो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The fourth sūkta, of nine Ṛks, beginning उप नः सुतं (*upa naḥ sutaṃ*), has Viśvāmitra as seer, is in the Gāyatrī metre and is addressed to Indra. The Anukramaṇikā (index) begins it with उप न (*upa na*). Its viniyoga (ritual use) is the one stated together with the previous sūkta.

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning उप नः सुतं (*upa naḥ sutaṃ*) is the fourth sūkta in the fourth anuvāka. It has nine Ṛks. Its seer is Viśvāmitra, its deity Indra, its metre Gāyatrī. The Anukramaṇikā begins it with उप नः (*upa naḥ*). Its viniyoga was stated at the beginning of the previous sūkta.
Sūkta—42
Maṇḍala—3 · Anuvāka—4 · Sūkta—42
Aṣṭaka—3 · Adhyāya—3 · Varga—5, 6
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta—9
Ṛṣi—Viśvāmitra
Devatā—Indra
Chandas—Gāyatrī
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उप॑ नः सु॒तमा ग॑हि॒ सोम॑मिंद्र॒ गवा॑शिरं ।  
> हरि॑भ्यां॒ यस्ते॑ अस्म॒युः ॥ १ ॥

> *upa naḥ sutamā gahi somamiṃdra gavāśiraṃ |*  
> *haribhyāṃ yaste asmayuḥ ‖ 1 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उप । नः । सुतं । आ । गहि । सोमं । इंद्र । गोऽआशिरं ।  
> हरिभ्यां । यः । ते । अस्मयुः ॥ १ ॥

> *upa | naḥ | sutaṃ | ā | gahi | somaṃ | iṃdra | go 'āśiraṃ |*  
> *haribhyāṃ | yaḥ | te | asmayuḥ ‖ 1 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र नोऽस्मदीयं सुतमभिषुतं गवाशिरं पयसा मिश्रितं सोमं प्रति
> उपा गहि । समीप आगच्छ । यतो हरिभ्यामश्वाभ्यां युक्तस्तव रथोऽस्मयुरस्मा-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, come near to our pressed (सुतं (*sutaṃ*)) Soma, mixed with milk (गवाशिरं (*gavāśiraṃ*)), since your chariot, yoked with two horses, is desirous of us (अस्मयुः (*asmayuḥ*), "wishing us").

### Printed p. 38

> स्कामयमानो वर्तते ॥ गवाशिरं । आज् पूर्वस्य श्रीणातेः क्विप्यपस्पृधेथामान्यचुरित्यादिनाशिरित्यादेशः । बहुव्रीहौ पूर्वपदस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The remainder of the block gives only the derivation of गवाशिरं (*gavāśiraṃ*), with the substitution āśir for the root śrīṇāti after ā-√ac, and its accent; this belongs to grammar.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- नः (*naḥ*)—by us
- सुतं (*sutaṃ*)—pressed
- गवाशिरं (*gavāśiraṃ*)—mixed with milk
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—toward the Soma juice
- उप आ गहि (*upa ā gahi*)—graciously come near
- हरिभ्यां (*haribhyāṃ*)—joined with horses
- ते (*te*)—your
- यः (*yaḥ*)—the chariot that there is
- आस्मयुः (*āsmayuḥ*)—is favourable toward us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, come near toward the Soma juice pressed by us and mixed with milk. Your chariot, joined with horses, is favourable toward us.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Come, Indra, to this our effused Soma juice mixed with milk and curds, with your horses (yoked to your car), which is favourably disposed to us.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

सुतं आ गहि (*sutaṃ ā gahi*)—toward the Soma juice that we have prepared; that is, the idea is "come near us to drink Soma."
गवाशिरं (*gavāśiraṃ*)—पयसा मिश्रितं सोमं । (*payasā miśritaṃ somaṃ |*) It is the custom to mix milk, curds and wheat flour into the Soma juice to make it tasty. Soma juice mixed with milk is called gavāśira, Soma juice mixed with curds is called dadhyāśira, and Soma juice mixed with flour is called yavāśira. For these āśira substances, see what we have written in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 2, page 172.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तमिं॑द्र॒ मद॒मा ग॑हि बर्हि॒ष्ठां ग्राव॑भिः सु॒तं ।  
> कुविन्नस्य तृप्नवः ॥ २ ॥

> *tamiṃdra madamā gahi barhiṣṭhāṃ grāvabhiḥ sutaṃ |*  
> *kuvinnasya tṛpnavaḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तं । इंद्र । मदं । आ । गहि । बर्हिःस्थां । ग्रावभिः । सुतं ।  
> कुवित् । नु । अस्य । तृप्नवः ॥ २ ॥

> *taṃ | iṃdra | madaṃ | ā | gahi | barhiḥsthāṃ | grāvabhiḥ | sutaṃ |*  
> *kuvit | nu | asya | tṛpnavaḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

### Printed p. 39
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ग्रावभिः सुतमभिषुतं बर्हिष्ठां बर्हिषि स्थितं मदं । मद्यतेऽनेनेति मदः सोमः । तमिमं सोममा गहि । आगच्छ । कुवित्प्रभूतं यथा भवति
> तथास्य सोमस्य पाने नु क्षिप्रं तृप्नवः । तृप्तो भव ॥ मदं । मदी हर्षे ।
> मदोनुपसर्ग इत्यप्पत्ययः । तस्य पित्वादनुदात्तत्वे धातुस्वरः । बर्हिष्ठां ।
> ष्ठा गतिनिवृत्तौ । क्विप् । संहितायां पूर्वपदसकारस्यादेशप्रत्यययोरिति षत्वं ।
> ष्टुत्वं । कृदुत्तरपदस्वरः । तृप्नवः । तृप प्रीणन इत्यस्य लेट्यडागमः । कुविद्योगादनिघातः । कुभ्नादिषु नृनमन तृप्नोतीति पठितं । तथापि सर्वविधीनां छंदसि विकल्पितत्पादत्र णत्वप्रतिषेधो न भवति । प्रत्ययस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, this Soma, pressed out with the pressing stones and lying on the sacred grass, is the exhilarating (मदं (*madaṃ*)) drink; Soma is called मदः (*madaḥ*) because by it one is gladdened. Sāyaṇa asks Indra to come to this Soma. कुवित् (*kuvit*) means "abundantly": as there is abundance, may Indra quickly (नु (*nu*)) become satisfied in drinking this Soma.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- ग्रावभिः (*grāvabhiḥ*)—by the pressing stones
- सुतं (*sutaṃ*)—pressed out
- बर्हिष्ठां (*barhiṣṭhāṃ*)—placed upon the darbha seats
- मदं (*madaṃ*)—and gladdening
- तं (*taṃ*)—this
- Soma juice: toward it
- आ गहि (*ā gahi*)—be pleased to come
- कुवित् (*kuvit*)—abundant
- अस्य (*asya*)—by drinking this juice
- नु (*nu*)—quickly
- तृष्णवः (*tṛṣṇavaḥ*)—attain satisfaction

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, be pleased to come toward this Soma juice, pressed by the pressing stones, placed upon the darbha seats and gladdening. Quickly attain satisfaction by drinking this abundant juice.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Come, Indra, to the exhilarating (Soma) expressed by the stones, and placed upon the sacred grass : drink of it to satiety, for there is plenty.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

बर्हिष्ठां ग्रावभिः सुतं (*barhiṣṭhāṃ grāvabhiḥ sutaṃ*)—The Soma juice, crushed, pressed and filtered with stones, is placed upon the spread darbha grass called barhis. Indra is prayed to come to drink it.
नु (*nu*)—Although the particle नु (*nu*) has many meanings, the commentator here takes the meaning क्षिप्रं (*kṣipraṃ*), "quickly", and explains accordingly.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॑मि॒त्था गिरो॒ ममाच्छा॑गुरिषि॒ता इ॒तः ।  
> आ॒वृते॒ सोम॑पीतये ॥ ३ ॥

> *iṃdramitthā giro mamācchāguriṣitā itaḥ |*  
> *āvṛte somapītaye ‖ 3 ‖*

### Printed p. 40

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रं । इत्था । गिरः । मम । अच्छ । अगुः । इषिताः । इतः ।  
> आवृते । सोमपीतये ॥ ३ ॥

> *iṃdraṃ | itthā | giraḥ | mama | accha | aguḥ | iṣitāḥ | itaḥ |*  
> *āvṛte | somapītaye ‖ 3 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र इषितास्त्यदर्थं प्रेरिता इत्येत्थमनेन प्रकारेणोच्यमाना मम
> गिरः स्तुतिलक्षणा वाच इंद्रं त्वामितोऽस्माद्देवयजनदेशादच्छागुः । अभिमुख्येन
> गच्छंतु । किमर्थं । सोमपीतये सोमपानार्थमावृते त्वामावर्तयितुं ॥
> इत्था । इदंशब्दात्था हेत् च छंदसीति व्यत्ययेन थाप्रत्ययः । इदम एतेत् रथोरितीदित्यादेशः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । अच्छ । अच्छ गत्यर्थवदेष्टिति गतिसंज्ञा । अगुः । इण्
> गतावित्यस्य लुङि रूपं । निघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, my praises, which are impelled (इषिताः (*iṣitāḥ*)) toward you and are thus recited, are to go from this place of the sacrifice toward you, Indra, face to face. For what purpose? For drinking Soma, आवृते (*āvṛte*), that is, to bring you here.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- इषुताः (*iṣutāḥ*)—impelled toward you
- इत्था (*itthā*)—recited in this manner
- मम (*mama*)—my
- गिरः (*giraḥ*)—words in the form of praise
- सोमपीतये (*somapītaye*)—for the drinking of Soma
- आवृते (*āvṛte*)—to bring you here
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—you, who are Indra
- आच्छागुः (*ācchāguḥ*)—let them set out toward

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, may my words of praise, impelled toward you and recited in this manner, set out toward you, who are Indra, in order to bring you here for the drinking of Soma.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
May my praises, addressed from hence and in this manner, attain
Iudra, to bring him hither to drink the Soma juice

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

अच्छ (*accha*)—आभिमुख्येन (*ābhimukhyena*), "facing toward". Yāska, giving the derivation of the word अच्छ (*accha*), says अच्छाभेराप्तुमिति शाकपूणिः (*acchābherāptumiti śākapūṇiḥ*) (Ni. 5-28): it is the view of Śākapūṇi that the word अच्छ (*accha*) means "facing, coming to meet".

### Printed p. 41
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्रं॒ सोम॑स्य सी॒तये॒ स्तोमै॑रि॒ह ह॑वामहे ।  
> उ॒क्थेभिः॑ कु॒विदा॒गम॑त् ॥ ४ ॥

> *iṃdraṃ somasya sītaye stomairiha havāmahe |*  
> *ukthebhiḥ kuvidāgamat ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रं । सोमस्य । पीतये । स्तोमैः । इह । हवामहे ।  
> उक्केभिः । कुवित् । आगमत् ॥ ४ ॥

> *iṃdraṃ | somasya | pītaye | stomaiḥ | iha | havāmahe |*  
> *ukkebhiḥ | kuvit | āgamat ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सोमैस्त्रिवृत्पंचदशादिस्तोमसाध्यः सोत्रैरुक्षेभिरुक्षे शस्त्रैश्चेह
> कर्मणि सोमस्य पीतये सोमपानार्थमिंद्रं हवामहे । वयमाह्वयामः ।
> कविद्भुवारमाहूतः स इंद्र आगमत् । यज्ञं प्रत्यागच्छतु ॥ आगमत् । गमेर्लेट्यडागमः । आगमा अनुदात्ता इत्यटोऽनुदात्तत्वाद्धा तुस्वरः । तिजि चोदात्तवतीति
> गतेर्निघातः । कुविद्योगादनिघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

With stotras accomplished by the trivṛt, pañcadaśa and other stomas, with ukthas, and with śastras, we invoke Indra in this rite to drink Soma. **NOTE** Invoked repeatedly (कुवित् (*kuvit*)), may that Indra come, that is, approach the sacrifice.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- स्तोमैः (*stomaiḥ*)—with praises in the form of stomas
- उक्षेभिः (*ukṣebhiḥ*)—with śastras in the form of ukthas **NOTE**
- इह (*iha*)—in this sacrifice
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—of the Soma juice
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—for drinking
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- हवामहे (*havāmahe*)—we invoke
- कुपित् (*kupit*)—Indra, invoked many times **NOTE**
- आगमत् (*āgamat*)—may he come to this place of sacrifice

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

With praises in the form of stomas and with śastra-mantras in the form of ukthas **NOTE** we invoke Indra in this sacrifice to drink the Soma juice. May Indra, who is invoked many times, come to this place of sacrifice.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
We invoke Indra to this rite with prayers to drink the Soma juice;
may he, repeatedly invoked, come hither

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

कुवित् (*kuvit*)—Since the word कुवित् (*kuvit*) is read among the twelve words for "much", such as उरु (*uru*) and तुवि (*tuvi*) (Ni. 3-13), it means "many times, abundantly".

### Printed p. 42
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॒ सोमाः॑ सु॒ता इ॒मे तान्द॑धिष्व शतक्रतो ।  
> ज॒ठरे॑ वाजिनीवसो ॥ ५ ॥

> *iṃdra somāḥ sutā ime tāndadhiṣva śatakrato |*  
> *jaṭhare vājinīvaso ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्र । सोमाः । सुताः । इमे । तान् । दधिष्व । शतक्रतो इति शतक्रतो ।  
> जठरे । वाजिनीवसो इति वाजिनीवसो ॥ ५ ॥

> *iṃdra | somāḥ | sutāḥ | ime | tān | dadhiṣva | śatakrato iti śatakrato |*  
> *jaṭhare | vājinīvaso iti vājinīvaso ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे शतक्रतो इंद्र इमे सोमाः सुताः । ग्रावभिस्त्वदर्थमभिषुताः । हे
> वाजिनीवसो अन्नधन । यद्वा । वाजोऽन्नमासु फलत्वेनास्तीति वाजिन्यः क्रियाः ।
> तासां वासक हे इंद्र तान्सोमरसान् जठरे दधिष्ट । धारय ॥ वाजिनीवसो ।
> वाजशब्दान्मत्वर्थेऽत इनिरनावितीनिप्रत्ययः । ऋन्नेभ्यो जीबिति जीप् । तासां
> वसो । संबुद्धौ चेति गुणः । आमंत्रितत्वादाष्टमिकमनुदात्तत्वं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra of a hundred acts, these Soma juices are pressed; they have been pressed out with the stones for you. O वाजिनीवसो (*vājinīvaso*), one whose wealth is food. Or else: वाज (*vāja*) is food, and वाजिन्यः (*vājinyaḥ*) are the rites which have food as their fruit; Indra is the dweller (वासक (*vāsaka*)) in them. O Indra, hold those Soma juices in your belly.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शतक्रतो (*śatakrato*)—O doer of many deeds of valour
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- इमे सोमाः (*ime somāḥ*)—these Soma juices
- सुताः (*sutāḥ*)—are pressed
- वाजिनीवसो (*vājinīvaso*)—O Indra, whose wealth is food
- तान् (*tān*)—these Soma juices
- जठरे (*jaṭhare*)—in the belly
- दधिष्व (*dadhiṣva*)—hold

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, doer of many deeds of valour, these Soma juices have been pressed and are ready. O Indra, whose wealth is food, hold these Soma juices in your belly.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra, these Soma juices are poured out : take them, Shatakratu, into your stomach ; you who are rich in (abundant) food

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

शतक्रतो (*śatakrato*)—शत (*śata*) means hundreds, many; क्रतु (*kratu*) means act. The word means: O Indra, who have performed many deeds of valour.

### Printed p. 43
अन्नवे धन (*annave dhana*)… वाजिनीवसो (*vājinīvaso*)—One whose wealth is food, that is, one who has abundant food. Or else, वाज (*vāja*) means food, and वाजिनी (*vājinī*) means rites such as sacrifices that have food as their fruit. Because he dwells in such rites, that is, because he is praised in such sacrifices, Indra is named वाजिनीवसु (*vājinīvasu*). Thus the commentator explains two meanings.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वि॒द्मा हि त्वा॑ धनंज॒यं वाजे॑षु दधृ॒षं क॑वे ।  
> अथा॑ ते सु॒न्नुमी॑महे ॥ ६ ॥

> *vidmā hi tvā dhanaṃjayaṃ vājeṣu dadhṛṣaṃ kave |*  
> *athā te sunnumīmahe ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विद्म । हि । त्वा । धनंजयं । वाजेषु ' दधृषं । कवे ।  
> अध । ते । सुम्मं । ईमहे ॥ ६ ॥

> *vidma | hi | tvā | dhanaṃjayaṃ | vājeṣu ' dadhṛṣaṃ | kave |*  
> *adha | te | summaṃ | īmahe ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे कवे क्रांतदर्शिन्निंद्र वाजेषु युद्धेषु दधृषं शत्रूणामभिभवितारं
> आत एव धनंजयं । धनं जयतीति धनंजयः । तादृशं त्वा त्वां विद्म हि । जानीमः
> खलु । अधानंतरं ते तव सुम्मं धनमीमहे । याचामहे ॥ विद्म । विदेर्लटि
> विदो लटो वेति मसो मादेशः । पादादित्पादनिघातः । धनंजयं । जि जय इत्यस्माद्धन उपपदे संज्ञायां भृत्यवृजिधारिसहितपिदम इति खच् प्रत्ययः । अरुर्द्विपदजंतस्य मुमिति पूर्वपदस्य मुमागमः । चित्तादंतोदात्तः । दधृषं । इद्धृषा
> प्रागल्ब इत्यस्य यज् लुक्यभ्यासस्य रीगभावश्चांदसः । तदंतात्पचाद्यचि यजोऽचि
> चेति यजो लुक् । लघूपधगुणे प्राप्ते न धातुलोप इति तस्य प्रतिषेधः ।
> चित्तादंतोदात्तः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O far-seeing (कवे (*kave*)) Indra, in battles (वाजेषु (*vājeṣu*)) you overcome enemies, and therefore you are धनंजयं (*dhanaṃjayaṃ*), "winner of wealth", one who wins wealth. We know you to be such. Thereafter we ask, beg, for सुम्मं (*summaṃ*), your wealth.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कवे (*kave*)—O far-seeing Indra
- वाजेषु (*vājeṣu*)—in battles
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- दधृषं (*dadhṛṣaṃ*)—as the destroyer of enemies
- धनंजयं (*dhanaṃjayaṃ*)—and as the gatherer of the enemies' wealth
- विद्म हि (*vidma hi*)—we indeed know
- अध (*adha*)—therefore
- ते (*te*)—your (from you)
- सुम्नं (*sumnaṃ*)—wealth (happiness) **NOTE**
- ईमहे (*īmahe*)—we pray for

### Printed p. 44

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O far-seeing Indra, in battles we know you indeed as the destroyer of enemies and the gatherer of the enemies' wealth. Therefore we pray for your wealth.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Far-seeing Indra, we know you to be victorious and winner of wealth
in battles: therefore we ask of you riches.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the final letters of the words विद्म (*vidma*) and अध (*adha*) are long in the Saṃhitā-pāṭha for the convenience of the metre.
वाजेषु दधृषं (*vājeṣu dadhṛṣaṃ*)—The word वाजः (*vājaḥ*) is read among the names of food (Ni. 3-9) and also among the names of battle (Ni. 3-9). Here the commentator takes the meaning battle or war, and explains: one who overcomes enemies in battles.
धनंजयं (*dhanaṃjayaṃ*)—Indra, who defeats enemies in battles and wins their wealth.
सुम्मं (*summaṃ*)—Since the word सुम्मं (*summaṃ*) is read among the names of happiness (Ni. 3-13), it means happiness.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मिं॑द्र॒ गवा॑शिरं॒ यवा॑शिरं च नः पिब ।  
> आ॒गत्या॒ वृष॑भिः सु॒तं ॥ ७ ॥

> *imiṃdra gavāśiraṃ yavāśiraṃ ca naḥ piba |*  
> *āgatyā vṛṣabhiḥ sutaṃ ‖ 7 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमं । इंद्र । गोऽआशिरं । यवऽआशिरं । च । नः । पिब ।  
> आगत्य । वृषभिः । सुतं ॥ ७ ॥

> *imaṃ | iṃdra | go 'āśiraṃ | yava 'āśiraṃ | ca | naḥ | piba |*  
> *āgatya | vṛṣabhiḥ | sutaṃ ‖ 7 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र गवाशिरं गव्यमिश्रणोपेतं सोमं तथा यवाशिरं यवमिश्रणोपेतं वृषभिग्रावभिः सुतमभिषुतं नोऽस्मदीयमिमं सोमं देवयजनमागत्य
> पिब ॥ गवाशिरं यवाशिरमित्यत्र आज् पूर्वस्य श्रीणातेः क्विप्यपस्पृधेथा मान्यचुरित्यादिनाशिरित्यादेशः । बहुव्रीहौ पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, come to the place of sacrifice and drink this Soma of ours, which is गवाशिरं (*gavāśiraṃ*), mixed with milk products, and यवाशिरं (*yavāśiraṃ*), mixed with barley, pressed out with the stones. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 45
#### Ṛk 7

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- गवाशिरं (*gavāśiraṃ*)—that which is mixed with milk products
- यवाशिरं (*yavāśiraṃ*)—that which is well prepared with barley grain
- वृषभिः (*vṛṣabhiḥ*)—by the pressing stones
- सुतं (*sutaṃ*)—and pressed
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- इमं (*imaṃ*)—for this Soma-drinking
- आगत्य (*āgatya*)—having come here
- पिब (*piba*)—drink

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, come here and drink this Soma of ours, which is mixed with milk products, mixed with barley grain, and pressed by the pressing stones.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Having come (to our rite) drink, Indra, this our (libation), effused by the stones, and mixed with milk and curds and with barley.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

वृषभीः सुतं (*vṛṣabhīḥ sutaṃ*) means ग्रावभिः अभिषुतं (*grāvabhiḥ abhiṣutaṃ*): the Soma juice taken out by crushing the Soma plants with stones. We have already explained the meaning of the words गवाशिरं (*gavāśiraṃ*) and यवाशिरं (*yavāśiraṃ*) in the special topics of the first Ṛk of this sūkta.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तुभ्येदिं॑द्र॒ स्व ओ॒क्ये॒३॒॑सोमं॑ चोदामि पी॒तये॑ ।  
> ए॒ष रा॑रंतु ते हृ॒दि ॥ ८ ॥

> *tubhyediṃdra sva okyesomaṃ codāmi pītaye |*  
> *eṣa rāraṃtu te hṛdi ‖ 8 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शुभ्य । इत् ! इंद्र । स्वे । ओक्ये । सोमं । चोदामि । पीतये ।  
> एषः । ररंतु । ते । हृदि ॥ ८ ॥

> *śubhya | it ! iṃdra | sve | okye | somaṃ | codāmi | pītaye |*  
> *eṣaḥ | raraṃtu | te | hṛdi ‖ 8 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र तुभ्येत्तुभ्यमेव पीतये पानार्थं स्वे स्वकीय ओक्ये स्थाने जठरेऽभिषुतमिमं सोमं चोदामि । प्रेरयामि । एष पीतः सोमस्ते तव हृदि
> हृदये ररंतु । अत्यर्थं रमतां ॥ तुभ्य । युष्मच्छब्दस्य चतुर्थ्येकवचने तुभ्यमह्याविति तुभ्यादेशः । सुपां सुलुगिति सुपो लुक् । जयि चेत्याद्युदात्तः । ओक्ये ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, for you alone, for drinking, in your own abode, in the belly, I urge this pressed Soma." So Sāyaṇa glosses: चोदामि (*codāmi*) is "I impel". "May this Soma, once drunk, delight exceedingly in your heart" is his gloss of ररंतु (*raraṃtu*). The rest of the block, which begins with the word तुभ्य (*tubhya*), is grammatical.

### Printed p. 46

> वस्वादित्वात्स्वार्थिको यत्प त्ययः । तित्स्वरितः । चोदामि । चुद प्रेरणे । ण्यंतस्य
> लटि रूपं । बहुलमन्यत्रापीति णेर्लुक् । निघातः । रारंतु । रमु क्रीडायामित्यस्य
> यज् लुकि लोटि सर्वविधीनां छंदसि विकल्पितत्वादत्राभ्यासस्य नुमभावः। संहितायामान्येषामपीत्यभ्यासस्य दीर्घः । निघातः । हृदि । हृदयशब्दस्य पद्दन्नोमास्मृदित्यादिना हृदादेशः । ऊडिदमिति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This part of the block contains only grammatical analysis of the words ओक्ये (*okye*), चोदामि (*codāmi*), रारंतु (*rāraṃtu*) and हृदि (*hṛdi*) (affixes, elisions, accent); the Grammar section is not given here.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- तुभ्येत् (*tubhyet*)—with you alone in view
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—for your drinking
- स्वे (*sve*)—your own
- ओक्ये (*okye*)—in the place that is the belly
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—this Soma
- चोदामि (*codāmi*)—I offer
- एषः (*eṣaḥ*)—this Soma
- ते (*te*)—your
- हृदि (*hṛdi*)—in the heart
- ररंतु (*raraṃtu*)—let it cause delight

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, with you alone in view, for your drinking, I offer this Soma in your own place, the belly. May this Soma cause delight in your heart.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
I offer to you, Indra, this Soma juice for your drinking, to be taken into its proper receptacle ; may it delight your heart.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

तुभ्य (*tubhya*) is तुभ्यं (*tubhyaṃ*); here the anusvāra has been dropped for the convenience of the metre. तुभ्य (*tubhya*) + इत् (*it*) = तुभ्येत् (*tubhyet*) means "for you alone".
स्वे ओक्ये (*sve okye*): Yāska has given the etymological explanation ओक इति निवासनामोच्यते (*oka iti nivāsanāmocyate*), that is, that ओक (*oka*) is a name for a dwelling-place. Here स्वे ओक्ये (*sve okye*) means "in one's own dwelling-place", or "in one's own belly".

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वां सु॒तस्य॑ पी॒तये॑ प्र॒त्नमिं॑द्र हवामहे ।  
> कु॒शि॒कासो॑ अव॒स्यवः॑ ॥ ९ ॥

> *tvāṃ sutasya pītaye pratnamiṃdra havāmahe |*  
> *kuśikāso avasyavaḥ ‖ 9 ‖*

### Printed p. 47

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वां । सुतस्य । पीतये । प्रत्नं । इंद्र । हनामहे ।  
> कुतिकासः । अवस्यवः ॥ ९ ॥

> *tvāṃ | sutasya | pītaye | pratnaṃ | iṃdra | hanāmahe |*  
> *kutikāsaḥ | avasyavaḥ ‖ 9 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र अवस्यवस्त्वत्तो रक्षणमिच्छंतः कुशिकासः कुशिकगोत्रोत्पन्ना वयं
> सुतस्य ग्रावभिरभिषुतस्य सोमस्य पीतये पानार्थं प्रश्नं पुरातनं त्वां हवामहे ।
> स्तुतिलक्षणाभिर्गिर्भिराह्वयामः ॥ पीतये । स्थागापापचो भाव इति क्विन् । हिष्ठवत्सरतिशत्थांतानां । फि. १-७ । इत्यंतोदात्तत्वं । हवामहे । स्वयत्वेर्लटि बहुलं
> छंदसीति संप्रसारणे रूपं । अवस्यवः । अव इच्छंतीत्यर्थे सुपः क्यच् । क्याछंदसीत्युप्रत्ययः । कुशिकासो अवस्यव इत्यत्र संहितायामव्यादवद्यादित्यादिना । पा.
> ६ १-११६ । एजिः प्रकृतिभावः

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, we, born in the Kuśika lineage and desiring protection from you, invoke you, the ancient one, for the drinking of the Soma pressed with the stones; we call you with speech in the form of praise." कुशिकासः (*kuśikāsaḥ*) is explained as "born in the Kuśika gotra", अवस्यवः (*avasyavaḥ*) as "desiring protection", प्रत्नं (*pratnaṃ*) as "ancient", हवामहे (*havāmahe*) as "we call". The rest is grammatical.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- अवस्यवः (*avasyavaḥ*)—we who desire to obtain your protection
- कुशिकासः (*kuśikāsaḥ*)—and who are born in the Kuśika line
- सुतस्य (*sutasya*)—of this pressed Soma juice
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—for the drinking
- प्रत्नं (*pratnaṃ*)—ancient
- त्वां (*tvāṃ*)—you
- हवामहे (*havāmahe*)—we call with prayer

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, we who desire to obtain your protection and are born in the Kuśika line call you, the ancient one, with prayer, for the drinking of this pressed Soma juice.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Descendants of Kusha, desirous of protection, we invoke you, Indra, who are of old, to drink of the libation.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

प्रत्नं पुरातनं (*pratnaṃ purātanaṃ*). Since the word प्रत्न (*pratna*) is read among the names of "ancient", "ancient" means "existing from beginningless time".
कुशिकासः (*kuśikāsaḥ*)—कुशिकगोत्रोत्पन्ना वयं (*kuśikagotrotpannā vayaṃ*): "we, born in the Kuśika gotra", says the seer Viśvāmitra. On the meaning of the word Kuśika Yāska says: कुशिकस्य सूनुः कुशिको राजा बभूव । क्रोशतेः शब्दकर्मणः । क्रंशतीर्वा स्यात्प्रकाशयति कर्मणः साधुविक्षोशयतार्थानामिति वा ॥ (*kuśikasya sūnuḥ kuśiko rājā babhūva | krośateḥ śabdakarmaṇaḥ | kraṃśatīrvā syātprakāśayati karmaṇaḥ sādhuvikṣośayatārthānāmiti vā ‖*) (Ni. 9-25)

### Printed p. 48
Thus he has given the etymological explanation. Kuśika was a king. His descendants are called Kuśikāḥ or Kuśikāsaḥ. The reason why this name came to this king is as follows: the word Kuśika is formed from the root क्रंशति (*kraṃśati*) and gives the sense "making sound". Making sound means uttering words and praising. Therefore the word Kuśikāḥ can also mean people such as priests who praise and recite mantras; or the word Kuśika can be explained as one who makes manifest, that is, who protects, sacrificial rites or the dharmas; or as one who gives artha, that is, wealth and the like, to good people. This is the opinion of the Nirukta authors. In the present Ṛk the word कुशिकासः (*kuśikāsaḥ*) means the seers such as Viśvāmitra, born in the Kuśika gotra.

## Sūkta 43 (Maṇḍala 3)

> आ याह्यर्वाज्यात्यष्टर्चं पंचमं सूक्तं वैश्वामित्रं त्रैष्टुभम्यंद्रं ।
> आ याह्यष्टावित्यनुक्रमणिका ॥ दशरात्रे चतुर्थेऽहनि स्तोमवृद्दौ माध्यंदिने
> सवनेऽच्छावाकशस्त्रे स्तोमाभिशंसनार्थमिदं सूक्तं। सूत्रितः च। आ याह्यर्वाज्यात्यष्टर्चान्यावपेरन् । आ ७. १२. । इति ॥ > महाव्रतेऽप्यावपनीयं ॥

> *ā yāhyarvājyātyaṣṭarcaṃ paṃcamaṃ sūktaṃ vaiśvāmitraṃ traiṣṭubhamyaṃdraṃ |*
> *ā yāhyaṣṭāvityanukramaṇikā ‖ daśarātre caturthe 'hani stomavṛddau mādhyaṃdine*
> *savane 'cchāvākaśastre stomābhiśaṃsanārthamidaṃ sūktaṃ| sūtritaḥ ca| ā yāhyarvājyātyaṣṭarcānyāvaperan | ā 7. 12. | iti ‖ > mahāvrate 'pyāvapanīyaṃ ‖*

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The fifth sūkta, of eight Ṛks, beginning आ याह्यर्वाज् (*ā yāhyarvāj*), is by Viśvāmitra, addressed to Indra, in the Triṣṭubh metre; the Anukramaṇikā says आ याह्यष्टावित्यनुक्रमणिका (*ā yāhyaṣṭāvityanukramaṇikā*). In the Daśarātra, on the fourth day, at the midday pressing, this sūkta is used in the Acchāvāka's śastra for the praise of the stomas, for increase of the stoma. It is also stated in the sūtra आ याह्यर्वाज्यात्यष्टर्चान्यावपेरन् (*ā yāhyarvājyātyaṣṭarcānyāvaperan*) (Ā. 7-12). It is also an insertion hymn in the Mahāvrata.

##### Anuvāda

The sūkta beginning आ याह्यर्वाज् (*ā yāhyarvāj*) is the fifth sūkta in the fourth anuvāka. It has eight Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Viśvāmitra; the deity is Indra; the metre is Triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇikā it is introduced with the words आ याह्यष्टौ (*ā yāhyaṣṭau*). In the sacrifice called Daśarātra, on the fourth day, at the midday pressing, for the increase of the stoma, this sūkta is applied (viniyoga) to the śastra mantras that serve the praise of the stoma, to be recited by the priest called Acchāvāka; this is explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, in the sūtra आ याह्यर्वाजित्यष्टर्चान्यावपेरन् (*ā yāhyarvājityaṣṭarcānyāvaperan*) (Ā. 7-12). It is also applied in the sacrifice called Mahāvrata.

Sūkta—43
Maṇḍala 3; Anuvāka 4; Sūkta 43.
Aṣṭaka 3; Adhyāya 3; Varga 7.
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta—8.
Seer: Viśvāmitra.
Deity: Indra.
Metre: Triṣṭubh.

### Printed p. 49
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ या॑ह्य॒र्वाजुप॑ वंधुरे॒ष्ठास्तवेदनु॑ प्र॒दिवः॑ सोम॒पेयं॑।  
> प्रि॒या सखा॑या॒ वि मु॒चोप॑ ब॒र्हिस्ता मि॒मे ह॑व्य॒वाहो॑  
> हवंते ॥ १ ॥

> *ā yāhyarvājupa vaṃdhureṣṭhāstavedanu pradivaḥ somapeyaṃ|*  
> *priyā sakhāyā vi mucopa barhistā mime havyavāho*  
> *havaṃte ‖ 1 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । याहि । अर्वाज् । उप । वंधुरेऽस्थाः । तव । इत् । अनु । प्रदिवः । सोमपेयं ।  
> प्रिया । सखाया । वि । मुच । उप । बर्हिः । त्वां । इमं । हव्यवाहः । हवंते ॥

> *ā | yāhi | arvāj | upa | vaṃdhure 'sthāḥ | tava | it | anu | pradivaḥ | somapeyaṃ |*  
> *priyā | sakhāyā | vi | muca | upa | barhiḥ | tvāṃ | imaṃ | havyavāhaḥ | havaṃte ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र वंधुरेष्ठाः । वंधुरशब्देनेषाद्वयसंबंधस्थानमभिधीयते ।
> तेन तद्विशिष्टो रथो लक्ष्यते । तस्मिन् रथे तिष्ठन् त्वमर्वाजिस्मदभिमुखः सन् आ
> अभिलक्ष्योप याहि । समीप आगच्छ । प्रदिवः प्रश्नॆं पूर्वक्रमगतं तवेत्तवैव संबंधि
> सोमपेयं सोमपानं त्वमनु उद्दिश्य प्रिया तव प्रियतम् सखाया समानख्यानावश्च बर्हिरुप बर्हिषः समीपॆ: वि मुच । विमुंच । हव्यवाहः । हव्यं
> वहंतीति हव्यवाहः । इमे ऋत्विजस्तां हवंते । सोमपानार्थमाह्वयंति । तस्मादागच्छेति भावः ॥ वंधुरेष्ठाः । तिष्ठतेर्विच् । तत्पुरुषे कृति बहुलमिति सप्तम्या
> अलुक् । प्रदिवः । सकारांतमव्ययं । सोमपेयं । पा पान इत्यस्य भावेऽचो
> यदिति यत्पत्ययः । ईद्यतीतीत्वं । अर्धधातुकलक्षणो गुणः। यतोऽनाव इत्याद्युदात्तत्वं । समासे कृदुत्तरपदस्वरः । मुच । मंचतेर्लोट्यागमानुशासनसानित्यत्वान्नुमभावः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, you who sit in the vandhura! The word vandhura denotes the place where the two shafts join; by it the chariot characterised by it is meant. Standing in that chariot, facing us, come near. Having in view, for your own Soma-drinking that is ancient, belonging to you alone from the earlier course, release your dear, companionable horses near the sacred grass. These oblation-carriers, the priests, call you for the drinking of Soma; therefore come: this is the sense." हव्यवाहः (*havyavāhaḥ*) is explained as "those who carry the oblation". The remainder gives the grammar of वंधुरेष्ठाः (*vaṃdhureṣṭhāḥ*), प्रदिवः (*pradivaḥ*), सोमपेयं (*somapeyaṃ*) and मुच (*muca*).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) वंधुरेष्माः (*vaṃdhureṣmāḥ*)—seated in a chariot with an excellent seat
- अर्वाज् (*arvāj*)—and facing us
- आ उप याहि (*ā upa yāhi*)—be pleased to come near us
- प्रदिवः (*pradivaḥ*)—offered from former times
- तवेत् (*tavet*)—and intended for you alone
- सोमपेयं अनु (*somapeyaṃ anu*)—for the Soma-drinking
- प्रिया (*priyā*)—those dear to you
- सखाया (*sakhāyā*)—and that are together
- (the horses) बर्हिः उप (*barhiḥ upa*)—near the sacred-grass seats
- वि मुंच (*vi muṃca*)—unharness and make stand
- हव्यवाहः (*havyavāhaḥ*)—holding the oblation
- इमे (*ime*)—these priests
- त्वां (*tvāṃ*)—you
- हवंते (*havaṃte*)—call

### Printed p. 50

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, seated in a chariot with an excellent seat and facing us, be pleased to come near us. For the Soma-drinking that was offered before all else and is intended for you alone, unharness your dear and companionable horses near the sacred-grass seats and make them stand. These priests, who hold the oblation, call you.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Descend to us riding in your car, for verily yours is this ancient libation ; unharness your beloved and friendly (horses) near to the sacred grass, for these offerers of the oblation present it to you.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

वंधुरेष्ठाः (*vaṃdhureṣṭhāḥ*)—The word वंधुर (*vaṃdhura*) is used as a synonym for the seat of a chariot. But since Sāyaṇa explains it as ईषाद्वयसंबंधस्थान (*īṣādvayasaṃbaṃdhasthāna*), here it can be taken to mean a chariot with a seat made by joining two shafts. Earlier, this word has been used in many places in describing the famous chariot of deities such as the Aśvins; it was explained earlier in places such as अधि वां स्थाम वंधुरे (*adhi vāṃ sthāma vaṃdhure*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-139-4). In places such as रथो यो वां त्रिवंधुरः (*ratho yo vāṃ trivaṃdhuraḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-22-5) too, it means a chariot with three shafts. In the present place it must mean an excellent seat. "One seated in a chariot with an excellent seat" is an epithet of Indra.
प्रदिवः (*pradivaḥ*)—प्रत्नं (*pratnaṃ*), प्रदिवः (*pradivaḥ*) (Ni. 3-19) are read among the names of "ancient". The Nirukta author quotes the Ṛk मरुत्वाइंद्र वृषभो (*marutvāiṃdra vṛṣabho*) and there explains this word as पूर्वेष्टपृहस्सु (*pūrveṣṭapṛhassu*). This means "just like the former days". The sense is that, just as the oblation has been offered from very ancient times, so it is offered even now.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ या॑हि पू॒र्विरिति॑ चर्ष॒णीरा आ॒र्य आ॒शिष॒ उप॑ नो॒  
> हरि॑भ्यां ।  
> इ॒मा हि त्वा॑ म॒तयः॒ स्तोम॑तष्टा॒ इंद्र॒ हवं॑ते स॒ख्यं  ॒॑
> जुषाणाः ॥ २ ॥

> *ā yāhi pūrviriti carṣaṇīrā ārya āśiṣa upa no*  
> *haribhyāṃ |*  
> *imā hi tvā matayaḥ stomataṣṭā iṃdra havaṃte sakhyaṃ*  
> *juṣāṇāḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

### Printed p. 51
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । याहि । पूर्विः । अति । चर्षणीः । आ । अर्यः । आशिषः । उपॆ । नः । हरिभ्यां ।  
> इमाः । हि । त्वा । मतयः । स्तोमतष्टाः । इंद्र । हवंते । सख्यं । जुषाणाः ॥ २ ॥

> *ā | yāhi | pūrviḥ | ati | carṣaṇīḥ | ā | aryaḥ | āśiṣaḥ | upè | naḥ | haribhyāṃ |*  
> *imāḥ | hi | tvā | matayaḥ | stomataṣṭāḥ | iṃdra | havaṃte | sakhyaṃ | juṣāṇāḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र, पूर्वीः सर्वाश्चर्यणीः प्रजा अत्या याहि । अतिक्रम्यागच्छ । द्वितीय
> आज् पूरणः । आर्योऽस्माकं स्वामी त्वं न आशिषस्वमत्रागत्य सोमं पिबेत्येवमादिका अस्माकं प्रार्थना हरिभ्यामश्वाभ्यां युक्तः सन् उपागच्छ । किंच ते तव
> सख्यं जुषाणाः सेवमानाः स्तोमतष्टाः स्तोमकारिभिः स्तोतृभिः कृता इमा
> मतयः स्ततयो हवंते । सोमपानार्थं त्वामाह्वयंति । हि प्रसिद्धौ । करणे
> कर्तृव्यापारः । काष्ठानि पचंतीतिवत् ॥ आ । संहितायामाजोऽनुनासिकश्चंदसीत्यनुनासिकः । आर्यः । ऋ गतौ । अर्यः । स्वामिवैश्ययोरिति निपातनाद्यत्पत्ययः ।
> यतोऽनाव इत्याद्युदात्तत्वे प्राप्तेऽर्यस्य स्वाम्याख्या चेत् । फि १-१८ । इत्यंतोदात्तत्वं । आशिषः । क्लिपि शास इदज् हलोरित्युपधाया इत्वं । शासिवसिघसीनाचेति षत्वं । स्तोमतष्टाः । तक्षू त्वक्कू तनूकरण इत्यस्मात्कर्मणि क्तः । तृतीया
> कर्मणीति पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses Indra: "O Indra, pass beyond all the peoples (चर्षणीः (*carṣaṇīḥ*) = prajāḥ) and come to us." Aryaḥ means our lord. "Our prayers (आशिषः (*āśiṣaḥ*)) are of this kind: 'Come here and drink the Soma'. Accepting them, come to us yoked with your two horses." Further, these hymns (मतयः (*matayaḥ*), praises), fashioned by the praisers, desiring your friendship, invoke you for the drinking of Soma. हि (*hi*) is the well-known particle. The action of the doer is attributed to the instrument, as in "the firewood cooks". **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- पूर्वीः (*pūrvīḥ*)—all
- चर्षणीः (*carṣaṇīḥ*)—the people
- अति (*ati*)—passing beyond
- आ याहि (*ā yāhi*)—graciously come to us
- अर्यः (*aryaḥ*)—you, the venerable
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- आशिषः (*āśiṣaḥ*)—accepting the prayers
- हरिभ्यां (*haribhyāṃ*)—together with the horses
- उप (*upa*)—come graciously
- सख्यं (*sakhyaṃ*)—your friendship
- जुषाणाः (*juṣāṇāḥ*)—desiring
- स्तोमतष्टाः (*stomataṣṭāḥ*)—fashioned by the praisers
- इमा मतयः (*imā matayaḥ*)—these praises
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- हवंते हि (*havaṃte hi*)—(for the drinking of Soma) call

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, passing beyond all the peoples, graciously come to us. Venerable one, accept our prayers and come graciously together with your horses. These praises, which desire your friendship and are fashioned by the praisers, call you for the drinking of Soma.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Passing by multitudes, come hither, noble Indra, with your steeds,
(to recive) our benedictions; for these praises, Indra, composed by the devout,
invoke you, propitiating your friendship.

### Printed p. 52

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

चर्षणी (*carṣaṇī*)—The explanation of this word has been given in detail on p. 49 of Part 2 of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā. In many contexts this word means "seer". The meaning "man" is also indicated in many contexts. For words such as चर्षणि (*carṣaṇi*) and कृष्टि (*kṛṣṭi*) the meaning "man" is indicated not directly but through tradition. Even so, this meaning is well known in usage: in places such as Ṛ. Saṃ. 5, 86, 2 : 7, 15, 2; 9, 101, 9, the words पंचचर्षणयः (*paṃcacarṣaṇayaḥ*) and पंचकृष्टयः (*paṃcakṛṣṭayaḥ*) are in use instead of पंचजनाः (*paṃcajanāḥ*). When deities such as Agni and Indra have epithets such as चर्षणीनां पतिः (*carṣaṇīnāṃ patiḥ*) and चर्षणि नां राजा (*carṣaṇi nāṃ rājā*) there is also some breadth of meaning; the special meanings suited to such contexts were stated earlier. But in contexts such as 5-23-4, where this word occurs as an epithet of Agni in विश्वचर्षणिः (*viśvacarṣaṇiḥ*), the meaning "seer of all" is natural. In this way the meaning must be given according to each context.
अर्यः (*aryaḥ*)—This word is read among the names of the Lord, such as राष्ट्री (*rāṣṭrī*) and अरः (*araḥ*) (Ni. 2-22). In this context it is rendered "venerable".
आशिषः (*āśiṣaḥ*)—In common usage the word आशीः (*āśīḥ*) is used in the sense of elders wishing good for the young. But in the Ṛk Saṃhitā it is used in the senses of prayer and desired object. For example, where Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-179-6 says सत्या देवेष्वाशिषो जगाम (*satyā deveṣvāśiṣo jagāma*), the purport is that the sage Agastya obtained all his desired objects. In the same way, in the present Ṛk also the meaning is "our prayers".

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ नो॑ य॒ज्ञं न॑मो॒वृधं॑ स॒जोषा॒ इंद्र॑ देव॒ हरि॑भिर्याहि॒  ॑
> तूयं ।  
> अ॒हं हि त्वा॑ म॒तिभि॒र्जोह॑वीमि घृ॒तप्र॑याः सध॒मादे॒  ॑
> मधूनां ॥ ३ ॥

> *ā no yajñaṃ namovṛdhaṃ sajoṣā iṃdra deva haribhiryāhi*  
> *tūyaṃ |*  
> *ahaṃ hi tvā matibhirjohavīmi ghṛtaprayāḥ sadhamāde*  
> *madhūnāṃ ‖ 3 ‖*

### Printed p. 53

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । नः । यज्ञं । नमःवृधं । सजोषां । इंद्र । देव । हरिभिः । याहि ।  
> तूयं ।  
> अहं । हि । त्व । मतिभिः । जोहवीमि । घ्प्तप्रयाः । सध्वमादे । मधूनां ॥ ३ ॥

> *ā | naḥ | yajñaṃ | namaḥvṛdhaṃ | sajoṣāṃ | iṃdra | deva | haribhiḥ | yāhi |*  
> *tūyaṃ |*  
> *ahaṃ | hi | tva | matibhiḥ | johavīmi | ghptaprayāḥ | sadhvamāde | madhūnāṃ ‖ 3 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> देव द्योतमान हे इंद्र नोऽस्माकं नमोवृधं नमसोन्नस्य वर्धकमिमं यज्ञं हरिभिरश्वैः सजोषाः सहप्रीतिस्त्वं तूयिं क्षिप्रमा याहि । धृतप्रयाः । धृतसहितानि प्रयांस्यन्यरूपाणि हवींषि यस्य स तथोक्तः । तादृशोऽहं मधूनां सेमानां सधमादे । सह माद्यंति यत्र सोम इति सधमादः ।
> तस्मिन् स्थाने मतिभिः स्तुतिभिस्ता त्वां जोहवीमि हि । भृशमाह्वयामि खलु ॥
> जोहवीमि । यज् लुक्यभ्यस्तस्य चेति संप्रसारणं । गुणो यज् लुकोरित्यभ्यासस्य
> गुणः । यजो वेतीडागमः । हियोगादनिघातः । अभ्यस्तानामादिरित्याद्युदात्तः ।
> धृतप्रयाः । घृ क्षरणदीप्त्योरित्यस्मादंजिघृषिभ्यः क्त इति क्तप्रत्ययः । बहुव्रीहौ
> पूर्वपदस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O shining Indra, with your horses and in accord with us (सजोषाः (*sajoṣāḥ*), with joint pleasure), come quickly to this sacrifice of ours, which increases the food (नमोवृधं (*namovṛdhaṃ*), the augmenter of नमस् (*namas*), food)." घृतप्रयाः (*ghṛtaprayāḥ*) is one whose offerings (prayāṃsi, other forms of oblation) are accompanied by ghee. Being such, "I invoke you vehemently with praises (मतिभिः (*matibhiḥ*)) in the सधमादे (*sadhamāde*) of the sweet Soma libations." A सधमादः (*sadhamādaḥ*) is the place where they rejoice together over Soma. जोहवीमि (*johavīmi*) means "I call greatly, indeed".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देव (*deva*)—shining
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- नमोवृधं (*namovṛdhaṃ*)—food-increasing
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—to the sacrifice
- हरिभिः (*haribhiḥ*)—with the horses
- सजोषाः (*sajoṣāḥ*)—joined with affection
- तूयं (*tūyaṃ*)—quickly
- आ याहि (*ā yāhi*)—come graciously
- घृतप्रयाः (*ghṛtaprayāḥ*)—endowed with oblations accompanied by ghee
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- मधूनां (*madhūnāṃ*)—of the Soma juices
- सधमादे (*sadhamāde*)—in the place of exhilaration
- मतिभिः (*matibhiḥ*)—with praises
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- जोहवीमि (*johavīmi*)—I pray and call

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Shining Indra, come quickly and affectionately, with your horses, to our food-increasing sacrifice. I, provided with oblations accompanied by ghee, pray to you and call you with praises in the place of exhilaration of the Soma juice.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Divine Indra, come quickly with your steeds, and well pleased, to our food-augmenting sacrifice; offering (sacred) food with butter, I invoke you verily with praises in the chamber of the sweet libations.

### Printed p. 54

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

सधमादे (*sadhamāde*)—The word is formed from the root मद् (*mad*) together with सह (*saha*). It means the region where all, drinking Soma together, find satisfaction. It is an epithet of the sacrificial region, which gives exhilaration to all.
मतिभिः (*matibhiḥ*)—It has been said in many earlier places that the word मति (*mati*) means speech in the form of praise, and the special meaning of this word has been explained.
नमोवृधं यज्ञं (*namovṛdhaṃ yajñaṃ*)—The meaning is the sacrifice that increases food. The idea well known from the Śruti, यज्ञाद्भवति पर्जन्यः, पर्जन्यादन्नसंभवः (*yajñādbhavati parjanyaḥ, parjanyādannasaṃbhavaḥ*), is expressed here. The sacrifice must cause rain, and through it the wealth of food must increase. The sacrifice is the root of all creation and preservation in the universe. And it is the root not only of the food that man needs but also of the increase of the food-wealth of the gods. As said in sentences such as यज्ञ उ देवानामन्नं (*yajña u devānāmannaṃ*) (Śa. Brā. 8-1-2-10), the sacrifice is also praised as the food of the gods.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ च॒ त्वामे॒ता वृष॑णा॒ वहा॑तो॒ हरी॒ सखा॑या सु॒धुरा॒  ॑
> स्वंगा ।  
> धा॒नाव॒दिंद्रः॒ सव॑नं जुषा॒णः सखा॒ सख्युः॑ शृणव॒द्वंद॑नानि॥४॥

> *ā ca tvāmetā vṛṣaṇā vahāto harī sakhāyā sudhurā*  
> *svaṃgā |*  
> *dhānāvadiṃdraḥ savanaṃ juṣāṇaḥ sakhā sakhyuḥ śṛṇavadvaṃdanāni‖4‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । च । त्वां । एता । वृषणा । वहातः । हरी इति । सखाया । सुधुरा । सुअंगा ।  
> धानावत् । इंद्रः । सवनं । जुषाणः । सखा । सख्युः । शृणवत् । वंदनानि ॥ ४ ॥

> *ā | ca | tvāṃ | etā | vṛṣaṇā | vahātaḥ | harī iti | sakhāyā | sudhurā | suaṃgā |*  
> *dhānāvat | iṃdraḥ | savanaṃ | juṣāṇaḥ | sakhā | sakhyuḥ | śṛṇavat | vaṃdanāni ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र वृषणा सेचनसमर्थौ सुधुरा शोभनया धुरान्वितौ स्वंगा शोभनावयवोपेतौ सखाया शोभनाख्यानावेतैतौ हरी त्वामा वहातश्च । यज्ञं
> प्रत्यागमनाय रथे त्वामावहतः । अथ धानावत् भृष्टयवोपेतं सवनं जुषाणः
> सेवमानः सखा सखिभूतः स इंद्रः सख्युः स्तोतुर्मम वंदनानि स्तोत्राणि शृण-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, these two horses, vigorous (वृषणा (*vṛṣaṇā*), able to sprinkle), well yoked to the pole (सुधुरा (*sudhurā*)), well limbed (स्वंगा (*svaṃgā*)), friendly (सखाया (*sakhāyā*), of good name), bring you in the chariot to come to the sacrifice. Then he, Indra, being a friend, accepting (जुषाणः (*juṣāṇaḥ*)) the sacrifice with parched grain (धानावत् (*dhānāvat*)), may hear (शृणवत् (*śṛṇavat*)) the praises (वंदनानि (*vaṃdanāni*)) of me, his friend (सख्युः (*sakhyuḥ*)), the praiser."

### Printed p. 55

> वत् । श्रुयात् ॥ वहातः । वहेर्लेट्याडागमे रूपं । चवायोगे। प्रथमेति न
> निघातः । श्रवणत् । श्रु श्रवण इत्यस्य लेट्यडागमे रूपं । निघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This part of the block gives only grammatical remarks on वहातः (*vahātaḥ*) and श्रवणत् (*śravaṇat*) (the leṭ forms with the augment aṭ) and on accent; the Grammar section carries them.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) वृषणा (*vṛṣaṇā*)—capable of sprinkling
- सुधुरा (*sudhurā*)—able to bear the burden
- स्वंगा (*svaṃgā*)—having excellent limbs
- सखाया (*sakhāyā*)—joined together
- एता हरी (*etā harī*)—these horses (of Indra)
- त्वां (*tvāṃ*)—you
- आ वहातः (*ā vahātaḥ*)—bring (to the sacrificial region)
- धानावत् (*dhānāvat*)—provided with ripe barley grain
- सवनं (*savanaṃ*)—the sacrifice
- जुषाणः (*juṣāṇaḥ*)—accepting
- सखा (*sakhā*)—being a friend
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—you, in the form of Indra
- सख्युः (*sakhyuḥ*)—of me, your friend
- वंदनासि (*vaṃdanāsi*)—the praises
- शृणवत् च (*śṛṇavat ca*)—be a hearer

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, these horses, capable of sprinkling, able to bear the burden, having excellent limbs and joined together, bring you to the sacrificial region. You, in the form of Indra, who accept the sacrifice provided with ripe barley grain and are a friend, be a hearer of the praises of me, your friend.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
May these two vigorous friendly burthen-bearing and well-limbed
steeds convey you hither, where, Indra, approving of the ceremony (in
which the offering) is parched grain, may hear as a friend the praises of his
friend (the worshipper)

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

वंदनानि (*vaṃdanāni*)—In common usage वंदन (*vaṃdana*) means salutation. But in the present context the word is used in the sense of praise. The word is also used in many places as a synonym for disease, demon and the like. For example, in the Ṛks beginning न यातव इंद्र जूजुवुर्नो न वंदना शविष्ठ वेद्याभिः । (*na yātava iṃdra jūjuvurno na vaṃdanā śaviṣṭha vedyābhiḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-21-5) this word means a demon. वंदन (*vaṃdana*) is also a well-known name of a sage.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कु॒विन्ना॑ गो॒पां कर॑से॒ जन॑स्य कु॒विद्राजा॑नं मघवन्नृजीषिन् ।  
> कु॒विन्न॒ ऋषिं॑ पपि॒वांसं॑ सु॒तस्य॑ कु॒विन्ने॒ वस्वो॑ अ॒मृत॑स्य॒  ॑
> शिक्षाः ॥ ५ ॥

> *kuvinnā gopāṃ karase janasya kuvidrājānaṃ maghavannṛjīṣin |*  
> *kuvinna ṛṣiṃ papivāṃsaṃ sutasya kuvinne vasvo amṛtasya*  
> *śikṣāḥ ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कुवित् । मा । गोपां । करसे । जनस्य । कुवित् । राजानं । मघवन् । ऋजेषिन् ।  
> कुवित् । मा । ऋषिं । पप्सिवांसं । सुतस्य । कुवित् । मे । वस्वः । अमृतस्य । शिक्षाः॥

> *kuvit | mā | gopāṃ | karase | janasya | kuvit | rājānaṃ | maghavan | ṛjeṣin |*  
> *kuvit | mā | ṛṣiṃ | papsivāṃsaṃ | sutasya | kuvit | me | vasvaḥ | amṛtasya | śikṣāḥ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र कुविदपि च मा मां जनस्य गोपां गोप्तारं करसे । कुरु । तथा
> हे मघवन्छनवन्नृजीषिन्नोमवन् हे इंद्र मां राजानं सर्वस्य स्वामिनं कुरु ।
> कुविच्च मा मामृषिमतींद्रियार्थस्य प्रष्टारं कुरु । ऋषिर्दशनादिति यास्कः । तथा
> सुतस्याभिषुतस्य सोमस्य पपिवांसं पातारं कुरु । अपि च मे मह्यममृतस्य
> क्षयरहितं वस्वो वसु शिक्षाः । प्रयच्छ ॥ करसे । कृञ् करणे । लेट्यडागमे रूपं।
> कुविद्योगादनिघातः । म ऋषिमित्यत्र संहितायामृत्यक इति प्रकृतिभावो ह्रस्वश्च ।
> पपिवांसं । पा पान इत्यस्य क्वसौ वस्वेकाजाद्भसामितीडागमः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । शिक्षाः ।
> शिक्षतिर्दानकर्मा । आस्माल्लेट्प्याडमे रूपं । कुविद्योगादनिघातः । आगमा अनुदात्ता इत्याटोऽनुदात्तत्वे धातुस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, make me, many times (कुवित् (*kuvit*)), the protector (गोपां (*gopāṃ*)) of the people. O wealthy (मघवन् (*maghavan*)), Soma-possessing Indra, make me king, the lord of all. Make me also a ऋषिं (*ṛṣiṃ*), a questioner of supersensuous matters; "a seer from seeing", says Yāska. Make me also a drinker (पपिवांसं (*papivāṃsaṃ*)) of the pressed Soma. And give me imperishable wealth (अमृतस्य (*amṛtasya*) वस्वः (*vasvaḥ*)). शिक्षाः (*śikṣāḥ*) means 'give'; the root शिक्षति (*śikṣati*) has the sense of giving." **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) कुवित् (*kuvit*)—many times
- मा (*mā*)—me
- जनस्य (*janasya*)—for the people
- गोपां (*gopāṃ*)—as protector
- करसे (*karase*)—make
- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—wealthy
- ऋजीषिन् (*ṛjīṣin*)—Soma-possessing Indra
- मा (*mā*)—me
- कुवित् (*kuvit*)—likewise
- राजानं (*rājānaṃ*)—make a lord
- कुवित् (*kuvit*)—otherwise
- ऋषिं (*ṛṣiṃ*)—make one capable of seeing supersensuous matters
- सुतस्य (*sutasya*)—of the pressed Soma juice
- पपिवांसं (*papivāṃsaṃ*)—make a drinker
- मे (*me*)—to me
- अमृतस्य (*amṛtasya*)—imperishable
- वस्वः (*vasvaḥ*)—wealth
- शिक्षाः (*śikṣāḥ*)—give

### Printed p. 57

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, make me the protector of the people. O Indra, possessor of wealth and of Soma, make me a lord. Otherwise, make me one able to see the supersensuous meaning. Make me qualified to drink the Soma juice. Give me imperishable wealth.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Possessor of wealth, accept of the spiritless Soma juice; make me the protector, or rather a monarch of men; make me verily a holy sage, a drinker of the libation; make me verily the possessor of imperishable wealth.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Kuvit: this word is read among the "many names" of the Nirukta (Ni. 3-1). In the Ṛk Saṃhitā also, in places such as कुवित्स देवीः सनयो नवो वा (*kuvitsa devīḥ sanayo navo vā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-51-4), it is used in the sense "many times, in many ways". In the present Ṛk the word is employed in three senses: first in the established sense कुवित्-अपि च (*kuvit-api ca*); second in a conjunctive sense; the third time in the contrary sense "or".

Ṛṣi: the word ṛṣi is explained here as a knower of reality (tattvajña) who has the capacity to see supersensuous meanings. That the word can bear this meaning was shown earlier in the Special Topics of Ṛk 1-179-6 (Ṛ. Saṃ.). In general the word ṛṣi is used in the Ṛk Saṃhitā in the sense of a poet (kavi) who sees beyond the ordinary:

अग्निः पूर्वभिर्यषिभिरीड्यः (*agniḥ pūrvabhiryaṣibhirīḍyaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-1-2);
ये च पूर्व ऋषयो ये च नूत्नाः (*ye ca pūrva ṛṣayo ye ca nūtnāḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-22-9);
तमेव ऋषिं तमु ब्रह्माणमाहुः (*tameva ṛṣiṃ tamu brahmāṇamāhuḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-107 6).

In such contexts the meaning "poet" is well established, and in contexts such as अग्निं मनुष्या ऋषयः समीधिरे (*agniṃ manuṣyā ṛṣayaḥ samīdhire*) (10-150-4) the meaning "praiser" is well established. The meaning "hotṛ" also becomes possible in some places.

> वहंतु ।

> *a tvā bṛhaṃto harayo yujānā arvāgiṃdra sadhamādo*
