> प्र । ये । द्विता । दिवः । यंजंति । आताः । सुसंमृष्टासः । वृषभस्य । मूराः ॥ ६ ॥

> *ā | tvā | bṛhaṃtaḥ | harayaḥ | yujānāḥ | arvāk | iṃdra | sadhvamādaḥ | vahaṃtu |*  
> *pra | ye | dvitā | divaḥ | yaṃjaṃti | ātāḥ | susaṃmṛṣṭāsaḥ | vṛṣabhasya | mūrāḥ ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वा त्वां बृहंतो महांतो युजाना रथे युज्यमाना
> हरयोऽश्वाः सधमादः सह मादयंतः संतोऽर्वाक् अस्मदभिमुखमा वहंतु ।
> वृषभस्य कामानां वर्षितुरिंद्रस्य मूराः शत्रोणां मारकाः सुसंमृष्टास इंद्रस्य
> हस्ताभ्यां पृष्ठभागे सुष्टु संस्पृष्टा दिवो नभसः सकाशादागच्छंतो येऽश्वा
> आताः । अभिमुख्यन गम्यंते प्राणिभिस्तत्तत्कार्यं प्रतीत्याता दिशः । सर्वा दिशो
> द्विता द्वैधं यथा भवति तथा प्र ऋंजंति । प्रकर्षण साधयंति ॥ युजानाः ।
> युजिर् योगे युजिबुधिदृशः किच्च । उ. २.९०. । इत्यानच् । चित्वादंतोदात्तः ।
> सधमादः । मदी हर्ष इत्यस्माण्यंतात् क्विप् । कृदुत्तरपदस्वरः । द्विता । द्विशब्दात्संख्याया विधार्थे धेति धाप्रत्ययः । धकारस्य तकारश्चांदसः । प्रत्ययस्वरः ।
> दिवः । ऊडिदमिति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं । आताः । आत सातत्यगमन इत्यस्मात्कर्मणि घञ् ।
> व्यत्ययेनाद्युदात्तः ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, may the great (बृहंतः (*bṛhaṃtaḥ*)) horses, yoked to the chariot, delighting together (सधमादः (*sadhamādaḥ*)), bring you hither, toward us. Those horses are the slayers (मूराः (*mūrāḥ*)) of the enemies of Indra, the "bull", the showerer of desires. They are well rubbed (सुसंमृष्टासः (*susaṃmṛṣṭāsaḥ*)) on the back by Indra's two hands, and they come from heaven, from the sky. They are ātāḥ: the directions are called ātāḥ because creatures go toward them for their various tasks. These horses accomplish (प्र ऋंजंति (*pra ṛṃjaṃti*): they bring to completion eminently) all the directions in a twofold manner (द्विता (*dvitā*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- बृहंतः (*bṛhaṃtaḥ*)—of great form
- युजानाः (*yujānāḥ*)—yoked to the chariot
- हरयः (*harayaḥ*)—horses
- सधमादः (*sadhamādaḥ*)—rejoicing together
- अर्वाक् (*arvāk*)—toward us
- आ वहंतु (*ā vahaṃtu*)—let them bring
- वृषभस्य (*vṛṣabhasya*)—of Indra, the showerer of desires
- मूराः (*mūrāḥ*)—destroyers (of enemies)
- सुसंमृष्टासः (*susaṃmṛṣṭāsaḥ*)—(lovingly) patted on the back
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—from the atmosphere
- ये (*ye*)—which, those very horses
- आताः (*ātāḥ*)—in all directions
- द्विता (*dvitā*)—doubled
- प्र ऋंजंति (*pra ṛṃjaṃti*)—(exceedingly) shine

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, may the great horses, yoked to the chariot, rejoicing together, bring you toward us. Those same horses of Indra, the showerer of desires, destroyers of enemies, patted on the back, shine doubled in all the directions.

### Printed p. 59

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
May your mighty horses, Indra, harnessed (in your car), and alike exhilarated (by the beverage) bring you to our presence, for they are the destroyers (of the foes) of the showerer (of benefits), and kindly rubbed by his hand they traverse, going quickly, the regions of the sky, making them as it were twofold.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Mūrāḥ: for the word mūra the meaning "foolish" (mūḍha), i.e. ignorant, is generally well known; mūra is popularly an ignorant person and amūra a knowing one. In the present Ṛk, however, Sāyaṇa explains the word as a destroying power. The scholar Monier Wiliam, explaining this word, cites this very Ṛk and in his dictionary gives the meaning "Rushing, impetuous"; he also records Sāyaṇa's opinion "destroyer" (māraka). Besides, it also means "firm, secure, well established". The word is also used in the sense of "root, base" (A. Ve. 1-28-3).**NOTE**

Ātāḥ: since it is the means by which birds and others move here and there, ātā is a synonym for the side of a direction. In many places it also means the door of a shutter.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॒ पिब॒ वृष॑धूतस्य॒ वृष्ण॒ आ यं ते॑ श्ये॒न उ॑श॒ते ज॒भार॑ ।  
> यस्य॒ मदे॑ च्या॒वय॑सि॒ प्र कृ॒ष्टीर्यस्य॒ मदे॒ अप॑ गो॒त्रा  ॒॑
> ववर्थ ॥ ७ ॥

> *iṃdra piba vṛṣadhūtasya vṛṣṇa ā yaṃ te śyena uśate jabhāra |*  
> *yasya made cyāvayasi pra kṛṣṭīryasya made apa gotrā*  
> *vavartha ‖ 7 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्र । पिब । वृषधूतस्य । वृष्णः ! आ । यं । ते । श्येनः । उशते । जभार ।  
> यस्य । मदे । च्यवयसि । प्र । कृष्टीः । यस्य । मदे । अप । गोत्रा । ववर्थ ॥ ७ ॥

> *iṃdra | piba | vṛṣadhūtasya | vṛṣṇaḥ ! ā | yaṃ | te | śyenaḥ | uśate | jabhāra |*  
> *yasya | made | cyavayasi | pra | kṛṣṭīḥ | yasya | made | apa | gotrā | vavartha ‖ 7 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र वृषधूतस्य ग्रावभिरभिषुतं वृष्णोऽभिमतफलसेचकं सोमं
> पिब । श्येनः शंसनीयगतिश्चंदोरूपः सुपर्ण उशते सोमं कामयमानाय

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, drink the Soma that is pressed with the stones (ग्रावभिरभिषुतं (*grāvabhirabhiṣutaṃ*)) and is the sprinkler (वृष्णः (*vṛṣṇaḥ*)) of the desired fruit. The Śyena, the hawk of praiseworthy flight, who has the form of a metre, brought (जभार (*jabhāra*): आजहार (*ājahāra*)) to you, who desire Soma, that Soma.

### Printed p. 60

> ते तुभ्यं यं सोममा जभार । आजहार । यस्य सोमस्य मदे हर्ष संजाते
> सति कृष्टीः । आकर्षंति वशीकुर्वंति कर्मणा सर्वानिति कृस्टयो मनुष्याः । तान्
> सपत्नभूतान्मनुष्यान् प्र च्यावयसि । प्रकर्षण पातयसि । यस्य सोमस्य मदे
> संजाते सति गोत्रा । गामुदकं रश्मिभिरावृतं वर्षास्वतुषु त्रायंते पालयंतीति
> गोत्रा मुघाः । तानह ववर्थ । अपवृणेषि ॥ वृषधूतस्य । धूञ् कंपन इत्यस्य
> निष्ठायां यस्य विभाषेतीट् प्रतिषेधः । तृतीया कर्मणीति पूर्वपदस्वरः । शैनः ।
> श्येज् गतौ । श्यास्ताहृञ्विभ्य इनच् । उ २.४६. । इतीनच् प्रत्ययः । श्यायत इति
> शैनः । चित्तादंतोदात्तः । जभार । हरतेर्लिटि णलि रूपं । हृग्रहोर्भश्चंदसीति
> भकारः । यद्वृत्तयोगादनिघातः । लित्स्वरः । मदे । मदी हर्षे । मदोऽनुपसर्ग
> इत्यप्रत्ययः । तस्य पित्वादनुदात्तत्वे धातुस्वरः । गोत्रा । त्रैज् पालन इत्यस्यातोऽनुपसर्गे क इति कः । सुपो डादेशः । एकादेशस्वरः । ववर्थ । वृञ् वरण इत्यस्य
> लिट थल्यतो भारद्वाजस्येतीट् प्रतिषेधः । गुणः लित्स्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When joy is born from that Soma, you make fall, you cast down eminently, the kṛṣṭayaḥ, human beings who are your rivals (kṛṣṭayaḥ are people who draw all, bring all under control, by their action). When joy is born from that Soma, you uncover (अप ववर्थ (*apa vavartha*): अपवृणेषि (*apavṛṇeṣi*)) the gotrās, the clouds, which in the rainy seasons protect (त्रायंते पालयंतीति (*trāyaṃte pālayaṃtīti*)) the water enveloped in rays (go means water).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- यं (*yaṃ*)—which Soma
- श्येनः (*śyenaḥ*)—the Śyena bird in the form of a metre
- उशते (*uśate*)—to you who desired it
- ते (*te*)—for you
- आ जभार (*ā jabhāra*)—brought (and)
- यस्य (*yasya*)—by drinking which Soma juice
- मदे (*made*)—becoming exhilarated
- कृष्टीः (*kṛṣṭīḥ*)—men (who are enemies)
- प्रच्यावयसि (*pracyāvayasi*)—you beat down and drive away (and)
- यस्य (*yasya*)—by drinking which Soma juice
- मदे (*made*)—becoming exhilarated
- गोत्रा (*gotrā*)—the clouds
- अप ववर्थ (*apa vavartha*)—you split open; such
- वृषधूतस्य (*vṛṣadhūtasya*)—pressed by the pressing stones
- वृष्णः (*vṛṣṇaḥ*)—the Soma that grants desires
- पिब (*piba*)—drink

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, drink that pressed Soma juice, the granter of desires, which the Śyena bird in the form of a metre brought for you who desired it; by drinking which, rejoicing, you beat down the enemies; and by drinking which Soma, exhilarated, you split open the clouds for the sake of rain.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Drink, Indra, of the bountiful (libation) expressed by the beneficent (stones); that which the falcon has borne to you desiring it, in whose exhilaration you cast down (opposing) men, in whose exhilaration you have set open the clouds.

### Printed p. 61

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Śyena: the occasion on which the Śyena bird, of the nature of the Gāyatrī, brought the Soma was told earlier, and it will be told later when the greatness of the Gāyatrī is described.

Kṛṣṭīḥ: the special meanings of the words kṛṣṭi and carṣaṇi were explained earlier. The uses of the words pañcakṛṣṭayaḥ and pañcajanāḥ, which signify identity, were also explained.

Gotrā: the word gotra generally means a cowshed that protects cows; this is well known, and the same meaning is indicated in the Nirukta (Ni. 1-2). In the present Ṛk gotra is explained as "cloud". Since the word go is a synonym of water, and since the cloud holds back and protects the water, gotra also means "cloud". The special meaning of this word will be explained when Indra's valour is described with the word गोत्रभित् (*gotrabhit*).

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> शु॒नं हु॑वेम म॒घवा॑न॒मिंद्र॑म॒स्मिन्भरे॒ नृत॑मं॒ वाज॑सात् ।  
> शृ॒ण्वंत॑मु॒ग्रमू॒तये॑ स॒मत्तु॒ घ्नंतं॑ वृ॒त्राणि॑ सं॒जितं॒  ॑
> धॆनानां ॥ ८ ॥

> *śunaṃ huvema maghavānamiṃdramasminbhare nṛtamaṃ vājasāt |*  
> *śṛṇvaṃtamugramūtaye samattu ghnaṃtaṃ vṛtrāṇi saṃjitaṃ*  
> *dhènānāṃ ‖ 8 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शुनं । हुवेम । मघवानं । इंद्रं । अस्मिन् । भरे । नृतमं । वाजसात् ।  
> शृण्वंतं । उग्रं । ऊतये । समत्सु । घ्नंतं । वृत्राणि । संजितं । धनानां ॥

> *śunaṃ | huvema | maghavānaṃ | iṃdraṃ | asmin | bhare | nṛtamaṃ | vājasāt |*  
> *śṛṇvaṃtaṃ | ugraṃ | ūtaye | samatsu | ghnaṃtaṃ | vṛtrāṇi | saṃjitaṃ | dhanānāṃ ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र वाजसात् । वाजस्यान्नस्य सातिर्लाभो यस्मिन् सोऽयं वाजसातिः।
> अस्मिन्भरे । बिभ्रति जयलक्ष्मीमनेन योद्धार इति भरः संग्रामः । तस्मिन् संग्रामे
> शुनं शूनमुत्साहेन प्रवृद्धं । यद्वा शुनमिति सुखनाम । सुखकरं मघवानं धनवंतं आत एवेंद्रं निरतिशयैश्वर्यसंपन्नं नृतमं सर्वस्य जगतोऽतिशयेन
> नेतारं त्वां हुवेम । कुशिका वयं यज्ञार्थमाह्वयेम । तथा तृण्यंतमस्माभिः
> क्रियमाणां स्तुतिं शृण्यंतमुग्रं शत्रूणां भयंकरं समत्तु संग्रामेषु वृत्राणि

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, vājasāt: the one in whom there is the gain (सातिः (*sātiḥ*)) of food (वाज (*vāja*)). Bhara is battle, since in it warriors bear (बिभ्रति (*bibhrati*)) the goddess of victory. In this battle we call you, who are śuna, swollen, grown through enthusiasm; or else śuna is a name for happiness, so "making happiness"; who are wealthy (मघवानं (*maghavānaṃ*)), and therefore Indra endowed with unsurpassed lordship, the supreme leader (नृतमं (*nṛtamaṃ*)) of the whole world. We Kuśikas call you for the sacrifice. We call you who hear the praise made by us, fierce (उग्रं (*ugraṃ*)), a terror to enemies, in the battles (समत्सु (*samatsu*))…

### Printed p. 62

> वृत्रोपलक्षितानि सर्वाणि रक्षांसि घ्नंतं हिंसंतं धनानां शत्रुसंबंधिनां संजितं
> सम्यग्नेतारं त्वामूतये रक्षणाय वयमाह्वयेम ॥ शुनं । टुओश्चि गतिवृद्ध्योरित्यस्य निष्ठायां यस्य विभाषेतीट् प्रतिषेधः । यजादित्वात्संप्रसारणं । दीर्घाभावश्चांदसः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । हुवेम । ह्वयतेर्बहुलं छंदसीति संप्रसारणे कृते
> शपो लुकि कृते च लिजि रूपं । निघातः । भरे । बृञ् भरणे । घः । वृषादित्वादाद्युदात्तः । वाजसात् । षणु दाने । भावे क्तिन् । जनसनेत्यादिनानुनासिकस्यात्वं ।
> बहुप्रीहौ पूर्वपदस्वरः । समत्तु । अद भक्षणे । संपूर्वात् क्विप् । संभक्षयंति
> योद्धाणामायूंशीति समदः संग्रामाः ! कृदुत्तरपदस्वरः । घ्नंतं । हन हिंसागत्योरित्यस्य शतरि गमहनेत्यादिनोपधालोपः । हो हंतेरिति घत्वं । संजितं ।
> जि जये । क्विप् !'

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

…who destroy (घ्नंतं (*ghnaṃtaṃ*)) all the Rākṣasas, who are called by the name Vṛtra; who is the good leader and conqueror (संजितं (*saṃjitaṃ*)) of the wealth belonging to the enemies. We call you for protection (ऊतये (*ūtaye*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- वाजसात् (*vājasāt*)—proceeding for the sake of food
- भरे (*bhare*)—in the battle
- शुनं (*śunaṃ*)—who grow through enthusiasm, or who produce happiness
- मघवानं (*maghavānaṃ*)—wealthy
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—endowed with unsurpassed lordship
- नृतमं (*nṛtamaṃ*)—the leader; you
- हुवेम (*huvema*)—we invoke and call (likewise)
- शृण्वंतं (*śṛṇvaṃtaṃ*)—who listen (to our praises)
- उग्रं (*ugraṃ*)—fearsome to enemies
- समत्तु (*samattu*)—in battles
- वृत्राणि (*vṛtrāṇi*)—the Asuras beginning with Vṛtra
- घ्नंतं (*ghnaṃtaṃ*)—who injure and destroy
- धनानां (*dhanānāṃ*)—the wealth (of enemies)
- संचितं (*saṃcitaṃ*)—who gather and give (to us); you
- ऊतये (*ūtaye*)—for protection
- हवेम (*havema*)—(we invoke and call)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, in the battle waged for the sake of food, you who produce happiness, are wealthy, are endowed with unsurpassed lordship, are the leader, listen to our praises, are fearsome to enemies, injure and destroy Vṛtra and the other Asuras in battles, and gather the wealth of the enemies and give it to us—you we invoke and call for protection.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
We invoke for our protection the opulent Indra, distinguished in this combat, the leader in the food-bestowing (strife), hearing (our praises), terri-ble in battles, the destroyer of foes, the conqueror of wealth.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The special topics of this Ṛk have already been explained earlier.

## Sūkta 44 (Maṇḍala 3)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयं ते अस्त्विति पंचर्चं षष्ठं सूक्तं वैश्वामित्रं बार्हतमैंद्रं ।
> अत्रानुक्रमणिका । आयं ते पंच बार्हतं त्विति ॥ महाव्रते बार्हत्यां तृचाशीताविदं सूक्तं । ऐ. आ ५२ ॥ षोडशिशस्त्रेयं ते अस्तु हर्यत इत्याद्यस्तृचो विनियुक्तः ! सूत्रितं च । आयं ते अस्तु हर्यत इत्यौष्टिहबार्हतौ तृचौ । आ. ६.२ ।
> इति !!
> अनुवादवु — आयं ते अस्तु ऎंब ई सूक्तवु नाल्कनॆय अनुवाकदल्लि आरनॆय सूक्तवु.
> इदरल्लि ऐदु ऋक्कुगळिरुववु. ई सूक्तक्कॆ विश्वामित्रनु ऋषियु, इंद्रनु देवतॆयु, बृहती
> छंदस्सु- अनुक्रमणिकॆयल्लि—आयं ते पंच बार्हतं तु ऎंदु हेळिरुवुदु. महाव्रत
> दल्लि बृहती छंदस्सिन तृच (मूरु ऋक्कु) क्कागि ई सूक्तद विनियोगविरुवुदॆंदु ऐतरेयारण्य
> कदल्लियू (ऐ. आ. ५-२) षोडशियॆंब यागदल्लि शस्त्रमंत्रपठनकालदल्लि आयं ते अस्तु
> हर्यतः ऎंब ई सूक्तद मॊदलनॆय मूरु ऋक्कुगळ विनियोगविरुवुदॆंदु अश्वलायनश्रौत
> सूत्रद—आयं ते अस्तु हर्यत इत्यौष्ठिहबार्हत् तृचौ ऎंब सूत्रदिंदलू विवृत
> वागिरुवुदु (आ. ६-२).
> ॥ ओं ॥
> सूक्त—४४
> मंडल—३ ॥ अनुवाक—४ ॥ सूक्त—४४ ॥
> अष्टक—३ ॥ अध्याय—३ ॥ वर्ग—८ ॥
> ॥ सूक्तदल्लिरुव ऋक्संख्यॆ—५ ॥
> ॥ ऋषिः—विश्वामित्रः ॥
> ॥ देवता—इंद्रः ॥
> ॥ छंदः—बृहती ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta अयं ते अस्तु (*ayaṃ te astu*), of five Ṛks, is the sixth sūkta; its seer is Viśvāmitra, it is Bārhata in metre and addressed to Indra. On it the Anukramaṇikā says आयं ते पंच बार्हतं त्विति (*āyaṃ te paṃca bārhataṃ tviti*). In the Mahāvrata this sūkta is used for the Bṛhatī tṛca: महाव्रते बार्हत्यां तृचाशीताविदं सूक्तं (*mahāvrate bārhatyāṃ tṛcāśītāvidaṃ sūktaṃ*) (Ai. Ā. 52). In the Ṣoḍaśin śastra the first tṛca of it, beginning अयं ते अस्तु हर्यतः (*ayaṃ te astu haryataḥ*), is applied; and it is given in the sūtra: आयं ते अस्तु हर्यत इत्यौष्टिहबार्हतौ तृचौ (*āyaṃ te astu haryata ityauṣṭihabārhatau tṛcau*) (Ā. 6-2).

The Anuvāda adds the particulars: Maṇḍala 3, Anuvāka 4, Sūkta 44; Aṣṭaka 3, Adhyāya 3, Varga 8; number of Ṛks in the sūkta 5; seer Viśvāmitra; deity Indra; metre Bṛhatī. It gives the Aitareya Āraṇyaka reference as Ai. Ā. 5-2 and the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra reference as 6-2.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒यं ते॑ अस्तु हर्य॒तः सोम॒ आ हरि॑भिः सु॒तः ।  
> जु॒षा॒ण इं॑द्र॒ हरि॑भिर्न॒ आ ग॒ह्या ति॑ष्ठ॒ हरि॑तं॒ रथं॑ ॥ १ ॥

> *ayaṃ te astu haryataḥ soma ā haribhiḥ sutaḥ |*  
> *juṣāṇa iṃdra haribhirna ā gahyā tiṣṭha haritaṃ rathaṃ ‖ 1 ‖*

### Printed p. 64

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अयं । ते । अस्तु । हर्यतः । सोमः । आ । हरिभिः । सुतः ।  
> जुषाणः । इंद्र । हरिभिः । नः । आ । गहि । आ । तिष्ठ । हरितं । रथं ॥ १ ॥

> *ayaṃ | te | astu | haryataḥ | somaḥ | ā | haribhiḥ | sutaḥ |*  
> *juṣāṇaḥ | iṃdra | haribhiḥ | naḥ | ā | gahi | ā | tiṣṭha | haritaṃ | rathaṃ ‖ 1 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र हरिभिः । हरंति सोमरसानेभिरिति हरयो ग्रावाणः । तैरा सुतः
> सर्वतोऽभिषुतो हर्यतः कमनीयो जषाणः प्रीतिविषयोऽयं सोमस्ते
> तुभ्यमस्तु । त्वं हरिभिरश्वैर्युक्तं हरितं हरिद्वर्णं रथमा तिष्ठ । अधितिष्य । ततो
> नोऽस्मानभिलक्ष्या गहि । आगच्छ ॥ हर्यतः । हर्य गतिकांतोरित्यस्मादृमृदृशीत्यादिनातच् प्रत्ययः । चित्वादंतोदात्तः । हरितं । हृञ् हरणे । दृश्याभ्यामितन् ।
> उ. ३-९३ । इतीतन्न प्रत्ययः ! नित्वादाद्युदात्तः । रथं । रमु क्रीडायां । हनिकुषिनीत्यादिना क्फन्न प्रत्ययः । कित्वादनुनासिकलोपः । नित्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, the harayaḥ are the pressing-stones, so called because they carry off (हरंति (*haraṃti*)) the Soma juice. This Soma, pressed all round by them, desirable and an object of delight, is for you. Mount the green-hued chariot yoked with tawny horses (हरिभिः (*haribhiḥ*)), and then come towards us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- हरिभिः (*haribhiḥ*)—by the pressing-stones
- आ सुतः (*ā sutaḥ*)—fully pressed
- हर्यतः (*haryataḥ*)—attractive
- जुषाणः (*juṣāṇaḥ*)—dear, an object of delight
- आयं सोमः (*āyaṃ somaḥ*)—this Soma juice
- ते (*te*)—directed to you
- अस्तु (*astu*)—is ready
- हरिभिः (*haribhiḥ*)—joined with horses
- हरितं (*haritaṃ*)—of green colour
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—in the chariot
- आ तिष्ठ (*ā tiṣṭha*)—mount and sit
- नः (*naḥ*)—towards us
- आ गहि (*ā gahi*)—be gracious to come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, this Soma juice, fully pressed by the pressing-stones, attractive and dear, is ready for you. Mount and sit in the green-coloured chariot yoked with horses, and be gracious to come towards us.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
May this desirable and gratifying Soma expressed by the stones, be,
Indra, for you : ascend the verdant chariot, and with your tawny (steeds)
come to us.

### Printed p. 65
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ह॒र्यन्नु॒षस॑मर्चयः॒ सूर्यं॑ ह॒र्यन्न॑रोचयः ।  
> वि॒द्वांश्चि॑कि॒त्वान्ह॑र्यश्व वर्धस॒ इंद्र॒ विश्वा॑ अ॒भि श्रियः॑ ॥ २ ॥

> *haryannuṣasamarcayaḥ sūryaṃ haryannarocayaḥ |*  
> *vidvāṃścikitvānharyaśva vardhasa iṃdra viśvā abhi śriyaḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> हर्यन् । उषसं । अर्चयः । सूर्यं । हर्यन् । आरोचयः ।  
> विद्वान् । चिकित्वान् । हरिअश्व । वर्धसे । इंद्र । विश्वाः । अभि । श्रियः ॥ २ ॥

> *haryan | uṣasaṃ | arcayaḥ | sūryaṃ | haryan | ārocayaḥ |*  
> *vidvān | cikitvān | hariaśva | vardhase | iṃdra | viśvāḥ | abhi | śriyaḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र हर्यन् सोमं कामयमानस्त्वमुषसमुषःकालमर्चयः ।
> पूजयसि । तथा हर्यन्नुदिते सूर्यॆ सोमं कामयमानस्त्वं सूर्यमरोचयः । दीपयसि । हे हर्यश्वेंद्र विद्यानेतत्सर्वं जानानश्चि कित्वानस्मदभिलषितफलविषयज्ञानवांस्तं विश्वाः सर्वाः श्रियो नः संपदोऽभि वर्धसे । अभिमतफलप्रदानेनाभितो वर्धयसि ॥ चिकित्वान् । कित ज्ञान इत्यस्य क्वसौ रूपं । प्रत्ययस्वरः ।
> वर्धसे । वृधु वर्धन इत्य स्यांतर्भावितण्यर्थस्य लटि रूपं । निघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, desiring (हर्यन् (*haryan*)) Soma, you honour the Dawn, the time of dawn. Likewise, desiring Soma when the sun has risen, you make the sun shine. O Indra, lord of tawny horses, knowing all this and aware of the fruit we desire, you increase all our prosperity on every side by granting the desired fruit.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra, you
- हर्यन् (*haryan*)—desiring the drinking of Soma
- उषसं (*uṣasaṃ*)—the time of dawn
- अर्चयः (*arcayaḥ*)—you honour
- हर्यन् (*haryan*)—desiring the Soma (offered after sunrise)
- सूरं (*sūraṃ*)—the sun
- अरोचयः (*arocayaḥ*)—you make shine
- हरश्व (*haraśva*)—O Indra, possessing horses named Hari
- विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—knowing everything
- चिकित्वान् (*cikitvān*)—knowing our desired ends
- विश्वाः श्रियः (*viśvāḥ śriyaḥ*)—all riches
- अभि वर्धसे (*abhi vardhase*)—you cause to increase among us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, desiring the drinking of the Soma offered before sunrise, you honour the time of dawn. Then, desiring the Soma offered afterwards, you make the sun shine. O Indra, possessor of the horses named Hari, being all-knowing and knowing our desired ends, you cause all riches to increase among us.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Desiring (the Soma), you honour the dawn ; desiring the Soma,
you have lighted up the sun ; knowing and discriminating (all our wishes),
lord of the tawny steeds, you augment upon us all (sorts of) prosperity.

### Printed p. 66

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

As the words of the Supreme Person say, उत्तीदेयुरिमे लोकाः न कुर्यां कर्म चेदहं (*uttīdeyurime lokāḥ na kuryāṃ karma cedahaṃ*)**NOTE**—"if I did not perform action, all the worlds would perish"—so, if Indra, the lord of the universe, were not constantly engaged in action useful for the conduct of the world, the ruin of the world would be certain. He is the cause of the appearance of Dawn and of the rising of the sun. As described in स्वराजः, महतः, ते (*svarājaḥ, mahataḥ, te*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-46-1), he alone is lord of the whole universe and holds sovereign rank. Even so, because the performance of sacrifice nourishes the world and is the cause of the growth of prosperity of the whole world, of gods, men and others, it is said that Indra honoured Dawn and the Sun, the primary causes of that sacrifice. The purport of the present Ṛk is that, though himself the lord, he worshipped his own creation, which nourishes the world.

The two epithets विद्वान् (*vidvān*) and चिकित्वान् (*cikitvān*) are also noteworthy here. Indra is all-knowing and knows the intention of the sacrificer. Therefore, being the cause of the appearance of Dawn so as to suit the mutual dealings of gods and men, he worshipped that Dawn of great form. In this sūkta, from beginning to end, a spiritual principle lies hidden. The epithet हरस्व (*harasva*) and descriptions such as सूर‍्यं अरोचयः (*sūryaṃ arocayaḥ*) and उषसं अर्चयः (*uṣasaṃ arcayaḥ*) indicate this hidden meaning. Although an explanation of these is quite necessary, there is no room here for a detailed one, because of limits of space. It will be explained later as occasion arises.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> द्यामिंद्रो॒ हरि॑धायसं पृथि॒वीं हरि॑वर्पसं ।  
> अधा॑रयुद्ध॒रितो॒र्भूरि॒ भोज॑नं॒ ययो॑रं॒तर्हरि॒श्चर॑त् ॥३॥

> *dyāmiṃdro haridhāyasaṃ pṛthivīṃ harivarpasaṃ |*  
> *adhārayuddharitorbhūri bhojanaṃ yayoraṃtarhariścarat ‖3‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> द्यां । इंद्रः । हरिधायसं । सृथिवीं । हरिवर्पसं ।  
> अधारयत् । हरितोः । भूरि । भोजनं । ययोः । अंतः । हरिः । चरत् ॥ ३ ॥

> *dyāṃ | iṃdraḥ | haridhāyasaṃ | sṛthivīṃ | harivarpasaṃ |*  
> *adhārayat | haritoḥ | bhūri | bhojanaṃ | yayoḥ | aṃtaḥ | hariḥ | carat ‖ 3 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सोऽयमिंद्रो हरिधायसं । हरितो हरितवर्णा धायसो धारका
> रत्नयो यस्याः सा । तां द्यामधारयत् । तथा हरिवर्पसनोषधीभिर्हरितवर्णां

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra upheld the heaven that is हरिधायसं (*haridhāyasaṃ*): the green-hued supporters (dhāyasaḥ) of which are रत्नयो (*ratnayo*)**NOTE**. Likewise he upheld the earth, हरिवर्पसं (*harivarpasaṃ*), green with plants.

### Printed p. 67

> पृथिवीमधारयत् । हरितोर्हरिद्दर्णयोर्ययोर्द्यावापृथिव्योर्मध्यॆ स्वकीययोरश्वयोर्भूरि प्रभूतं भोजनं । भुज्यत इति भोजनं यवसादि । लभ्यते ।
> ययोश्च व्यावापृथिव्योरः तर्मध्यॆ हरिरिंद्रश्चरत् । चरति । ते द्यावापृथिव्यानिंद्रोऽधारयदित्यर्थ: ॥ हरिधायसं । डुधाण् धारणपोषणयोरित्यस्माद्वहिहाथाञ्छंदसीत्यसुन् । णुदित्यनुवृत्तेरातो युक् । बहुव्रीहौ पूर्वपदस्वरः ।
> पृथिवीं । प्रथेः शिवन् संप्रसारणं चेति पिवन्त्ययः । षिद्गौरादिभ्यश्चति जीष् ।
> प्रत्ययस्वरेणांतोदात्तः । हरिवर्पसं । वृज् संभक्तौ । वृजशीङ्ब्यां रूपस्वांगयोरित्यसुन्त्ययः। पुगागमश्च । संभज्यते तदिति वर्पो रूपं। बहुव्रीहौ पूर्वपदस्वरः । हरितोः । हृञ् हरण इत्यस्मात् हृस्यरुहि युषिभ्य इतिः । उ. १-९९ ।
> इतीतिप्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । भोजनं । भुज पालनाभ्यवहारयोरित्यस्मात्कृत्यल्युटो बहुलमिति कर्मणि ल्युट् । लित्स्वरः । अंतः । स्वरादिष्टंतोदात्तत्वेन पठितत्पादंतोदात्त, । चरत् । चरतिर्गत्यर्थः । लेटि रूपं । यद्वृत्तयोगादनिघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Between the two, heaven and earth, of green hue, his own two horses have abundant भोजनं (*bhojanaṃ*) (food), such as fodder, which is obtained; and between those same heaven and earth Hari, Indra, moves. The meaning is that Indra upheld those two, heaven and earth.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ययोः (*yayoḥ*)—of which heaven and earth
- अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*)—throughout the middle
- हरिः (*hariḥ*)—Indra
- हरितोः (*haritoḥ*)—to his horses of green-tawny hue
- भूरि (*bhūri*)—abundant
- भोजनं (*bhojanaṃ*)—food such as fodder
- (he has) made; and ययोः अंतः (*yayoḥ aṃtaḥ*)—throughout the middle of which heaven and earth
- चरत् (*carat*)—he travels, such
- हरिधायसं (*haridhāyasaṃ*)—endowed with golden rays
- द्यां (*dyāṃ*)—the world of heaven
- हरिवर्पसं (*harivarpasaṃ*)—adorned with crops and emeralds
- पृथिवीं (*pṛthivīṃ*)—the earth
- आधारयत् (*ādhārayat*)—has lifted and held up (established)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

He has lifted and held up the heaven endowed with golden rays and the earth adorned with crops and emeralds: the heaven and earth between which Indra has abundantly provided fodder and other food for his horses of green-tawny hue, and throughout the middle of which he travels. (He has created them.)

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra has upheld the yellow-rayed heaven , the verdant tinted earth ; there is abundant pasture (for his steeds) in the two azure spheres of heaven and earth, between which Hari travels.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The greatness of Indra is described by saying that he is the cause of the mutual growth of prosperity of heaven and earth. The description हरिधायसं द्यां हरिवर्पसं पृथिवीं (*haridhāyasaṃ dyāṃ harivarpasaṃ pṛthivīṃ*) is poetic

### Printed p. 68
and describes a principle of beauty; it is not merely a description of material wealth. The sky with its golden rays and the earth full of crops and emeralds, describing the beauty of the universe, make known the greatness of Indra, the primary cause of that beauty. Indra is the cause of the origin, the maintenance and the growth of heaven and earth.

Although हरिधायसं (*haridhāyasaṃ*) means "endowed with golden rays", wherever the words धायसे (*dhāyase*) and धायसा (*dhāyasā*) occur (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-95-1) the explanation is "supporting". The rays of the Sun support the universe. So the purport is "the rays of the Sun, which support the universe", and the meaning is the world of heaven endowed with such rays. Here the word हरि (*hari*) means golden. In the epithet हरिवर्पसं (*harivarpasaṃ*) the word हरि (*hari*) means emerald, and वर्पस् (*varpas*) means a radiant form. The earth has the epithet हरिवर्पसं (*harivarpasaṃ*) because it is endowed with world-attracting crops and emeralds. Indra has established heaven and earth, which are such nourishers of the world and endowed with world-attracting radiance.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ज॒ज्ञा॒नो हरि॑तो॒ वृषा॒ विश्व॒मा भा॑ति रोच॒नं ।  
> हर्य॑श्वो॒ हरि॑तं धत्त॒ आयु॑ध॒मा वज्रं॑ बा॒ह्वोर्हरिं॑॥ ४ ॥

> *jajñāno harito vṛṣā viśvamā bhāti rocanaṃ |*  
> *haryaśvo haritaṃ dhatta āyudhamā vajraṃ bāhvorhariṃ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> जज्ञानः । हरितः । वृषा । विश्वं । आ । भाति । रोचनं ।  
> हरिऽश्वः । हरितं । धत्ते । आयुधं । आ । वज्रं । बाह्वोः । हरिं ॥ ४ ॥

> *jajñānaḥ | haritaḥ | vṛṣā | viśvaṃ | ā | bhāti | rocanaṃ |*  
> *hari 'śvaḥ | haritaṃ | dhatte | āyudhaṃ | ā | vajraṃ | bāhvoḥ | hariṃ ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वृषा कामानां वर्षको हरितो हरिद्वर्णोपेतो जज्ञानो जात इंद्रो
> विश्वं सर्वं रोचनं दीप्यमानं लोकमा भाति । सर्वतः प्रकाशयति । तथा हर्यश्व
> इंद्रो हरितं हरिद्वर्णोपेतमायुधं धत्ते । बाह्वोर्धत्ते । तथा हरिं शत्रूणां
> प्राणहारकं वज्रं बाह्वोराधत्ते ॥ जज्ञानः । जनी प्रादुर्भाव इत्यस्य लिटः कानच् ।
> गमहनेत्यादिनोपधालोपः । चित्वादंतोदात्तः । भाति । भा दीप्ता वित्यस्यांतर्भावितण्यर्थस्य लटि रूपं । रोचनं । रुच दीप्तावित्यस्मादनुदात्तेतश्च हलादेरिति
> युच् । चित्वादंतोदात्तः । बाह्वोः । उदात्तयणो हल्पूर्नादिति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, the showerer of desires, of green-tawny hue, being born, illumines on every side the whole shining world. Likewise Indra, lord of tawny horses, holds in his arms the weapon of tawny hue; and he holds in his arms the thunderbolt, हरिं (*hariṃ*), the taker of the lives of enemies.

### Printed p. 69

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—showerer of desires
- हरितः (*haritaḥ*)—of golden form; Indra
- बज्ञानः (*bajñānaḥ*)—as soon as born
- विश्वं (*viśvaṃ*)—the entire
- रोचनं (*rocanaṃ*)—attractive world
- अ भाति (*a bhāti*)—he causes to shine
- हरश्वः (*haraśvaḥ*)—Indra, possessing horses named Hari
- हरितं (*haritaṃ*)—golden
- आयुधं (*āyudhaṃ*)—weapon
- धत्ते (*dhatte*)—holds
- हरिं (*hariṃ*)—that takes away (the lives of enemies)
- वज्रं (*vajraṃ*)—the thunderbolt weapon
- बाह्वोः (*bāhvoḥ*)—in his arms
- (अधत्ते) (*(adhatte)*)—holds

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, the showerer of desires, of golden form, causes the entire attractive world to shine as soon as he is born. Indra, possessor of the horses named Hari, holds a golden weapon. He holds in his arms the thunderbolt that takes away the lives of enemies.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
The azure-coloured showerer (of benefits), illumes, as soon as born, the whole luminous region ; the lord of the tawny steeds holds in his hands the yellow weapon, the destroying thunderbolt.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

When the greatness of Indra, the cause of the world's prosperity, is described, descriptions such as आयुधं धत्ते (*āyudhaṃ dhatte*) come along with it. Indra is a protector, and likewise a destroyer (of enemies). In the description that he makes the world attractive and radiant (विश्वं रोचनं आ भाति (*viśvaṃ rocanaṃ ā bhāti*)) and also lifts and holds a destructive weapon, the two forms of Indra are understood. The descriptions of Indra's thunderbolt, Viṣṇu's discus and Īśvara's trident are all of this kind. They are a harmony of protection and chastisement for the world, a harmony of truth and beauty. In their description a hidden spiritual principle is indicated. This principle of harmony will be discussed later, when the great nature of Indra's thunderbolt is explained.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्रो॑ ह॒र्यंत॒मर्जु॑नं॒ वज्रं॑ शु॒क्रैर॒भीवृ॑तं ।  
> आपा॑वृणो॒द्धरि॑भि॒रद्रि॑भिः सु॒तमुद्गा हरि॑भिराजत ॥ ५ ॥

> *iṃdro haryaṃtamarjunaṃ vajraṃ śukrairabhīvṛtaṃ |*  
> *āpāvṛṇoddharibhiradribhiḥ sutamudgā haribhirājata ‖ 5 ‖*

### Printed p. 70
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रः । हर्यंतं । अर्जुनं । वज्रं । शुक्रैः । अभिवृतं ।  
> अप । अवृणोत् । हरिभिः । अद्रिभिः । सुतं । उत् । गाः । हरिभिः । आजत ॥ ५ ॥

> *iṃdraḥ | haryaṃtaṃ | arjunaṃ | vajraṃ | śukraiḥ | abhivṛtaṃ |*  
> *apa | avṛṇot | haribhiḥ | adribhiḥ | sutaṃ | ut | gāḥ | haribhiḥ | ājata ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सोऽयमिंद्रो हर्यंतं कमनीयमर्जुनं शुभ्रं । शुभ्रत्वमुपपादयति । शुक्रैः शुभैः क्षीरादिभिरभिवृतं व्याप्तं वज्रं वेगवंतं हरिभिः । हरंत्येभिः
> सोमरसानिति हरयः। तैरद्रिभिग्रावभिः सुतमभिषुतं सोममपावृणोत् । आवरणरहितमकरोत् । तथा च मंत्रवर्णः । पूषा राजानमाघृणिरपगूळ्हं गुहा हितं
> अविंदत् । ऋग्वे. १-२३-१४ । इति । तथा गाः पणिभिरपहृता गा हरिभिरश्वैरुपेतः स
> इंद्र उदाजत । विलान्नि रगमयत् ॥ अर्जुनं । अर्ज षर्ज आर्जन इत्यस्मादर्जॆर्णिलुक्चेत्युनन् । नित्स्वरः । अभिवृतं । गतिरनंतर इति गतेः प्रकृतिस्वरत्वं । आजत । अज
> गतिक्षेपणयोः । लजि व्यत्ययेनात्मनेपदं । निघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra has uncovered the desirable (हर्यंतं (*haryaṃtaṃ*)) bright (अर्जुनं (*arjunaṃ*)) Soma. Sāyaṇa justifies the brightness: it is overlaid (अभिवृतं (*abhivṛtaṃ*)) with shining milk and the like; the vajra is swift. The हरिभिः (*haribhiḥ*) are the stones by which the Soma juices are carried off (हरंत्येभिः (*haraṃtyebhiḥ*)); with these pressing stones he made the pressed Soma free of covering. The mantra text is cited: "Pūṣan found the king, hidden, placed in the cave" (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-23-14). Likewise Indra, accompanied by his tawny horses, drove out the cows carried off by the Paṇis, making them come out of the cave. The grammatical remainder is omitted.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- हर्यंतं (*haryaṃtaṃ*)—attractive
- अर्जुनं (*arjunaṃ*)—bright
- शुक्रैः (*śukraiḥ*)—with pure milk and the like
- अभिवृतं (*abhivṛtaṃ*)—overlaid
- वज्रं (*vajraṃ*)—swift-flowing
- हरिभिः (*haribhiḥ*)—pressing out the juice
- अद्रिभिः (*adribhiḥ*)—with stones
- सुतं (*sutaṃ*)—crushed and pressed, the Soma juice
- अपावृणोत् (*apāvṛṇot*)—released from concealment and made to appear shining
- गाः (*gāḥ*)—the cows (carried off by the Paṇis)
- हरिभिः (*haribhiḥ*)—Indra, furnished with horses
- उत् अजत (*ut ajata*)—made them come out of the cave

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra uncovered the Soma juice, which is attractive, bright, overlaid with pure milk and the like, swift-flowing, and crushed and pressed by the pressing stones, releasing it from concealment and making it appear shining. Indra, furnished with horses, made the cows carried off by the Paṇis come out of the cave.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra has uncovered the desirable white-coloured, fast-flowing Soma, effused by the expressing stones, and overlaid with the shining (milk and other liquids), in like manner as when, borne by his tawny steeds, he rescued the cattle
### Printed p. 71

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this mantra the words arjuna, vajra and the rest are all epithets of the Soma juice. Arjuna is taken to mean bright; it is formed from the root raṃj, "to shine". In the Nirukta the word arjuna is read among the names of dawn (Ni. 1-8) and among the names of form (Ni. 3-7). The Nirukta author quotes
अहश्च कृष्णमहरर्जुनं च वि वर्तेते रजसी वेद्याभिः (*ahaśca kṛṣṇamahararjunaṃ ca vi vartete rajasī vedyābhiḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-9-1)
and explains kṛṣṇa as night and arjuna as the day of white colour. Also in भद्रा वस्त्राण्यर्जुना वसाना (*bhadrā vastrāṇyarjunā vasānā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-9-2) it indicates the bright colour of cloth. Thus the word serves not only where a merely physical matter is expressed but also where a spiritual principle is expressed. For example, the two names Kṛṣṇa and Arjuna may be said to reflect two principles of the Lord, the hidden and the manifest. All principles perceptible to the outer eye are indicated by the word arjuna; and Ṛgvedic passages describing the hidden principle, such as महत्तन्नाम गुह्यं (*mahattannāma guhyaṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-55-1) and एकस्यदृशे न रूपं (*ekasyadṛśe na rūpaṃ*) (1-164-44), suggest the meaning of the Bhagavadgītā phrase गुह्याद्गुह्यतरं (*guhyādguhyataraṃ*). So the uses of kṛṣṇa and arjuna in the senses of hidden (secret) and perceptible (bright) convey a spiritual principle.
Vajra: explained as विवर्जयति प्राणान् (*vivarjayati prāṇān*), the thunderbolt having a destructive power. Its particulars were given earlier. In the present Ṛk it is used in the sense "swift-going".
Adribhiḥ: आद्रिरादृणात्येतेन । अपि वा अत्तेः सात् (*ādrirādṛṇātyetena | api vā atteḥ sāt*). The word adri is formed either from the root dṛ meaning "to split" or from the root ad meaning "to eat". It is used in many places as a synonym both for the stones that press Soma and for Indra's vajra. Its particulars are given in the Special Topics of Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-149-1, in the 11th volume of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā.
