## Sūkta 45 (Maṇḍala 3)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आ मंद्रॆरिति पंचर्चं सप्तमं सूक्तं वैश्वामित्रं बार्हतमैंद्रं ; आ
> मंद्रॆरित्यनुक्रमणिका । पूर्वसूक्ते बार्हतं त्वित्युक्तत्वादस्यापि बार्हतत्वं ॥
> विशेषविनियोगस्तु यत्र श्रुत्या न सूत्रितः । स्मार्तं तत्र विजानीयादृग्विधानादि
> शास्त्रतः ॥
> अनुवादवु—आ मंद्रॆः ऎंब ई सूक्तवु नाल्कनॆय अनुवाकदल्लि एळनॆय सूक्तवु.
> इदरल्लि ऐदु ऋक्कुगळिरुववु. ई सूक्तक्कॆ विश्वामित्रनु ऋषियु, इंद्रनु देवतॆयु, बृहती
> छंदस्सु. अनुक्रमणिकॆयल्लि आ मंद्रॆः ऎंदु मॊदलागि हेळिरुवुदु. हिंदिन सूक्तदल्लि
> हेळिरुवंतॆ बृहतीछंदस्सिन शस्त्रमंत्रगळिगागि ई सूक्तद विनियोगविरुवुदु. याव सूक्त
> अथवा मंत्रगळिगॆ शौतसूत्रादिगळल्लि विनियोगवु हेळल्पट्टिरुवुदिल्लवो अवुगळिगॆ स्मार्तप्रयोग
> गदल्लियू, शौनकोक्तवाद ऋग्विधानवॆंब ग्रंथदल्लि हेळिरुवंतॆयू, इतर शास्त्रगळल्लि हेळिरु
> वंतॆयू विनियोगविरुवुदॆंदु भाविसबेकु.
> ॥ ओं ॥
> सूक्त—४५
> मंडल—३ ॥ अनुवाक—४ ॥ सूक्त—४५ ॥
> अष्टक—३ ॥ अध्याय—३ ॥ वर्ग—९ ॥
> ॥ सूक्तदल्लिरुव ऋक्संख्यॆ—५ ॥
> । ऋषिः— विश्वामित्र ।
> । देवता— इंद्र ।
> । छंदः— बृहती ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta आ मंद्रैः (*ā maṃdraiḥ*) has five Ṛks and is the seventh sūkta; seer Viśvāmitra, deity Indra; the Anukramaṇikā begins "ā mandraiḥ". Since the previous sūkta was said to be Bārhata, this one too is Bārhata. Where no specific use is given by Śruti-sūtra, one should know it from the Smārta rule, the Ṛgvidhāna and other śāstras.

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

This sūkta, आ मंद्रैः (*ā maṃdraiḥ*), is the seventh in the fourth anuvāka. It has five Ṛks. Viśvāmitra is the seer, Indra the deity, Bṛhatī the metre. Its use is for the śastra mantras of Bṛhatī metre, as stated for the previous sūkta. For any sūkta or mantras whose use is not stated in the Śrauta-sūtras and the like, the use is to be understood as in the Smārta practice, in the Ṛgvidhāna attributed to Śaunaka, and in other śāstras.

##### Particulars

Maṇḍala 3; Anuvāka 4; Sūkta 45; Aṣṭaka 3; Adhyāya 3; Varga 9. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5. Seer: Viśvāmitra. Deity: Indra. Metre: Bṛhatī.
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ मं॒द्रैरिं॑द्र॒ हरि॑भिर्या॒हि म॒यूर॑रोमभिः ।  
> मा त्वा॒ के चि॒न्नि य॑म॒न्विं न पा॒शिनोऽति॒ धन्वे॑व॒ ता इ॑हि ॥ १ ॥

> *ā maṃdrairiṃdra haribhiryāhi mayūraromabhiḥ |*  
> *mā tvā ke cinni yamanviṃ na pāśino 'ti dhanveva tā ihi ‖ 1 ‖*

### Printed p. 73

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । मंद्रैः । इंद्र । हरिभिः । याहि । मयूररोमभिः ।  
> मा । त्वा । के । चित् । नि । यमन् । विं । न । पाशिनः । अति । धन्वइव । तान् । इहि॥१॥

> *ā | maṃdraiḥ | iṃdra | haribhiḥ | yāhi | mayūraromabhiḥ |*  
> *mā | tvā | ke | cit | ni | yaman | viṃ | na | pāśinaḥ | ati | dhanvaiva | tān | ihi‖1‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र मंद्रैर्मादयितृभिर्मयूररोमभिर्मयूररोमसदृशरोमयुक्तैर्हरिभिरश्चैरुपेतस्त्वमा याहि । यज्ञं प्रत्यागच्छ । केचिदपि जनास्त्वा त्वां
> मा नि यमन् । मा नियच्छंतु । गमनप्रतिबंधं मा कुर्वंत्वित्यभिप्रायः । प्रतिबंधे दृष्टांतः । विं न । यथा पाशिनः पाशहस्ता व्याधा विं गच्छंतं दक्षिणं नियच्छंति तद्वन्मा नियच्छंतु । किंच धन्वेव । यथा पांथा धन्व मरुदेशं शीघ्रमतिगच्छंति । तद्वद्दमनप्रतिबंधकानतीत्य शीघ्रमिहि । आगच्छ ॥ मंद्रॆः । मदि स्तुत्यादिश्वित्यस्य स्थायितंचीत्यादिना रक्षृत्ययः । इदित्तान्नुम् । प्रत्ययस्वरः । मयूररोमभिः । मयूररोमवद्रोमाणि येषामिति सप्तम्युपमानपूर्वपदस्य बहुव्रीहिरुत्तरपदलोपश्च । पा. २-२-२४-१२ । इति समास उत्तरपदलोपश्च । मयूरशब्दो मीनातेरूरन् । उ. १-६८ । इत्यूरन्प्रत्ययांतः । मीनाति सर्पानिति मयूरो बर्हि । नित्वादाद्युदात्तत्वे प्राप्ते मादीनां । फि. ३-४ । इति सूत्रेण मध्योदात्तः । बहुव्रीहौ पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरः । यमन् यम उपरम इत्यस्य लेट्यडागमे रूपं । इहि । इण् गतावित्यस्य लोटि रूपं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, come to the sacrifice with your delighting horses, whose hair is like peacock hair. Let no persons restrain you." The example of restraint: as fowlers with snares in hand restrain a bird flying straight, let them not restrain you. Further, धन्वेव (*dhanveva*): as travellers quickly cross a desert, so pass beyond those who would hold you back and come quickly. Mayūra is derived from मीनाति सर्पानिति (*mīnāti sarpāniti*) ("he injures snakes"), the peacock. The grammatical remainder is omitted.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- मंद्रैः (*maṃdraiḥ*)—radiant
- मयूररोमभिः (*mayūraromabhiḥ*)—having hair attractive like peacock feathers
- हरिभिः (*haribhiḥ*)—you, joined with the horses
- आ याहि (*ā yāhi*)—come to the sacrificial place
- पाशिनः (*pāśinaḥ*)—fowlers holding a net
- विं न (*viṃ na*)—as they stop a bird
- के चित् (*ke cit*)—let no one (on the way)
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- मा नि यमन् (*mā ni yaman*)—stop
- धन्वेव (*dhanveva*)—as travellers walk quickly to cross the obstacle of a desert
- तान् (*tān*)—(you also) the obstacles (on the way)
- अति (*ati*)—having crossed
- इहि (*ihi*)—come quickly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, come to our sacrificial place with horses that are radiant and have hair attractive like peacock feathers. As fowlers holding a net stop a bird
### Printed p. 74
so let no one on the way stop you. As travellers walk quickly and cross a desert, so do you also cross the obstacles that may arise on the way and come quickly.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Come, Indra, with your exulting peacock-haired steeds; let no persons detain you as (fowlers) throwing snares catch a bird : pass them by (quickly) as (travellers cross) a desert.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In describing Indra's unobstructed power, his swiftness is compared to the unobstructed flight of a bird. There is another comparison, धन्वेव (*dhanveva*). The word dhanva is read in the Nirukta among the names of the atmosphere (Ni. 1-2) and among the names of motion (Ni. 4-2). Here it means motion; Indra's speed is compared to the swiftness of travellers in a desert.
In places such as धन्वचरो न वंसगस्तृषाणश्चकमानः (*dhanvacaro na vaṃsagastṛṣāṇaścakamānaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-36-1), dhanvacara is explained as a man who moves swiftly in a desert. Sāyaṇa sometimes explains it as a man who moves carrying a bow. Here the speed of motion and unobstructed power are indicated.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वृ॒त्र॒खा॒दो व॑लंरु॒जः पु॒रां द॒र्मो अ॒पाम॒जः ।  
> स्थात्ता॒ रथ॑स्य॒ हर्यो॑रभिस्व॒र इंद्रो॑ दृ॒ळ्हा चि॑दारु॒जः ॥ २ ॥

> *vṛtrakhādo valaṃrujaḥ purāṃ darmo apāmajaḥ |*  
> *sthāttā rathasya haryorabhisvara iṃdro dṛḷhā cidārujaḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वृत्रखादः । वलंरुज: । पुरां । दर्मः । अपां । अज: ।  
> स्थाता । रथस्य । हर्योः । अभिस्वरे । इंद्रः । दृळ्हा । चित् । आरुज: ॥ २ ॥

> *vṛtrakhādaḥ | valaṃruja: | purāṃ | darmaḥ | apāṃ | aja: |*  
> *sthātā | rathasya | haryoḥ | abhisvare | iṃdraḥ | dṛḷhā | cit | āruja: ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सोऽयमिंद्रो वृत्रखादः । वृत्रं खादति हिनस्तीति वृत्रखादः । वलंरुज: ।
> वृणोश्याकाशमिति वलो मेघः । तस्य भंजकः । तथा च मंत्रवर्णः । बिभेद वलं ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra is vṛtrakhāda: he devours (injures) Vṛtra. Valaṃruja: vala is the cloud, since it covers the sky (the verb is vṛṇoti); he is its breaker. So the mantra text: "he split Vala"
### Printed p. 75

> ऋग्वे ३-३४-१० । इति । ततोऽपामजो मेघभेदनद्वारापां प्रेरकः पुरां शत्रुसंबंधिनां पुराणां दर्मो दारकः । तथा हर्योरश्वयोरभिस्वरेऽस्मदाभिमुख्येन
> प्रेरणे निमित्तभूते सति रथस्य स्थाता रथमधिष्ठाता । तथा दृळ्हा चित् दृढानामतिबलवतां शत्रूणामस्यारुजः समंताद्वंजको भवति ॥ वृत्रखादः । खादृ भक्षण इत्यस्मात्मर्मण्यण् । कृदुत्तरपदस्वरः । वलंरुजः । रुजो भंगे। कर्मण्युपपदे मूलविभुजादित्वात्कृत्ययः । तत्पुरुषे कृति बहुलमिति द्वितीयाया आलुक् । थाधादिस्वरः।
> आरुजः । रुजो भंग इत्यस्मादिगुपधलक्षणः कः । समासे थाधादिस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-34-10). Hence he is apāmaja, the impeller of the waters by splitting the cloud; darma, the tearer of the cities of the enemy. When his two horses are urged towards us (abhisvara), he is the stander on the chariot, its mounter. And he is the breaker on all sides of firm, very mighty enemies. The grammatical remainder is omitted.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृत्रखादः (*vṛtrakhādaḥ*)—slayer of Vṛtra
- वलंरुजः (*valaṃrujaḥ*)—splitter of the cloud
- अपामजः (*apāmajaḥ*)—sender down of water in the form of rain
- पुरां (*purāṃ*)—of enemy cities
- दर्मः (*darmaḥ*)—destroyer
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- हर्योः (*haryoḥ*)—his horses
- अभिस्वरे (*abhisvare*)—to urge to run towards us
- रथस्य (*rathasya*)—in his chariot
- स्थाता (*sthātā*)—sitting
- दृळ्हा चित् (*dṛḷhā cit*)—even the exceedingly mighty enemies
- आरुजः (*ārujaḥ*)—has the power to destroy

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, the slayer of Vṛtra, splitter of the cloud, sender down of water in the form of rain, destroyer of enemy cities, sits in his chariot to urge his horses to run towards us, and has the power to destroy even the exceedingly mighty enemies.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
The devourer of Vritra, the fracturer of the cloud, the sender of the waters, the demolisher of cities. Indra, the destroyer of powerful enemies, has mounted his chariot to urge his horses to our presence.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The matters of Vala and Vṛtra were explained in detail earlier. Some hold that Vala and Vṛtra were brothers; they may also be understood as persons reflecting darkness. In the present Ṛk too, from वृणोतीति वलः (*vṛṇotīti valaḥ*) ("it covers the atmosphere"), the word vala is a synonym for cloud.
Abhisvare: the word abhisvara is used in different senses. Its particular meaning was given earlier in the Special Topics of Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-164-21. "अभिस्वर्तारो अर्कं न (*abhisvartāro arkaṃ na*)

### Printed p. 76

The word अभिस्वर (*abhisvara*) is a synonym for the worshippers in passages such as सुष्टुभः (*suṣṭubhaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-78-4): those who all around (अभितः (*abhitaḥ*)) make the sound of praise in order to impel the gods. In the present context, as it is explained in passages such as चतुष्पादेति द्विपदामभिस्वरे संपश्यन् पंक्तिरुपतिष्ठमानः (*catuṣpādeti dvipadāmabhisvare saṃpaśyan paṃktirupatiṣṭhamānaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-117-8), the word अभिस्वर (*abhisvara*) can be taken to mean "approach"; the purport is "go towards". The purport is: to impel the horses to run towards us. The technical usages such as अभिस्वरंति (*abhisvaraṃti*) were also explained earlier.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> गं॒भी॒रा उ॑द॒धीरि॑व॒ क्रतुं॑ पुष्यसि॒ गा इ॑व ।  
> प्र सु॑गो॒पा यव॑सं धे॒नवो॑ यथा प्र॒दं कु॒ल्या इ॑वाशत ॥

> *gaṃbhīrā udadhīriva kratuṃ puṣyasi gā iva |*  
> *pra sugopā yavasaṃ dhenavo yathā pradaṃ kulyā ivāśata ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> गंभीरान् । उदधीन् इव । क्रतुं । पुष्यसि । गाः इव ।  
> प्र । सुगोपाः । यवसं । धेनवः । यथा । ह्रदं । कुल्याः इव । आशश ॥ ३ ॥

> *gaṃbhīrān | udadhīn iva | kratuṃ | puṣyasi | gāḥ iva |*  
> *pra | sugopāḥ | yavasaṃ | dhenavaḥ | yathā | hradaṃ | kulyāḥ iva | āśaśa ‖ 3 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वं गंभीरान्महावकाशानुदधीन् समुद्रानुदकैर्यथा पोषयसि
> तद्वत्कृतुं यज्ञस्य कर्तारमिमं यजमानमभिपुतफलप्रदानेन पुष्यसि । पोषयसि । अत्र दृष्टांतः । यथा सुगोपाः सविचीनो गोपालो यवसेन गा:
> पोषयति तद्वत्।यथा धेनवो यवसं तृणादिकं प्राप्नुवंति तद्वत्त्वं सोमान्
> प्राप्नोषि । ते च सोमाः कुल्या इव कृत्रिमसरितो ह्रदं महाजलाशयं यथा
> प्राप्नुवंति तद्वत्वामाशत । व्याप्नुवंति ॥ गंभीरान् । गाध्य प्रतिष्ठालिप्सयोग्रंथे
> चेत्यस्माद्गंभीरगभीरावित्यनेन सूत्रेण इरच् प्रत्ययः । नुमागमोपधाह्रस्वभकाराः
> सर्वे निपात्यंते । चित्तादंतोदात्तः । उदधीनिव । उदकशब्द उपपदे दधातेरधिकरणेऽर्थे कर्मण्यधिकरणे चेति किप्रत्ययः । उदकशब्दस्य पेषंवासवाहनधिषु च ।
> पा. ६-३-५८ । इत्युदभावः । कृत्स्वरः । इवेन विभक्त्यलोपः । पुष्यसि । पुष पुष्टावित्यस्यांतर्णितण्यर्थस्य लटि रूपं । निघातः । सुगोपाः । गोपायतेः क्विपि रूपं ।
> यथा । यथेति पादांते : फि ४-१७ । इति निघातः । आतत । अशू व्याप्तानित्यस्माल्लजि
> बहुलं छंदसीति विकरणस्य लुक् । निघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, as you nourish with waters the deep oceans of great extent, so you nourish this sacrificer, the doer (क्रतुं (*kratuṃ*)) of the sacrifice, by granting the fruit he desires. An illustration is given here: as a good herdsman, one who watches well, nourishes the cows with fodder; as cows obtain fodder, grass and the like, so you obtain the Soma-juices. And those Soma-juices reach you, as artificial channels reach a lake, a great reservoir of water. They pervade you."

### Printed p. 77

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- गंभीरान् (*gaṃbhīrān*)—very deep
- उदधीन् इव (*udadhīn iva*)—(as you fill) the oceans (with waters)
- सुगोपाः (*sugopāḥ*)—an excellent herdsman
- गाः इव (*gāḥ iva*)—as (he) nourishes the cows
- क्रतुं (*kratuṃ*)—the sacrificer
- पुष्यसि (*puṣyasi*)—you nourish (with the granting of what he desires)
- यथा (*yathā*)—in the way in which
- धेनवः (*dhenavaḥ*)—cows
- यवसं (*yavasaṃ*)—obtain fodder and the like (so you too obtain the Soma)
- कुल्याः इव (*kulyāḥ iva*)—as small rivers
- ह्रदं (*hradaṃ*)—reach a lake
- प्र आशत (*pra āśata*)—(those Soma-juices) reach your belly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, as you fill the very deep oceans with waters, and as an excellent herdsman nourishes the cows, so you nourish the sacrificer with the granting of what he desires. As cows abundantly obtain fodder and the like, so you too obtain the Soma. As small rivers reach a lake, so those streams of Soma-juice reach your belly.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
You cherish the celebrator of the pious rite as (you fill) the deep seas (with water) ; or as a careful herdsman (cherishes) the cows ; (you imbibe the Soma) as cows (obtain) fodder, (and the juices flow into you) as rivulets flow into a lake.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Here the nature of the wealth that Indra provides for a sacrificer who performs good rites is described. Two aspects of this bestowal of wealth are given by way of comparison. This wealth is deep like the ocean. And the regard that Indra shows towards the sacrificer in this bestowal: both of these are possible only for an excellent sacrificer. In this Ṛk the word क्रतु (*kratu*) is a synonym for the sacrificer.

In the Ṛk-saṃhitā the word क्रतु (*kratu*) for the most part denotes the rite (karma); in some places it also denotes the aim of the rite. The deeper sense of this word has been explained in detail in the 13th part of the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā. Here, since it is a synonym for the sacrificer, it is used in a special sense. This word denotes both the sacrificer's aim and his power of action.

In the Saṃhitā and also in the Upaniṣads the two words क्रतु (*kratu*) and दक्ष (*dakṣa*) denote the person's power of will and power of action; or they may denote both the inner power and the outward action.

### Printed p. 78

The Ṛk-saṃhitā explains in detail that if a person's wish or resolve is pure, then his practical capacity to obtain the human aim also grows.
इंद्रः सुतेषु सोमेषु क्रतुं पुनीत उक्थं (*iṃdraḥ suteṣu someṣu kratuṃ punīta ukthaṃ*)
विदे वृधस्य दक्षसो महान्हि षः (*vide vṛdhasya dakṣaso mahānhi ṣaḥ*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-13-1)
Here, in वृधस्य दक्षसः विदे (*vṛdhasya dakṣasaḥ vide*), it is stated that in order to acquire strength that makes one grow, Indra purifies the sacrificer (his resolve), as in क्रतुं पुनीते (*kratuṃ punīte*). Even when the person's spiritual aim is explained, it is set out in sentences such as ओं क्रतो स्मर (*oṃ krato smara*) (Īśa. 17) **NOTE** and क्रतुमयःपुरुषः (*kratumayaḥpuruṣaḥ*) (Chā. U. 3-14-2). In such contexts Śaṅkarācārya has explained the word क्रतु (*kratu*) as resolution, or as the person's duty; the purport is resolve, and the power of action that accomplishes the resolve. According to this resolve and power the aim is accomplished. The nature of क्रतु (*kratu*) is stated thus: यथाक्रतुरस्मिल्लोके पुरुषो भवति तथेतः प्रेत्य भवति स क्रतुं कुर्वीत (*yathākraturasmilloke puruṣo bhavati tathetaḥ pretya bhavati sa kratuṃ kurvīta*) and so on. In the present Ṛk too the nature of the wealth that comes to such a person made of क्रतु (*kratu*) is described.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ न॒स्तुजं॑ र॒यिं भ॒रांशं॒ न प्र॑तिजान॒ते ।  
> वृ॒क्षं प॒क्वं फल॑मं॒कीव॑ धूनु॒हींद्र॑ सं॒पार॑णं॒ वसु॑ ॥ ४ ॥

> *ā nastujaṃ rayiṃ bharāṃśaṃ na pratijānate |*  
> *vṛkṣaṃ pakvaṃ phalamaṃkīva dhūnuhīṃdra saṃpāraṇaṃ vasu ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । नः । तुजं । रयिं । भर । अंशं । न । प्रतिजानते ।  
> वृक्षं । पक्वं । फलं । अंकीऽइव । धूनुहि । इंद्र । संपारणं । वसु ॥ ४ ॥

> *ā | naḥ | tujaṃ | rayiṃ | bhara | aṃśaṃ | na | pratijānate |*  
> *vṛkṣaṃ | pakvaṃ | phalaṃ | aṃkī 'iva | dhūnuhi | iṃdra | saṃpāraṇaṃ | vasu ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> -हे इंद्र नोऽस्माकं तुजं शत्रूणां बाधकं रयिं धनेनोपलक्षितं पुत्रमा भर । संपादय । तत्र दृष्टांतः । अंशं न ! यथा पिता प्रतिजानते व्यवहारज्ञाय पुत्राय स्वकीयस्य धनस्य भागं ददाति तद्वत् । किंच पक्वं फलं प्रति वृक्षमंकीवांकुशी यथा धूनोति तथा त्वं संपारणमस्मदच्छायाः सम्यक् पूरकं
> वसु धनं धूनुहि । चालय । प्रयच्छेत्यर्थः " प्रतिजानते । ज्ञा अवबोधन इत्यस्य

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, bring to us तुजं (*tujaṃ*), the harasser of enemies, रयिं (*rayiṃ*), that is, a son marked by wealth; provide him. An illustration: अंशं न (*aṃśaṃ na*), as a father gives a share of his own wealth to a son who knows how to manage affairs (प्रतिजानते (*pratijānate*)). Further: as a man with a hook (अंकी (*aṃkī*)) shakes a tree for the ripe fruit, so you shake down for us the wealth that is संपारणं (*saṃpāraṇaṃ*), that fully satisfies our wishes; that is, give it."

### Printed p. 79

> शतरि रूपं । प्रादिसमास- । शतुरनुम इति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं । धूनुहि । धूञ् कंपन
> इत्यस्य लोटि रूपं । संपारणं । प्प पालनपूरणयोरित्यस्य ण्यंतस्य करणे ल्युट् ।
> समासे कृत्स्वर ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This block contains only Sāyaṇa's grammatical notes on the words of the Ṛk.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- प्रतिजानते (*pratijānate*)—to a son who has acquired knowledge of practical affairs
- अंशं न (*aṃśaṃ na*)—as a father gives a share of wealth
- नः (*naḥ*)—to me
- तुजं (*tujaṃ*)—harmful to enemies
- रयिं (*rayiṃ*)—wealth in the form of a son
- आ भर (*ā bhara*)—provide
- सक्वं (*sakvaṃ*)—ripe
- फलं (*phalaṃ*)—for the fruit
- अंकी (*aṃkī*)—a hook
- वृक्षं इव (*vṛkṣaṃ iva*)—as it shakes the tree (and provides the fruit)
- संपारणं (*saṃpāraṇaṃ*)—that gives us satisfaction
- वसु (*vasu*)—wealth
- धनुहि (*dhanuhi*)—make it move (make it reach us)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, as a father gives a share of wealth to a son who has acquired knowledge of practical affairs, provide us with a son who harasses enemies. As a hook shakes the tree for the ripe fruit and provides the fruit, set in motion the wealth that gives us satisfaction and provide it to us.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Grant us riches, securing (us against foes), (as a father bestows) his portion on (a son) arrived at maturity; send down upon us, Indra, wealth adequate (to our desires), as a crook brings down the ripe fruit from a tree.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk too there is a description of the wealth that comes to a person who knows the duty of sacrifice. The word तुजं (*tujaṃ*) is used in the sense of harming. In the Nirukta this word is listed among the names of the thunderbolt. It is also used in the sense of giving: तुजतिः दानकर्मा (*tujatiḥ dānakarmā*). In Ṛks such as भूं चिद्दि तुजशो मर्त्यस्य (*bhūṃ ciddi tujaśo martyasya*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-39-8) the generosity of the patron is described as "giver of much". In the present Ṛk too, when it says तुजं रयिं आ भर (*tujaṃ rayiṃ ā bhara*), it may be explained either as "provide the wealth of a son who harasses enemies" or as "give a generous son".

संपारणं (*saṃpāraṇaṃ*) is formed from the root पृ पालनतरणयोः (*pṛ pālanataraṇayoḥ*), "to protect" or "to carry across". It may mean wealth that protects from poverty, or that carries one across poverty and brings one to the end. In sum the purport is: what gives satisfaction.

### Printed p. 80

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्व॒युरिं॑द्र स्व॒राळ॑सि॒ स्मद्दि॑ष्टिः॒ स्वय॑शस्तरः ।  
> स ना॑वृधा॒न ओज॑सा पुरुष्टुत॒ भवा॑ नः सु॒श्रव॑स्तमः ॥ ५ ॥

> *svayuriṃdra svarāḷasi smaddiṣṭiḥ svayaśastaraḥ |*  
> *sa nāvṛdhāna ojasā puruṣṭuta bhavā naḥ suśravastamaḥ ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्वयुः। इंद्र । स्वराट् । असि । स्मर्त्‌दिष्टिः । स्वयशःतरः !  
> सः । ववृधानः । ओजसा । पुरुस्तुत । भव । नः । सुश्रवःतमः ॥ ५ ॥

> *svayuḥ| iṃdra | svarāṭ | asi | smartdiṣṭiḥ | svayaśaḥtaraḥ !*  
> *saḥ | vavṛdhānaḥ | ojasā | purustuta | bhava | naḥ | suśravaḥtamaḥ ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र स्वयुर्धनवानसि । तथा स्वराट् स्वर्गस्य राजासि । स्मद्दिष्टिर्भद्रवाक्यः
> स्वयशस्तरोऽतिशयेन प्रभूतकीर्तिरसि । हे पुरुष्टुत बहुभिराहूतेंद्र स त्वमोजसा बलेन वावृधानः वर्धमानः सन् नोऽस्मदर्थं सुश्रवस्तमोऽतिशयेन शोभनान्नोपेतो भव ॥ स्वयुः स्वशब्दान्मत्त्वर्थियो युः। स्वराट् । राज्य दीप्ता । क्विप् ।
> व्रश्चादिना षत्वं । स्मद्दिष्टिः । स्सत् सुमदित्यर्थे । उकारलोपश्कांदसः । बहुव्रीहौ
> पूर्वपदस्वरः । पुरुष्टुत । संहितायां स्तुतस्तोमयोश्चंदसीति षत्वं । भव ।
> भवतेर्लोटि रूपं । द्व्यचोऽतस्तिज इति संहितायां दीर्घः । सुश्रवस्तमः । श्रवः
> शब्दोऽसुनंतः । बहुव्रीहावाद्युदात्तं द्व्यच्छंदसीत्युत्तरपदाद्युदात्तत्वं। तदंतादतिशायने तमप् । तस्य पित्तादनुदात्तत्वे बहुव्रीहिस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, you are स्वयुः (*svayuḥ*), possessed of wealth; likewise स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*), king of heaven. You are स्मद्दिष्टिः (*smaddiṣṭiḥ*), of auspicious speech, and स्वयशस्तरः (*svayaśastaraḥ*), of exceedingly great fame. O पुरुष्टुत (*puruṣṭuta*), Indra called upon by many, you, growing in strength (ओजसा (*ojasā*)), be for us सुश्रवस्तमः (*suśravastamaḥ*), most excellently endowed with good food." The rest of the block is grammatical.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- स्वयुः (*svayuḥ*)—(you) are possessed of wealth
- स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*)—lord of all heaven
- स्मद्दिष्टिः (*smaddiṣṭiḥ*)—of auspicious speech
- स्वयशस्तरः (*svayaśastaraḥ*)—exceedingly famous
- असि (*asi*)—you are
- पुरुष्टुत (*puruṣṭuta*)—O Indra, praised by many
- सः (*saḥ*)—you, of such greatness
- ओजसा (*ojasā*)—with strength
- वावृधानः (*vāvṛdhānaḥ*)—growing
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- सुश्रवस्तमः (*suśravastamaḥ*)—become an excellent giver of food

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you are possessed of wealth, lord of all heaven, of auspicious speech and exceedingly famous. O Indra, praised by many, you who have such greatness, growing in strength, become for us an excellent giver of food.

### Printed p. 81

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
You are possessed of opulence, Indra; you are the lord of heaven, auspicious and renowend: may you who are adored of many, increasing in vigour, be to us a most (bountiful bestower) of food.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Here Indra's independence and sovereignty are described. The words स्वायुः (*svāyuḥ*) and स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*) also indicate independence.

स्वयुः (*svayuḥ*)—This word is used in two places. In पशुर्नैति स्वयुरगोपाः (*paśurnaiti svayuragopāḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-4-7) it is explained as "one who moves about independently like an animal". In the present Ṛk it is explained as "one possessed of wealth". The purport lies in being without dependence on others and without subordination.

स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*)—It means one who is lord of himself. It can be taken as "self-luminous by his own greatness" or as "sovereign". This sovereignty is full of spiritual and moral principle. स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*) also means one who is lord over himself. Considered from that point of view, it also means that a person who is lord over himself is fit to be lord of the world. This very point is stated in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa: the purport is that one who is to be lord of the world must have complete self-restraint, and must have self-independence without being subject to outward powers. In this word स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*) a harmony of spiritual and Vedic practice is seen; the presence of a harmony of these two practices is the chief mark of Vedic culture. Besides this, the commentators have explained the word स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*) as "king of heaven". In accordance with the śruti sentence स एक राजो जगतः (*sa eka rājo jagataḥ*), the meaning that Indra himself is king of this world and of heaven is described in many places in the Ṛgveda and the Yajurveda.

As described in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa, the word स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*) must combine both aspects, lord of oneself and lord of the world. The purport is that one who is not self-restrained cannot be a sovereign. In the present Ṛk the two words स्वयुः (*svayuḥ*) and स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*) indicate these two special qualities of Indra. The meaning of the word स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*) can be stated from many points of view.

स्मद्दिष्टिः (*smaddiṣṭiḥ*)—It was stated earlier that the word स्मत् (*smat*) has the meanings beautiful, auspicious, propitious. दिष्टि (*diṣṭi*) is explained here as speech. The meaning is: one with auspicious speech. In Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-3-69 **NOTE** and similar places it means "of pleasing sight".

### Printed p. 82
## Sūkta 46 (Maṇḍala 3)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> युध्मस्य त इति पंचर्चमष्टमं सूक्तं वैश्वामित्रमैंद्रं । युद्धस्येत्यनुक्रमणिका ॥ दशरात्रे चतुर्थेऽहनि निष्केवल्ये युध्मस्य त इति सूक्तं निविद्वानं ।
> सूत्रितं च । कुह श्रुत इंद्रो युध्मस्य त इति निष्केवल्यं । आ. ७-११ । इति ॥ ऋषभनाम्मेकाहेऽपि निष्केवल्यइदं सूक्तं निविद्वानं । सूत्रितं च । युध्मस्य त इति
> मध्यंदिनः । आ. ९-७ इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The hymn beginning युध्मस्य ते (*yudhmasya te*) is a group of five ṛks, the eighth hymn. It is by Viśvāmitra and addressed to Indra (the Anukramaṇikā: युद्धस्य (*yuddhasya*)). Use: on the fourth day of the Daśarātra, at the Niṣkevalya, the hymn युध्मस्य ते (*yudhmasya te*) serves as the nivid-hymn. The sūtra is: कुह श्रुत इंद्रो युध्मस्य त इति निष्केवल्यं (*kuha śruta iṃdro yudhmasya ta iti niṣkevalyaṃ*) (Ā. 7-11). In the one-day rite called Ṛṣabha as well, this hymn is the nivid-hymn at the Niṣkevalya. The sūtra is: युध्मस्य त इति मध्यंदिनः (*yudhmasya ta iti madhyaṃdinaḥ*) (Ā. 9-7).

##### Anuvāda

This hymn, "Yudhmasya te", is the eighth hymn in the fourth anuvāka. It has five ṛks. Its seer is Viśvāmitra, its deity Indra, its metre Triṣṭubh. The Anukramaṇikā begins with "Yudhmasya". When the Niṣkevalya śastra mantras are recited on the fourth day of the sacrifice called Daśarātra, the ṛks of this hymn are used for the nivid mantras; this is stated by the Āśvalāyana-śrautasūtra, in the sūtra "Kuha śruta Indro yudhmasya ta iti niṣkevalyam" (Ā. 7-11). It is likewise explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana-śrautasūtra, "Yudhmasya ta iti madhyandinaḥ" (Ā. 9-7), that in the one-day sacrifice called Ṛṣabhanāman the ṛks of this hymn are used for the nivid mantras of the Niṣkevalya śastra.

##### Particulars

Hymn 46. Maṇḍala 3, Anuvāka 4, Sūkta 46. Aṣṭaka 3, Adhyāya 3, Varga 10. Number of the book (Ṛks) in the hymn: 5. Seer: Viśvāmitra. Deity: Indra. Metre: Triṣṭubh.

### Printed p. 83
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यु॒ध्मस्य॑ ते वृष॒भस्य॑ स्व॒राज॑ उ॒ग्रस्य॒ यूनः॒ स्थवि॑रस्य॒ फृष्टेः॑ ।  
> अजू॑र्यतो व॒ज्रिणो॑ वी॒र्या॒३॒॑णींद्र॑ श्रु॒तस्य॑ मह॒तो  ॒॑
> महानि ॥ १ ॥

> *yudhmasya te vṛṣabhasya svarāja ugrasya yūnaḥ sthavirasya phṛṣṭeḥ |*  
> *ajūryato vajriṇo vīryāṇīṃdra śrutasya mahato*  
> *mahāni ‖ 1 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युध्मस्य । ते । वृषभस्य । स्वराजः । उग्रस्य । यूनः । स्थविरस्य । फृष्टेः ।  
> अजूर्यतः । वज्रिणः । वीर्याणि । इंद्र । श्रुतस्य । महतः । महानि ॥ १ ॥

> *yudhmasya | te | vṛṣabhasya | svarājaḥ | ugrasya | yūnaḥ | sthavirasya | phṛṣṭeḥ |*  
> *ajūryataḥ | vajriṇaḥ | vīryāṇi | iṃdra | śrutasya | mahataḥ | mahāni ‖ 1 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र युध्मस्य योधनशीलस्य वृषभस्याभिमतफलसेचकस्य स्वराजो धनाधिपतेरुग्रस्य समर्थस्य यूनो नित्यतरुणस्य स्थविरस्य चिरंतनस्य घृष्टेः शत्रूणां
> संघर्षकस्याजूर्यतो जरारहितस्य वज्रिणः श्रुतस्य त्रिषु लोकेषु प्रसिद्धस्य अत
> एव महतस्ते तव वीर्याणि महानि । महांति खलु ॥ युध्मस्य । युध संप्रहारे ।
> इषियुधींधिदसीत्यादिना मक्प्रत्ययः । यूनः । यौतेः कनिन्युवृषीत्यादिना कनिन्।
> श्वयुवमघोनामतद्धित इति संप्रसारणं । नित्स्वरः । स्थविरस्य । अजिरशिशिरशिथिलेत्यादिना किरन्प्रत्ययांतो । निपातितः । घृष्टेः । घृषु संघर्षे । कृविघृष्टिच्छवीत्यादिना
> क्विन्प्रत्ययांतो निपात्यते । नित्त्वादाद्युदात्तः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, your heroic deeds are great: you are युध्मस्य (*yudhmasya*), given to fighting; वृषभस्य (*vṛṣabhasya*), the sprinkler of the desired fruit; स्वराजः (*svarājaḥ*), lord of wealth; उग्रस्य (*ugrasya*), capable; यूनः (*yūnaḥ*), ever young; स्थविरस्य (*sthavirasya*), ancient; घृष्टेः (*ghṛṣṭeḥ*), the grinder-down of enemies; अजूर्यतः (*ajūryataḥ*), free of old age; वज्रिणः (*vajriṇaḥ*), bearer of the thunderbolt; श्रुतस्य (*śrutasya*), famed in the three worlds. Therefore, being great, your heroic deeds are great; for they are indeed mighty. The remainder of the block gives derivations and accent notes on yudhmasya, yūnaḥ, sthavirasya and ghṛṣṭeḥ.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- युद्धस्य (*yuddhasya*)—of the excellent warrior
- वृषभस्य (*vṛṣabhasya*)—of the showerer of desires
- स्वराजः (*svarājaḥ*)—of the lord of all wealth
- उग्रस्य (*ugrasya*)—of the fearsome
- यूनः (*yūnaḥ*)—of the young
- स्थविरस्य (*sthavirasya*)—(at the same time) of the aged (the ancient)
- फृष्टैः (*phṛṣṭaiḥ*)—of him who crushes enemies
- अजूरतः (*ajūrataḥ*)—of him free from old age (decrepitude)
- वज्रिणः (*vajriṇaḥ*)—of the holder of the thunderbolt
- श्रुतस्य (*śrutasya*)—of him famed in the three worlds
- महतः (*mahataḥ*)—of him who is therefore endowed with greatness
- ते (*te*)—your
- वीर्याणि (*vīryāṇi*)—deeds of valour
- महानि (*mahāni*)—are most wonderful

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, excellent warrior, showerer of desires, lord of all wealth, young and ancient, crusher of enemies, free from old age, holder of the thunderbolt: for that very reason you are endowed with greatness, and your deeds of valour are most wonderful.

### Printed p. 84

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Vast are the energies of you, Indra, the warrior, (the showerer of benefits), the lord of wealth, the fierce; you are (both) young and old; the overcomer of foes, the undecaying, the wielder of the thunderbolt; and you are mighty and renowned.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

ते वीर्याणि महानि (*te vīryāṇi mahāni*)—In praising your heroic deeds as most wonderful, the verse also describes together with them the great qualities needed for such deeds. Earlier, "अहन्नहिमन्वपस्तर्द प्र वक्षणा अभिनत्पर्वतानां ; आत्सूरं जनयन्दामुषासं (*ahannahimanvapastarda pra vakṣaṇā abhinatparvatānāṃ ; ātsūraṃ janayandāmuṣāsaṃ*)" (Indra split the cloud, made a way for the streams flowing in the mountains, and created the sun, the dawn and the firmament for their light), and so on, describing Indra's valour, the glory of Indra was described with "इंद्रस्य नु वीर्याणि प्रवोचं (*iṃdrasya nu vīryāṇi pravocaṃ*)" (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-32). The fighting capacity, fierceness and self-rule useful for such great deeds are described in the present ṛk.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> म॒हा अ॑सि महिष॒ वृष्ट्ये॑भिर्धन॒स्पृदु॑ग्र॒ सह॑मानो अ॒न्यान् ।  
> एको॒ विश्व॑स्य॒ भुव॑नस्य॒ राजा॒ स यो॒धया॑ च क्ष॒यया॑ च॒  ॑
> जनान् ॥ २ ॥

> *mahā asi mahiṣa vṛṣṭyebhirdhanaspṛdugra sahamāno anyān |*  
> *eko viśvasya bhuvanasya rājā sa yodhayā ca kṣayayā ca*  
> *janān ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> महान् । असि । महिष । वृष्ण्येभिः । धनस्पृत् । उग्र । सहमानः । अन्यान् ।  
> एकः । विश्वस्य । भुवनस्य । राजा । सः । योधय । च । क्षयय । च । जनान् ॥ २ ॥

> *mahān | asi | mahiṣa | vṛṣṇyebhiḥ | dhanaspṛt | ugra | sahamānaḥ | anyān |*  
> *ekaḥ | viśvasya | bhuvanasya | rājā | saḥ | yodhaya | ca | kṣayaya | ca | janān ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे महिष महनीय उग्र इंद्र त्वं महानसि । धनस्पृत् धनानि दानभोगत्यागैः पारं नयंस्त्वं वृष्ट्येभिर्वीरैरन्यान् । शत्रून्सहमानोऽभिभवन्वर्तसे । तथा
> विश्वस्य सर्वस्य भुवनस्यैकोऽसहायो राजा स त्वं योधय च । ससत्नान् प्रहर ।
> जनान् क्षयय च । निवासय । बाधकान्हत्वा साधुजनान् स्वस्थाने स्थापयेत्यभिप्रायः ॥ महिष । मह पूजायां । अविमहोष्टिषच् । उ. १-४६ । इति टिषच्-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mahiṣa (worshipful), fierce Indra, you are great. As धनस्पृत् (*dhanaspṛt*) you carry riches across (to the far shore) by giving, enjoying and relinquishing them. With वृष्ट्येभिः (*vṛṣṭyebhiḥ*), with heroes, you overpower other enemies. You are the sole, unaided king of the whole world. Such as you, fight, and make the people dwell: slay the obstructors and settle good people in their own place, is the intention. The derivation of महिष (*mahiṣa*) is from mah, "to worship", with the suffix ṭiṣac (Uṇ. Sū. 1-46).

### Printed p. 85

> प्रत्ययः । आमंत्रितत्वान्निघातः । वृष्ट्येभिः । वृष्टि भवानि भवे छंदसीति यत् ।
> यतोऽनाव इत्याद्युदात्तः । योधय । युध संप्रहार इत्यस्य ण्यंतस्य लोटि
> रूपं । चादिषु चेति न निघातः । णित्स्वरः । क्षयय । क्षिनिवासगत्योरित्यस्य ण्यंतस्य
> लोटि रूपं । अत्रापि पूर्ववदनिघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This block continues with grammatical remarks (suffix, accent) on mahiṣa, vṛṣṭyebhiḥ, yodhaya and kṣayaya.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- महिष (*mahiṣa*)—venerable one
- उग्र (*ugra*)—fierce Indra
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- महान् असि (*mahān asi*)—you are of great form
- धनस्पृत् (*dhanaspṛt*)—you show wealth its uses such as giving
- वृष्ट्येभिः (*vṛṣṭyebhiḥ*)—by your valorous powers
- अन्यान् (*anyān*)—enemies
- सहमानः (*sahamānaḥ*)—you are the defeater
- विश्वस्य (*viśvasya*)—of all
- भुवनस्य (*bhuvanasya*)—the world
- एकः (*ekaḥ*)—alone
- राजा (*rājā*)—you are lord
- सः (*saḥ*)—such as you
- योधय च (*yodhaya ca*)—fight (our enemies and defeat them)
- जनान् (*janān*)—the people who perform sacrifice
- क्षयय चॆ (*kṣayaya cè*)—make them possessors of dwellings

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O worshipful and fearsome Indra, you are of great form. You show wealth its uses such as giving, and you defeat enemies by your valorous powers. You alone are lord of the whole world. Such as you, fight our enemies and defeat them, and give houses and other dwellings to the human sacrificers and protect them.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Adorable and powerful Indra, you are great and magnificent, over-coming adversaries by your prowess ; you alone are lord of all the world ; war (upon our enemies), and give safe dwellings to men.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

महिष (*mahiṣa*)—The word mahiṣa is read among the names for "great" in the Nirukta (Ni. 3-3). It means venerable, or else the accomplisher of great deeds. The Nirukta-author quotes the ṛk "अपामुपस्थे महिषा... (*apāmupasthe mahiṣā...*)" (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-8-4) and there explains this word as "deep". The intention here is that Indra's personal power is deep. In this power there is a blending of both beauty and terror(?). **NOTE**For that reason the epithet उग्र (*ugra*) is fitting. Hence, without giving here the meaning "sprinkler" for the word वृष्णेभिः (*vṛṣṇebhiḥ*), it is shown that it is used in the sense of "power": वृष्णेभिः (*vṛṣṇebhiḥ*) means "by powers that accomplish great deeds".

### Printed p. 86
As described in the Aitareya-brāhmaṇa, Indra, once consecrated, obtained the rank of self-rule. The description "भुवनस्य एकः राजाः (*bhuvanasya ekaḥ rājāḥ*)" shows the point that there is no other lord of the world than him. Or else this description also shows that there is no other illuminator of the world than Indra, who is of the nature of the sun.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र मात्रा॑भी रिरिचे॒ रोच॑मानः॒ प्र दे॒वेभि॑र्वि॒श्वतो॒  ॑
> अप्र॑तीतः ।  
> प्र म॒ज्मना॑ दि॒व इंद्रः॑ पृथि॒व्याः प्रोरोर्म॒हो अं॒तरि॑क्षादृजी॒षी ॥ ३ ॥

> *pra mātrābhī ririce rocamānaḥ pra devebhirviśvato*  
> *apratītaḥ |*  
> *pra majmanā diva iṃdraḥ pṛthivyāḥ prorormaho aṃtarikṣādṛjīṣī ‖ 3 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । मात्राभिः । रिरिचे । रोचमानः । प्र । देवेभिः । विश्वतः । अप्रतिइत ।  
> प्र । मज्मना । दिवः । इंद्रः । पृथिव्याः । प्र । उरोः । महः । अंतरिक्षात् । ऋजीषी॥

> *pra | mātrābhiḥ | ririce | rocamānaḥ | pra | devebhiḥ | viśvataḥ | apratiita |*  
> *pra | majmanā | divaḥ | iṃdraḥ | pṛthivyāḥ | pra | uroḥ | mahaḥ | aṃtarikṣāt | ṛjīṣī‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> रोचमानो दीप्यमानो विश्वतः सर्वैरप्रतीत एतावदस्य सामर्थ्यमित्यपरिच्छिद्यमान यजीषी सोमवानिंद्रो मात्राभिः । मीयंते परिच्चिद्यंत इति मात्राः
> शिलोच्चयाः । तेभ्योऽपि प्र रिरिचे । प्रकर्षण महत्तयातिरिक्कोऽभूत् । तथा
> देवेभिर्देवेभ्योऽपि मज्जना । मज्जयति शत्रोन्मुखसागर इति मज्ज बलं । तेन
> बलेन प्ररिरिचे । तथा दिवः पृथिव्या द्यावापृथिवीभ्यां च प्ररिरिचे । तथोरोर्विस्तीर्णान्महो महतोंतरिक्षादपि प्ररिरिचे॥ मात्राभिः माज् माने शब्द चेत्यस्मात्
> हुयामाश्रुभसिभ्यस्त्रन्निति त्रन्प्रत्ययः । नित्वादाद्युदात्तः । ढ्रलोपे पूर्वस्य
> दीर्घोऽण इति संहितायां दीर्घः । रोचमानः । रुच दीप्तावित्यस्मात् शानचि
> रूपं । तस्य लसार्वधातुकस्वरे धातुस्वरः । अप्रतीतः। इण् गतौ । कर्मणि क्तः। प्रादिसमासः । नञा समासे तस्य स्वरः । मज्जना । टुमस्टो शुद्धौ । औणादिको मनिच्प्रत्ययः । झलां झश् झशीति जत्त्वं । चित्वादंतोदात्तः । पृथिव्याः । उदात्तयणो हल्पूर्वादिति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, shining, not fully known by all ("this much is his power"), the Soma-possessor, has surpassed the मात्राः (*mātrāḥ*). These are things that are measured and bounded, namely rock-heaps (शिलोच्चयाः (*śiloccayāḥ*)); he has exceeded even these, becoming greatly excessive. Likewise he has surpassed even the gods by मज्जना (*majjanā*), that is by strength: majja is strength, because it plunges (मज्जयति (*majjayati*)) into the face of the sea of enemies. By that strength he has surpassed; so too heaven and earth, and the vast, great firmament. The rest of the block gives derivations and accent notes (mātrābhiḥ, rocamānaḥ, apratītaḥ, majjanā, pṛthivyāḥ). **NOTE**

### Printed p. 87

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- रोचमानः (*rocamānaḥ*)—shining
- विश्वतः (*viśvataḥ*)—by all people (by anyone)
- अप्रतीतः (*apratītaḥ*)—one who cannot be measured
- ऋजीषीः (*ṛjīṣīḥ*)—Soma-possessing
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- मात्राभिः (*mātrābhiḥ*)—than all beings
- प्र रिरिचे (*pra ririce*)—has grown with greater measure
- मज्मना (*majmanā*)—by power
- देवेभिः (*devebhiḥ*)—than the gods
- प्र (*pra*)—he is greater
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—than the heavenly world
- पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*)—than the earth
- प्र रिरिचे (*pra ririce*)—he is greater
- उरोः (*uroḥ*)—than the wide
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—and great
- अंतरिक्षात् (*aṃtarikṣāt*)—than the firmament
- प्र (रिरिचे) (*pra (ririce)*)—he is exceedingly greater

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, shining, immeasurable by anyone, and Soma-possessing, has grown with greater measure than all beings. In power he is greater than the gods. He is exceedingly greater than the firmament, which is wider and greater than the earth.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
The resplendent and every way unlimited Indra, drinking the spiritless Soma juice, far surpasses the elements, (far exceeds) the gods in power; he is more vast than heaven and earth, or the widespread spacious firmament.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Indra's immeasurable nature is described here. The meaning is that he is greater than all things that are bounded, that is, that come within man's measure; in other words he is not an object that comes within man's measure.
मात्राभिः (*mātrābhiḥ*)—As explained earlier in Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-38-3, here too it is explained: "मीयंते मांतीति वा मात्रा: शिलोच्चयाः (*mīyaṃte māṃtīti vā mātrā: śiloccayāḥ*)". It is formed from the root mā, meaning "to measure". Although it applies to all things possessing limit, here it is explained as lofty rock-heaps. When describing the nature of all the elements such as earth, the Upaniṣads use this word thus: "पृथिवी च पृथिवीमात्रा चापश्चापोमात्राच तेजश्चतेजोमात्रा च वायुश्च नायुमात्रा च आकाशश्चाकाशमात्रा ॥ (*pṛthivī ca pṛthivīmātrā cāpaścāpomātrāca tejaścatejomātrā ca vāyuśca nāyumātrā ca ākāśaścākāśamātrā ‖*)" (Praśne. 4-8). **NOTE** In the quoted sentence the word mātrā has the technical meaning tanmātra ("subtle element"). Here there is no room for a philosophical discussion; in sum, they may be called things confined to the limits of man. The intention is that Indra has grown beyond all these.

### Printed p. 88
ऋजीषी (*ṛjīṣī*)—This epithet is because he is accompanied by Soma. It is used chiefly in describing the nature of Indra and the Maruts. In such contexts as "अमत्रेभिर्यजीषिणिमिंद्रं (*amatrebhiryajīṣiṇimiṃdraṃ*)" (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-42-2) **NOTE** the word ṛjīṣa is explained as power; it is explained as "of Indra, who has obtained power that obstructs enemies". But in many contexts this epithet is well known precisely because he is accompanied by Soma.
As in "अणोरणीयान् महतो महीयान् (*aṇoraṇīyān mahato mahīyān*)", here too Indra's greatness is described by "उरोः महः अंतरिक्षात् प्र रिरिचे (*uroḥ mahaḥ aṃtarikṣāt pra ririce*)". Since Indra reflects all principles such as Vāyu and Āditya, it may be said that the supreme principle described in the Upaniṣads is at times regarded in the Saṃhitā in the form of Indra. This point will be explained later in the description of the spiritual nature of Indra and the other deities.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒रुं ग॑भी॒रं ज॒नुषा॒भ्यु१॒ं॑ग्रं वि॒श्वव्य॑चसमव॒तं म॑ती॒नां ।  
> इंद्रं॒ सोमा॑सः प्र॒दिवि॑ सु॒तासः॑ समु॒द्रं न स्र॒वत॒ आ वि॑शंति ॥

> *uruṃ gabhīraṃ januṣābhyuṃgraṃ viśvavyacasamavataṃ matīnāṃ |*  
> *iṃdraṃ somāsaḥ pradivi sutāsaḥ samudraṃ na sravata ā viśaṃti ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उरुं । गभीरं । जनुषा । अभि । उग्रं । विश्वव्यचसं । अवतं । मतीनां ।  
> इंद्रं । सोमासः । प्रदिवि । सुतासः । समुद्रं । न । स्रवतः । आ । विशंति ॥ ४ ॥

> *uruṃ | gabhīraṃ | januṣā | abhi | ugraṃ | viśvavyacasaṃ | avataṃ | matīnāṃ |*  
> *iṃdraṃ | somāsaḥ | pradivi | sutāsaḥ | samudraṃ | na | sravataḥ | ā | viśaṃti ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उरुं महांतं आत एव गभीरं महावकाशं जनुषा जन्मना स्वभावत एवोग्रं
> शत्रूणां भयंकरं । गभीरत्वमेवोच्यते । विश्वव्यचसं विश्वव्याप्तं मतीनां
> स्तुतीनां स्तोत्कणां वावतं रक्षकं तादृशनिंद्रं प्रदिवि पूर्वष्टहःसु सुतासो
> ग्रावभिरभिषुताः सोमासः सोमा अभि आभिमुख्येना विशंति । तत्र दृष्टांतः ।
> समुद्रं न । यथा स्रवतः स्रवंत्यो नद्यः समुद्रमाविशंति तद्वत् ॥ जनुषा । जनी
> प्रादुर्भाव इत्यस्माज्जनेरुसिरित्युसिप्रत्ययः । विश्वव्यचसं ! व्यच वाजीकरण इत्यस्मादसुन् । व्यचेः कुटादित्वमनसीति वक्तव्यमिश्यत्रानसीति पर्युदासात् जित्वाभावः ।
> बहुन्रीहौ विश्वं संज्ञायामिति पूर्वपदस्यांतोदात्तत्वं । अवतु । अव
> रक्षणादिषु । औणादिकोऽतच् प्रत्ययः । चित्वादंतोदात्तः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra is उरुं (*uruṃ*), great, and therefore गभीरं (*gabhīraṃ*), of great expanse; जनुषा (*januṣā*), by birth, by nature, fierce, terrifying to enemies. (Only depth is meant.) He is विश्वव्यचसं (*viśvavyacasaṃ*), all-pervading, and the protector (अवतं (*avataṃ*)) of the praises, or of the praisers. To such an Indra the Somas, pressed with the stones in प्रदिवि (*pradivi*), the earlier days, enter from the front. The illustration: समुद्रं न (*samudraṃ na*), as flowing rivers enter the ocean, so they. The rest of the block gives derivations and accent notes (januṣā, viśvavyacasam, avatam).

### Printed p. 89

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उरुं (*uruṃ*)—one of vast form
- गभीरं (*gabhīraṃ*)—one with an unfathomable belly
- जनुषा (*januṣā*)—naturally (from birth itself)
- उग्रं (*ugraṃ*)—terrible to enemies
- विश्वव्यचसं (*viśvavyacasaṃ*)—all-pervading
- मतीनां (*matīnāṃ*)—to the praises
- अवतं (*avataṃ*)—the protector
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra (accusative)
- प्रदिवि (*pradivi*)—on former days
- सुतासः (*sutāsaḥ*)—pressed
- सोमासः (*somāsaḥ*)—the Soma juices
- स्रवतः (*sravataḥ*)—flowing rivers
- समुद्रं न (*samudraṃ na*)—as they enter the deep sea
- अभि (*abhi*)—towards Indra's belly
- आ विशंति (*ā viśaṃti*)—enter, flowing

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As long-flowing rivers enter the unfathomable sea, the Soma juices pressed on former days enter Indra, who is of vast form, who has an unfathomable belly, who is naturally terrible to enemies, all-pervading, and the protector of the praises.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
The Soma juices daily poured out enter into the vast and profound Indra, fierce from his birth, all-pervading, the protector of the pious (worshippers), as rivers flow into the sea.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

As described in the previous Ṛk, here too Indra's all-pervasiveness is described. In such contexts सोमासः (*somāsaḥ*) cannot be given merely the sacrificial-procedure meaning "Soma juice"; moreover the simile स्रवतः समुद्रं न (*sravataḥ samudraṃ na*) conveys an extraordinary meaning in such contexts. What is Indra's belly? How does a flow of Soma run there? One must examine what common quality there is between this vast form and the form described by the epithet विश्वव्यचसं (*viśvavyacasaṃ*). Examining the extraordinary qualities of Indra described earlier and those to be described later, it must be decided further on whether the principle of Indra is identical with the principle of the Supreme Brahman described in the Upaniṣads and the like, or whether, as modern scholars hold, Indra is a deity subject to a lower principle.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यं सोम॑मिंद्र पृथि॒वीद्यावा॒ गर्भं॒ न मा॒ता बि॑भृ॒तस्त्वा॒या ।  
> तं ते॑ हिन्वंति॒ तमु॑ ते मृजंत्यध्व॒र्यवो॑ वृषभ॒ पात॒वा  ॑
> उ ॥ ५ ॥

> *yaṃ somamiṃdra pṛthivīdyāvā garbhaṃ na mātā bibhṛtastvāyā |*  
> *taṃ te hinvaṃti tamu te mṛjaṃtyadhvaryavo vṛṣabha pātavā*  
> *u ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यं । सोमं । इंद्र । प्रधिवीद्यावा । गर्भं । न । माता । बिभृतः । त्वाया ।  
> तं । ते । हिन्वंति । तं । ऊं इति । ते । मृजंति । अध्वर्यवः । वृषभ । पातवै ।  
> ऊं इति ॥ ५ ॥

> *yaṃ | somaṃ | iṃdra | pradhivīdyāvā | garbhaṃ | na | mātā | bibhṛtaḥ | tvāyā |*  
> *taṃ | te | hinvaṃti | taṃ | ūṃ iti | te | mṛjaṃti | adhvaryavaḥ | vṛṣabha | pātavai |*  
> *ūṃ iti ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र पृथिवीद्यावा द्यावापृथिव्य त्वाया त्वत्कामनया यं सोमं
> बिभृतोऽंतर्धारयतः । अत्र दृष्टांतः । गर्भ० न । यथा माता गर्भ० बिभर्ति तद्वत्
> हे वृषभ कामानां वर्षक हे इंद्र अध्वर्यवोऽध्वरं यजमानायेच्छंतस्ते
> त्वदर्थ० तं सोमं हिन्वंति । प्रेरयंति । किंच ते तव पातवै पानाय तं सोमं
> मृजंति । शोधयंति । उ प्रसिद्धौ ॥ पृथिवीद्यावा । दिवो द्यावेति द्यावादेशः ।
> आद्युदात्तः । नोत्तरपदेऽनुदात्तादावपृथिवीरुद्रपूषमंथिष्यत्र पर्युदासाद्देवताद्वंद्व चेत्युभयपदप्रकृतिस्वरः । कृतसमासस्य विपर्यासश्चांदसः । बिभृतः ।
> डुभृञ् धारणपोषणयोरित्यस्माल्लट भृञामिदित्यभ्यासस्यॆत्वं । यद्वृत्तयोगादनिघातः । अजादिप्रत्ययपरत्वाभावादत्राभ्यस्तानामादिरिति स्वरो न भवति । प्रत्ययस्वरः । त्वाया । त्वामिच्छतीत्यर्थे सुप आत्मनः क्यच् । पूर्वपददकारस्याकारश्चांदसः।
> अ प्रत्ययादिति भावेऽप्रत्ययः । सुपां सुलुगिति तृतीयायाः सवर्णदीर्घः । एकादेशस्वरः । अध्वर्यवः । अध्वरमिच्छंतः । सुपः क्यच् । कव्यध्वरपृतनस्यर्चि लोप
> इत्याकारलोपः । क्याच्छंदसीत्युप्रत्ययः। प्रत्ययस्वरः । पातवै । पा पाने। तुमर्थे
> तवैप्रत्ययः । अंतश्च तवै युगपदिति धात्वंतस्य प्रत्ययस्य चोदात्तत्वं । उञ् इति
> प्रगृह्यसंज्ञा ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, the Heaven and Earth, out of desire for you (त्वाया (*tvāyā*), "wishing you"), hold within themselves the Soma." Here is the example: गर्भ० न (*garbha0 na*)—as a mother carries the embryo. "O Indra, showerer (वृषभ (*vṛṣabha*)) of desires, the Adhvaryus, desiring the sacrifice for the sacrificer, impel (हिन्वंति (*hinvaṃti*), "send forth") that Soma for you. Moreover they purify (मृजंति (*mṛjaṃti*)) that Soma for your drinking (पातवै (*pātavai*))." उ (*u*) is a particle of well-known (emphatic) sense.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- माता (*mātā*)—the mother
- गर्भं न (*garbhaṃ na*)—as she carefully protects and bears her child in the womb with affection
- पृथिवीद्यावा (*pṛthivīdyāvā*)—Heaven and Earth both
- त्वाया (*tvāyā*)—with reference to you
- यं सोमं (*yaṃ somaṃ*)—which Soma
- बिभृतः (*bibhṛtaḥ*)—they hold
- तं (*taṃ*)—that same Soma
- वृषभ (*vṛṣabha*)—O Indra, showerer of desires
- अध्वरवः (*adhvaravaḥ*)—the Adhvaryus **NOTE**
- ते (*te*)—for you
- हिन्वंति (*hinvaṃti*)—press and hold
- ते (*te*)—your
- पातनै (*pātanai*)—for drinking **NOTE**
- तं (*taṃ*)—that Soma
- मृजंति (*mṛjaṃti*)—they purify

### Printed p. 91

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, showerer of desires, as a mother carefully protects and bears her child in her womb, so Heaven and Earth both hold for you the Soma; in the same way the Adhvaryus hold Soma for you. For your drinking they purify that same Soma.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Which Soma, Indra, the heaven and earth contain for you, as a mother contains the embryo; showerer (of benefits), the priests pour it out for you they purify it for your drinking.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

As indicated in the previous Ṛk, it is understood that here too the word सोम (*soma*) must have a special meaning. As described in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa (11-5-9-10-11), it can be understood as a special radiance that gives beauty to creative power; or it may be a special essence that is the cause of that radiance. The god Soma is also of the form of Āditya, lord of radiance (Śa. Brā. 11-5-9). In the contexts where it is said that Soma is offered to Soma himself, the same kind of special meaning must be given. There is a hidden meaning in the statement that Heaven and Earth hold Soma for the increase of Indra: just as Heaven and Earth, fundamental to the world's functioning, are the cause of the increase of Indra's radiance, so the Adhvaryus too are the cause. This purport is stated on the basis of the above-cited passage of the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa.

## Sūkta 47 (Maṇḍala 3)
The forty-seventh sūkta.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> मरुत्वानिति पंचर्चं नवमं सूक्तं वैश्वामित्रं त्रैष्टुभमैंद्रं । मरुत्वानित्यनुक्रमणिका ॥ दशरात्रे चतुर्थेऽहनि मरुत्वतीयशस्त्र एतत्तूक्तं निविद्धानीयं ।
> सूत्रितं च । श्रुधी हवमिंद्र मरुत्वा इंद्रेति मरुत्वतीयं । आ. ७-११ । इति ॥
> ऋषभनाम्न्यकाहेऽपि मरुत्वतीयशस्त्र एतन्निविद्वानं । सूत्रितं च । मरुत्वा इंद्र
> युध्मस्य त इति मध्यंदिनः । आ. ९-७ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning मरुत्वान् (*marutvān*) has five Ṛks; it is the ninth sūkta (of the anuvāka), by Viśvāmitra, in the Triṣṭubh metre, addressed to Indra; the Anukramaṇikā says "मरुत्वान् (*marutvān*)". In the Daśarātra, on the fourth day, this sūkta is used at the Marutvatīya śastra as the one carrying the nivid. The sūtra says: श्रुधी हवमिंद्र मरुत्वा इंद्रेति मरुत्वतीयं (*śrudhī havamiṃdra marutvā iṃdreti marutvatīyaṃ*) (Ā. 7-11). In the one-day rite named Ṛṣabha also, this is the nivid-bearing sūkta at the Marutvatīya śastra; the sūtra says: मरुत्वा इंद्र युध्मस्य त इति मध्यंदिनः (*marutvā iṃdra yudhmasya ta iti madhyaṃdinaḥ*) (Ā. 9-7).

### Printed p. 92

> अनुवादवु— मरुत्वान् ऎंब ई सूक्तवु नाल्कनॆय अनुवाकदल्लि ऒंभत्तनॆय सूक्तवु.
> इदरल्लि ऐदु ऋक्कुगळिरुववु. ई सूक्तक्कॆ विश्वामित्रनु ऋषियु, इंद्रनु देवतॆयु, त्रिष्टुप्
> छंदस्सु. अनुक्रमणिकॆयल्लि मरुत्वान् ऎंदु मॊदलागि हेळिरुवुदु दशरात्रवॆंब यागद
> नाल्कनॆय दिवस मरुत्वतीयशस्त्रमंत्रपठनकालदल्लि ई सूक्तवन्नु निविन्मंत्रगळिगागि उपयोगि
> सबेकॆंदु अश्वलायनशौतसूत्रद—श्रुधी हवमिंद्र मरुत्वा इंद्रेति मरुत्वतीयं ऎंब
> सूत्रदिंदलू (आ. ७-११), ऒंदु दिवसमात्र माडल्पडुव ऋषभनाम्मियॆंब यागदल्लि मरु
> त्वतीयशस्त्रमंत्रगळन्नु पठिसुवाग निविन्मंत्रगळिगागि ई सूक्तद विनियोगविरुवुदॆंदु आश्वलायन
> शौतसूत्रद—मरुत्वा इंद्र युध्मस्य त इति मध्यंदिनः ऎंब सूत्रदिंदलू (आ. ९-७)
> विवृतवागिरुवुदु.
> ॥ ओं ॥
> सूक्त—४७
> मंडल—३ ॥ अनुवाक—४ ॥ सूक्त—४७ ॥
> अष्टक—३ ॥ अध्याय—३ ॥ वर्ग—११ ॥
> ॥ सूक्तदल्लिरुव ऋक्संख्यॆ—५ ॥
> , ऋषिः— विश्वामित्रः
> । देवता— इंद्रः ।
> । छंदः — त्रिष्टुप् ।

Anuvāda: This sūkta beginning "Marutvān" is the ninth sūkta in the fourth anuvāka and has five Ṛks. Viśvāmitra is its seer, Indra the deity, Triṣṭubh the metre. The Anukramaṇikā begins with "Marutvān". It is to be used for the nivid mantras at the time of reciting the Marutvatīya śastra mantras on the fourth day of the sacrifice called Daśarātra, as stated by the Āśvalāyana-Śrautasūtra "श्रुधी हवमिंद्र मरुत्वा इंद्रेति मरुत्वतीयं (*śrudhī havamiṃdra marutvā iṃdreti marutvatīyaṃ*)" (Ā. 7-11); and in the sacrifice called Ṛṣabhanāmnī, performed in a single day, the use of this sūkta for the nivid mantras while reciting the Marutvatīya śastra mantras is explained by the Āśvalāyana-Śrautasūtra "मरुत्वा इंद्र युध्मस्य त इति मध्यंदिनः (*marutvā iṃdra yudhmasya ta iti madhyaṃdinaḥ*)" (Ā. 9-7). Particulars: Sūkta 47; Maṇḍala 3, Anuvāka 4, Sūkta 47; Aṣṭaka 3, Adhyāya 3, Varga 11; number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5; Ṛṣi: Viśvāmitra; Devatā: Indra; Chandas: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> म॒रुत्वा॑ इंद्र वृष॒भो रणा॑य॒ सिबा॒ सोम॑नुस्व॒धं  ॑
> मदा॑य ।  
> आ सिं॑चस्व ज॒ठरे॒ मध्व॑ ऊ॒र्मिं त्वं राजा॑सि प्र॒दिवः॑ सु॒तानां॑॥

> *marutvā iṃdra vṛṣabho raṇāya sibā somanusvadhaṃ*  
> *madāya |*  
> *ā siṃcasva jaṭhare madhva ūrmiṃ tvaṃ rājāsi pradivaḥ sutānāṃ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मरुत्वान् । इंद्र । वृषभः । रणाय । पिज । सोमं । अनुस्वधं । मदाय ।  
> आ । सिंचस्व । जठरे । मध्वः । उर्मिं । त्वं । राजा । असि । प्रदिवः । सुतानां ॥ १ ॥

> *marutvān | iṃdra | vṛṣabhaḥ | raṇāya | pija | somaṃ | anusvadhaṃ | madāya |*  
> *ā | siṃcasva | jaṭhare | madhvaḥ | urmiṃ | tvaṃ | rājā | asi | pradivaḥ | sutānāṃ ‖ 1 ‖*

### Printed p. 93

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र यो वृषभोऽपां वर्षिता मरुत्वान् । मरुतो देवविशस्तद्वान् ।
> स त्वमनुष्वधं स्वधया सवनीयपुरोडाशादिरूपेणान्नेनानुगतं सोमं रणाय
> रमणीयसंग्रामार्थं मदाय तं सोमं पिब । किंच मध्यो मदकॆरस्य सोमस्त्योर्मि संघातं बहुरसं जठरे । जग्धमन्नमस्मिंस्तिष्ठतीति जठरमुदरं । तस्मिन्नुदर आ सिंचस्व । सर्वतः क्षारय । यतस्त्वं प्रदिवः पूर्वेष्वहःसु सुतानामभिषुतानां सोमानां राजासीशितासि । न केवलमधुनातनानामिति भावः । उक्तमर्थं
> यास्को ब्रवीति । मरुत्वानिंद मरुद्भिस्तद्वान् वृषभो वर्षितापां रणाय
> रमणीयाय संग्रामाय पिब सोममनुष्वधमन्वन्नं मदाय मदनीयाय
> जैत्रायासिंच स्वजठरे मधुन ऊर्मि० । मधु सोममित्यौपमिकं माद्यतेरिदमपीतरन्मध्वतस्मादेव । त्वं राजासि पूर्वेष्वस्यहःसु सुतानां । नि. ४-८ । इति ॥
> अनुस्वधं । स्वधामनुगम्य वर्तमानं । अत्यादयः क्रांताद्यर्थे द्वितीययेति
> समासः । संहितायां पूर्वपदात् । पा. ८-३-१०६ । इति षत्वं । निरुदकादित्वादंतोदात्तत्वं । ऊर्मि० । अर्तॆरुच्चेति मिप्रत्ययः धातोरू इत्यादेशो रपरः ।
> प्रत्ययस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, you who are the showerer (वृषभ (*vṛṣabha*)) of waters and मरुत्वान् (*marutvān*)—possessed of the Maruts, the divine clan—drink that Soma, which is accompanied (अनुस्वधं (*anusvadhaṃ*)) by स्वधा (*svadhā*), that is, by food in the form of the puroḍāśa and the like of the pressing, for the delightful (रणाय (*raṇāya*)) battle and for exhilaration (मदाय (*madāya*)). Moreover, pour the wave (ऊर्मिं (*ūrmiṃ*), the collected mass, abundant juice) of the intoxicating sweet Soma into your belly (जठरे (*jaṭhare*); the belly is so called because the food eaten stays in it); pour it in all around. For you are the lord of the Somas pressed on former days (प्रदिवः (*pradivaḥ*))—not only of the present ones." Yāska states the same meaning: मरुत्वानिंद मरुद्भिस्तद्वान् वृषभो वर्षितापां रणाय रमणीयाय संग्रामाय पिब सोममनुष्वधमन्वन्नं मदाय मदनीयाय जैत्रायासिंच स्वजठरे मधुन ऊर्मि० । मधु सोममित्यौपमिकं माद्यतेरिदमपीतरन्मध्वतस्मादेव । त्वं राजासि पूर्वेष्वस्यहःसु सुतानां । (*marutvāniṃda marudbhistadvān vṛṣabho varṣitāpāṃ raṇāya ramaṇīyāya saṃgrāmāya piba somamanuṣvadhamanvannaṃ madāya madanīyāya jaitrāyāsiṃca svajaṭhare madhuna ūrmi0 | madhu somamityaupamikaṃ mādyateridamapītaranmadhvatasmādeva | tvaṃ rājāsi pūrveṣvasyahaḥsu sutānāṃ |*) (Ni. 4-8).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- वृषभः (*vṛṣabhaḥ*)—you who pour down rain
- मरुत्वान् (*marutvān*)—who are accompanied by the Maruts
- अनुष्वधं (*anuṣvadhaṃ*)—accompanied by food in the form of svadhā
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma
- रणाय (*raṇāya*)—in battle
- मदाय (*madāya*)—for increasing your valour
- पिब (*piba*)—drink
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—of the intoxicating Soma
- ऊर्मि (*ūrmi*)—the essence in the form of a wave
- जठरे (*jaṭhare*)—in your belly
- आ सिंचस्व (*ā siṃcasva*)—pour fully
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- प्रदिवः (*pradivaḥ*)—on former days
- सुतानां (*sutānāṃ*)—of all the pressed Somas
- राजा (*rājā*)—as lord
- असि (*asi*)—you are

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you who pour down rain and are accompanied by the Maruts, drink the Soma accompanied by food in the form of svadhā, in battle, to increase your valour. Pour fully into your belly the Soma essence, which is intoxicating and in the form of a wave. You are the lord of all the Somas pressed on former days.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra, attended by the Maruts, the showerer (of benefits), drink the Soma offered after the other presentations, for your exhilaration for battle ; take into your belly the (full) wave of the inebriating (Soma), for you are lord of libations from the days of old.

### Printed p. 94

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

अनुष्पधं (*anuṣpadhaṃ*)—two words, अनु (*anu*) + स्वधा (*svadhā*), are joined: drinking of Soma after the offering of food in the form of svadhā. The scholar Monier Williams, giving the meaning of the word अनुष्पधं (*anuṣpadhaṃ*), says "According one's own will ; voluntary ;" that is, acting by one's own impulse without the prompting of others, and he refers to the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā; but nowhere in the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā has Sāyaṇa given this meaning.
ऊर्मि (*ūrmi*)—ऊर्मिरूर्णोतेः (*ūrmirūrṇoteḥ*): it is the view of the Nirukta author that it is derived from the root ऊर्ण (*ūrṇa*), meaning to cover, conceal, envelop (Ni. 5-23). (The Uṇādi sūtra आर्तॆरच्च (*ārtèracca*) is recited.) Just as it is used in the sense of wave (billow), the word is also used in the sense of mass of water, collection of water. Here it indicates the abundance of the Soma juice.
रणाय (*raṇāya*)—On the basis of the original meaning "delightful", the word रण (*raṇa*) is a synonym of battle. When the gods gather together and fight, there is a joyful cry that victory is assured; so (रणयंति (*raṇayaṃti*)) the word indicates that the battlefield too is delightful.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒जोषा॑ इंद्र॒ सग॑णो म॒रुद्भिः॒ सोमं॑ पिब वृतह्र॒ा शू॑र  ॒
> विद्वान् ।  
> ज॒हि शत्रू॒रप॒ मृधो॑ नुद॒स्वाथाभ॑यं कृणुहि वि॒श्वतो॑ नः

> *sajoṣā iṃdra sagaṇo marudbhiḥ somaṃ piba vṛtahrā śūra*  
> *vidvān |*  
> *jahi śatrūrapa mṛdho nudasvāthābhayaṃ kṛṇuhi viśvato naḥ*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सजोषाः । इंद्र । सगणः । मरुत्भिः । सोमं । पिब । वृत्रहा । शूर । विद्वान् ।  
> जहि । शत्रून् । अप । मृधः । नुदस्व । अथ । अभयं । कृणुहि । विश्वतः । नः । ॥ २ ॥

> *sajoṣāḥ | iṃdra | sagaṇaḥ | marutbhiḥ | somaṃ | piba | vṛtrahā | śūra | vidvān |*  
> *jahi | śatrūn | apa | mṛdhaḥ | nudasva | atha | abhayaṃ | kṛṇuhi | viśvataḥ | naḥ | ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सजोषा इंद्रेति मरुत्वतीयग्रहस्य याज्या । सूत्रितं च । सजोषा इंद्र
> सगणो मरुद्भिरिति भक्षयित्वैतत्पात्रं । आ. ५-१४ । इति ॥
> हे शूर बलवन् हे इंद्र सजोषाः संगतो देवैर्मरुद्भिः सगणो मरुद्भिरेव वृत्रहा वृत्रस्य हंता विद्वान्कर्मविषयाभिज्ञस्त्वं सोमं पिब । ततो नः

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The verse सजोषा इंद्र (*sajoṣā iṃdra*) is the yājyā of the Marutvatīya graha. The sūtra says: सजोषा इंद्र सगणो मरुद्भिरिति भक्षयित्वैतत्पात्रं (*sajoṣā iṃdra sagaṇo marudbhiriti bhakṣayitvaitatpātraṃ*) (Ā. 5-14). "O mighty hero (शूर (*śūra*)) Indra, you who are united (सजोषाः (*sajoṣāḥ*)) with the gods the Maruts, accompanied by your troop (सगणः (*sagaṇaḥ*)) by the Maruts alone, the slayer of Vṛtra, knowing (विद्वान् (*vidvān*)) the subject of the rites—drink the Soma. Then for us …" (the block breaks off here; the next window continues).

### Printed p. 95
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> शत्रून् जहि । मृधो हिंसकान् शत्रूनप नुदस्व । यद्वा मृधः। हिंस्यंतेऽत्र प्राणिन
> इति मृधः संग्रामाः । तत्र वर्तमानान् शत्रूनसनुदस्व । अथानंतरं नोऽस्माकं
> विश्वतः सर्वतोऽभयं कृणुहि । कुरु । सर्वतो भयरहितानस्मान्कुर्वित्यर्थः ॥
> वृत्रहा । हंतेब्रह्मभ्रूणवृत्तेषु क्विप् । अभयं । इंभी भय इत्यस्मादेरच् ।
> नञा समासे तस्य स्वरः । विश्वतः । पंचम्यास्तसिल् । लित्स्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa continues: "Slay the enemies. मृधः (*mṛdhaḥ*) are harmful enemies; drive them away." Or else मृधः (*mṛdhaḥ*) means battles, because living beings are injured in them ("they are harmed here"); drive away the enemies who stand in those battles. Then, afterwards, make us fearless on every side. The meaning is "make us free from fear everywhere."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शूर (*śūra*)—valorous
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- मरुद्भिः (*marudbhiḥ*)—with the Maruts
- सगणः (*sagaṇaḥ*)—united (with them)
- सजोषाः (*sajoṣāḥ*)—having equal affection (for them)
- वृत्रहा (*vṛtrahā*)—slayer of Vṛtra
- विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—wise; you who are such
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma juice
- पिब (*piba*)—drink
- शत्र्यन् (*śatryan*)—our enemies
- जहि (*jahi*)—defeat
- मृधः (*mṛdhaḥ*)—the enemies in battle
- आप नुदस्व (*āpa nudasva*)—destroy
- अथ (*atha*)—afterwards
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- विश्वतः (*viśvataḥ*)—from all sides
- अभयं (*abhayaṃ*)—freedom from fear
- कृणुहि (*kṛṇuhi*)—bring about

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O valorous Indra, you who are united with the Maruts, who have equal affection for them, who are the slayer of Vṛtra and are wise, drink the Soma juice. Defeat our enemies. Destroy the enemies who are intent on battle, and afterwards make us free from fear on every side.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra, hero, rejoicing with and accompanied by the host of Maruts, drink the Soma, for you are the slayer of Vritra, the sage; subdue our enemies, drive away the malevolent, make us safe on every side from peril.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

That this Ṛk beginning सजोषा इंद्र (*sajoṣā iṃdra*) is to be used as the yājyā mantra for the Marutvatīya graha is explained by the Āśvalāyana sūtra सजोषा इंद्र सगणो मरुद्भिरिति भक्षयॆत्वैतत्पात्रं (*sajoṣā iṃdra sagaṇo marudbhiriti bhakṣayètvaitatpātraṃ*) (Ā. 5-14).

मृधः (*mṛdhaḥ*)—The word मृध (*mṛdha*) is read among the names of battle (Ni. 3-9). Its use is mostly in the sense of war. But in some contexts it is also understood as the enemy who is the cause of war. The Nirukta-kāra cites the Ṛk वि न इंद्र मृधो जहि नीचा यच्छ पृतन्यतः । (*vi na iṃdra mṛdho jahi nīcā yaccha pṛtanyataḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-152-5)

### Printed p. 96
and shows that there it has the meaning "enemies who wage war" (Ni. 7-2). In the present Ṛk too the word stands as a synonym of "enemy".

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त ऋ॒तुभि॑र्ऋतुपाः पाहि॒ सोम॒मिंद्र॑ दे॒वेभिः॒ सखि॑भिः ॒ ॑
> सुतं नः ।  
> या अभ॑जो म॒रुतो॒ ये त्वान्वह॑न्वृ॒त्रमद॑धु॒स्तुभ्य॒मोजः॑॥

> *uta ṛtubhirṛtupāḥ pāhi somamiṃdra devebhiḥ sakhibhiḥ*  
> *sutaṃ naḥ |*  
> *yā abhajo maruto ye tvānvahanvṛtramadadhustubhyamojaḥ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । ऋतुभिः । ऋतुपाः । पाहि । सोमं । इंद्र । देवेभिः । सखिभिः । सुतं ।  
> नः ।  
> यान् । आ । अभजः । मरुतः । ये । त्वा । अनु । अहन् । वृत्रं । अदधुः । तुभ्यं ।  
> ओजः ॥ ३ ॥

> *uta | ṛtubhiḥ | ṛtupāḥ | pāhi | somaṃ | iṃdra | devebhiḥ | sakhibhiḥ | sutaṃ |*  
> *naḥ |*  
> *yān | ā | abhajaḥ | marutaḥ | ye | tvā | anu | ahan | vṛtraṃ | adadhuḥ | tubhyaṃ |*  
> *ojaḥ ‖ 3 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उत अपि च हे ऋतुपाः कालनिर्वाहकतया ऋतूनां पातः । ऋतुषु
> सोमं पिबतीति वा ऋतुपाः । यद्वा ऋतुभिर्मरुद्भिः सह सोमं पिबतीति
> ऋतुपाः । तादृश हे इंद्र सखिभिः समानख्यानैर्यतुभिर्मरुद्भिर्देवेभिर्देवैः
> सहितस्त्वं नः संबंधिनं सुतमभिषुतं सोमं पाहि । पिब । किंच यान्मरुत आभजो
> युद्धसहायार्थमसेवथा ये च मरुतस्त्वा त्वां युद्धे स्वामिनमन्वभजंत ।
> ततस्त्वं वृत्रमहन् । हतवानसि । ते मरुतस्तुभ्यं त्वदर्थमोजो युद्धे शत्रुहननादिरूपं पराक्रममदधुः । अकुर्वन् ॥ अभजः । भज सेवायामित्यस्य लजि रूपं ।
> यद्वृत्तयोगादनिघातः । अहन् । हन हिंसागत्योरित्यस्य लजि सिपो हल्ड्या दिना
> लोपः । पादादित्पादनिघातः । अदधुः । दधातेर्लजि झेः सिजभ्यस्तेति जुस् ।
> वाक्यभेदादनिघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Moreover, O Ṛtupā": he who protects the seasons by regulating time; or he who drinks Soma in the seasons; or else he who drinks Soma together with the Ṛtus, that is, the Maruts. Such an Indra, being accompanied by his friends, the Maruts, who bear the same name as the Ṛtus, and by the gods, is to drink our pressed Soma. Further: the Maruts whom you resorted to for help in battle, and the Maruts who resorted to you as their lord in battle: thereupon you slew Vṛtra. Those Maruts placed in you ओजः (*ojaḥ*), vigour that takes the form of slaying enemies in battle; they produced it for you.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उत (*uta*)—and
- ऋतुसाः (*ṛtusāḥ*)—one who drinks Soma together with the Maruts (or: one who drinks Soma at the fixed times)
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- सखिभिः (*sakhibhiḥ*)—your friends

### Printed p. 97
- ऋतुभिः (*ṛtubhiḥ*)—with the Maruts
- देवेभिः (*devebhiḥ*)—and with the gods; you who are united with them
- नः (*naḥ*)—by us
- सुतं (*sutaṃ*)—pressed
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma
- पाहि (*pāhi*)—drink
- यान् (*yān*)—which Maruts
- आभजः (*ābhajaḥ*)—you have served
- ये (*ye*)—which Maruts
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- अनु (*anu*)—served (and for that very reason)
- वृत्रं (*vṛtraṃ*)—Vṛtra
- अहन् (*ahan*)—you have slain (those same Maruts)
- तुभ्यं (*tubhyaṃ*)—to you
- ओजः (*ojaḥ*)—the strength that destroys enemies
- आदधुः (*ādadhuḥ*)—produced

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, who drink Soma according to the regulation of time, together with your friends the Maruts and with the gods, drink the Soma pressed by us. Because you served the Maruts, and because the Maruts served you, you became able to slay Vṛtra; those same Maruts produced in you the strength that destroys enemies.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Drinker of the Soma in season, drink with your divine friends the Maruts, the Soma that is presented by us; those Maruts whose aid you have enjoyed (in battle), and who, following you, have given you the strength whereby you have slain Vritra.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The words ऋतुपाः (*ṛtupāḥ*) and ऋतुभिः (*ṛtubhiḥ*) must be explained according to the context. For the word ऋतु (*ṛtu*) the meaning "fixed state" or "regulation of time" is current. From the offering of oblations duly, in accordance with such a fixed ṛtu-dharma, the word ऋत्विक् (*ṛtvik*) is formed. Or, according to some, the word ऋतु (*ṛtu*) means a vessel in which oblations are offered. As indicated in "को अग्निमाट्टे हविषा धृतेन सुचा यजाता ऋतुभिर्घ्रवेभिः (*ko agnimāṭṭe haviṣā dhṛtena sucā yajātā ṛtubhirghravebhiḥ*)" (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-84-18), सुक् (*suk*), ऋतु (*ṛtu*) and ध्रुव (*dhruva*) are all three vessels for offering oblations. In places such as इंद्र सोमं पिब ऋतुना (*iṃdra somaṃ piba ṛtunā*) (1-15-1) and मरुतः पिब ऋतुना (*marutaḥ piba ṛtunā*) (1-15-2), one view is that the word ऋतु (*ṛtu*) means a vessel. But Sāyaṇa has explained ऋतु (*ṛtu*) in these contexts as the deity Ṛtu, and the Saṃhitā too shows the meaning "deity". Even in the Ṛk 1-84-18 cited above Sāyaṇa has not taken it to mean a vessel; he has given it as the seasons beginning with spring.

If the word ऋतु (*ṛtu*) is a synonym of "deity", then ऋत्विक् (*ṛtvik*) means a worshipper of the deity. In the present Ṛk, ऋतुपाः (*ṛtupāḥ*) can be explained as the Indra who drinks Soma in the seasons,

### Printed p. 98
and ऋतुभिः (*ṛtubhiḥ*) as the Indra who is united with the deities named Ṛtu. Sāyaṇa has explained them as the Marut deities. When one says ऋतुभिः देवेभिः (*ṛtubhiḥ devebhiḥ*), if ऋतुभिः (*ṛtubhiḥ*) is made an adjective of देवेभिः (*devebhiḥ*), it can also mean "with the deities named Ṛtu".

> गोऽइष्टौ ।

> *ye | tvā | ahihatye | maghavan | avardhan | ye | śāṃbare | haravaḥ | ye |*
