O Indra, famed as ancient, accept at the time of the morning pressing the purodāśa given by us. Your work is renowned as being of great nature.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra, renowned of old, accept our cakes and butter, offered at dawn;
for great are your deeds.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

प्रातःसावे (*prātaḥsāve*): सूयतेऽत्र सोम इति सावः । तस्मिन् प्रातस्सवने । (*sūyate 'tra soma iti sāvaḥ | tasmin prātassavane |*) Because in this rite the Soma juice is pressed out and prepared, it is called सावः (*sāvaḥ*) or pressing. This rite performed in the morning is called the morning pressing, that performed at midday the midday pressing, and that performed in the evening the third pressing. According to the Śruti sentence त्रीणि वाव सवनानि (*trīṇi vāva savanāni*), there are only these three pressings. Here only the morning pressing is indicated. In Ṛks 1–4 of this sūkta the morning pressing is the subject, in Ṛk 5 the midday pressing, and in Ṛk 6 the third pressing.
इंद्र क्रतुः हि ते बृहत् (*iṃdra kratuḥ hi te bṛhat*): Since Indra has performed many deeds of valour, such as the slaying of Vṛtra, he is praised here thus: O Indra, your deed of valour is very great, that is, extraordinary.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> माध्यं॑दिनस्य॒ सव॑नस्य धा॒नाः पु॑रो॒ळाश॑मिंद्र कृष्णे॒ह चारुं॑।  
> प्र यत्सो॒ता ज॑रि॒ता तूर्ण॑र्थो वृषा॒यमा॑ण॒ उप॑  
> गी॒र्भिरीट्टे॑ ॥ ५ ॥

> *mādhyaṃdinasya savanasya dhānāḥ puroḷāśamiṃdra kṛṣṇeha cāruṃ|*  
> *pra yatsotā jaritā tūrṇartho vṛṣāyamāṇa upa*  
> *gīrbhirīṭṭe ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> माध्यंदिनस्य । सवनस्य । धानाः । पुरोळाशं । इंद्र । कृष्ण । इह । चारुं ।  
> प्र । यत् । स्तोता । जरिता । तूर्णीर्थः । वृषयमाणः । उप । गीःभिः ईट्टे॥

> *mādhyaṃdinasya | savanasya | dhānāḥ | puroḷāśaṃ | iṃdra | kṛṣṇa | iha | cāruṃ |*  
> *pra | yat | stotā | jaritā | tūrṇīrthaḥ | vṛṣayamāṇaḥ | upa | gīḥbhiḥ īṭṭe‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> माध्यंदिनसवने सवनीयपुरोडाशस्य माध्यंदिनस्य सवनस्य धाना इत्यनुवाक्य । सूत्रितं च । माध्यंदिनस्य सवनस्य धाना इति माध्यंदिने । आ. ५-४ । इति ॥
> हे इंद्र माध्यंदिनस्य मध्यंदिने भवस्य सवनस्य संबंधिनीर्धाना बृष्टयवान् चारुं कमनीयं पुरोडाशं चेह कर्मण्यागत्य कृष्ण । भक्षणेन संस्कुरु ।
> यद्यदा जरिता भवत्परिचरणं कुर्वाणस्पूर्णर्थस्त्वां स्तोतुमितस्ततस्त्वरितगमनः
> अत एव वृषायमाणो वृषेवाचरन् स्तोता गीर्भिः स्तुतिलक्षणाभिर्वाग्निरुप समीपे
> प्रेट्टे प्रकर्षेण स्तौति । तदा पुरोडाशादिहवींषि भक्षयेति भावः ॥ माध्यंदिनस्य ।
> मध्यंदिनशब्दात्तत्र भवार्थ उत्सादित्वादञ् । ञ्त्वा दाम्युदात्तः । चारुं। चर गतिभक्षणयोः । दृसनिजनिचरिचटिरहिभ्यो ञ्णिणि ञुण् । णित्वादुपधावृद्धिः । ञ्त्वादाद्युदात्तः । तूर्णार्थः । तूर्तिः । ञ्त्वरा संभ्रम इत्याद्वहिश्रीत्यादिना निप्रत्ययो निच्च । अर्थः । ऋ गतौ । उषिकुषिगार्तिभ्यस्थन्निति धन् । बहुव्रीह पूर्वपदस्वरः । वृषायमाणः । वृषेवाचरतीत्यर्थे कर्तुः क्यज् सलोपश्चति क्यज् । अकृत्सार्वधातुकयोर्दिीर्घः । ञ्त्वादनुदात्तजित इत्यात्मनेपदं । लट् । शानच् ।
> तस्य लसार्वधातुकस्वरे धातुस्वरः । ईट्टे ! ईड स्तुतावित्यस्य लट रूपं । यद्योगादनिघातः ।'

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Viniyoga: the anuvākyā verse माध्यंदिनस्य सवनस्य धानाः (*mādhyaṃdinasya savanasya dhānāḥ*) is for the savanīya cake (puroḍāśa) at the midday pressing. It is prescribed in the sūtra माध्यंदिनस्य सवनस्य धाना इति माध्यंदिने (*mādhyaṃdinasya savanasya dhānā iti mādhyaṃdine*) (Ā. 5-4).

"O Indra, come to this rite and make fit by eating the roasted barley belonging to the midday pressing, that is, the pressing that takes place at midday, and the lovely, desirable cake. When the worshipper (jaritā), who serves you, hurries here and there to praise you, and therefore acts like a bull (vṛṣāyamāṇa), approaches you with hymns of praise and praises you intensely, then eat the cakes and other oblations." This is the sense.

The rest of the block is grammatical and is not summarised here (the derivations of māddhyandina, cāru, tūrṇārthaḥ, vṛṣāyamāṇaḥ and īṭṭe). He explains तूर्णर्थः (*tūrṇarthaḥ*) with तूर्तिः (*tūrtiḥ*) and इत्वरा संभ्रम (*itvarā saṃbhrama*), and says that ईट्टे (*īṭṭe*) is the present of the root ईड (*īḍa*), "to praise".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- जरिता (*jaritā*)—the praiser
- तूर्णर्थ (*tūrṇartha*)—one who hurries about in eagerness to praise you
- वृषायमाणः (*vṛṣāyamāṇaḥ*)—one who is swift like a bull
### Printed p. 163
(and intent on his duty)
- स्तोता (*stotā*)—the praiser
- गीर्भिः (*gīrbhiḥ*)—with words in the form of praise
- उप (*upa*)—(you) approaching
- प्र ईट्टे (*pra īṭṭe*)—praises intensely (then)
- माध्यंदिनस्य सवनस्य (*mādhyaṃdinasya savanasya*)—offered at the time of the midday pressing
- धानाः (*dhānāḥ*)—the barley grains
- चारुं (*cāruṃ*)—satisfying
- पुरोडाशं (*puroḍāśaṃ*)—the cake
- इह (*iha*)—in this sacrificial rite
- कृष्ण (*kṛṣṇa*)—(by your eating) make fit

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, when the praiser, who hurries about like a bull in eager haste to praise you, approaches you with words in the form of praise and praises you intensely, then eat the barley grains offered at that midday pressing and the satisfying cake in this sacrificial rite, and make them fit.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Partake, Indra, of the barley and the delicious cakes and butter of
the mid-day sacrifice, when by your zealous worshipper, hastening to adore
you, and eager as a bull, is present, and celebrates (you) with hymns.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra explains, in the sūtra माध्यंदिनस्य सवनस्य धाना इति माध्यंदिने (*mādhyaṃdinasya savanasya dhānā iti mādhyaṃdine*), that this Ṛk, माध्यंदिनस्य सवनस्य धाना (*mādhyaṃdinasya savanasya dhānā*), is to be recited as the puronuvākyā mantra in the cake-offering belonging to the midday pressing. (Ā. 5-4)

स्तोता जरिता (*stotā jaritā*)—Although these two words both indicate one meaning, "one who praises", the commentator explains a difference in meaning: for the word stotā he takes "one who praises with words in the form of praise", and for the word jaritā, भवत्परिचरणं कुर्वाणः (*bhavatparicaraṇaṃ kurvāṇaḥ*), "one who does service (śuśrūṣā) to you".

तूर्णर्थः (*tūrṇarthaḥ*)—त्वां स्तोतुमितस्ततस्वरितॆगमनः (*tvāṃ stotumitastatasvaritègamanaḥ*). The word tūrṇi, formed from the word इत्वरा संभ्रमे (*itvarā saṃbhrame*), means "running about in eagerness". Here it means the ṛtvij, or the praiser, who runs about eagerly here and there in order to praise Indra.

वृषायमाणः (*vṛṣāyamāṇaḥ*)—This word is an adjective of the word stotā. It describes the praiser who hurries about like a bull: as a bull moves swiftly of its own will, so the ṛtvij runs about eagerly; this is the comparison with the bull. While a sacrifice is performed, the praisers and the ṛtvij priests help one another and run about eagerly, heedless of any toil, in order to work with devotion
### Printed p. 164
at their own duties. To indicate this, the seer has used the words tūrṇarthaḥ and vṛṣāyamāṇaḥ and has made his intention plain.
#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तृ॒तीये॑ धा॒नाः सव॑ने पुरुष्टुत पुरो॒ळाश॒माहु॑तं मामहस्व नः ।  
> ऋ॒भु॒मंतं॒ वाज॑वंतं त्वा कवे॒ प्रय॑स्वंत॒ उप॑ शिक्षेम  ॒॑
> धीतिभिः ॥ ६ ॥

> *tṛtīye dhānāḥ savane puruṣṭuta puroḷāśamāhutaṃ māmahasva naḥ |*  
> *ṛbhumaṃtaṃ vājavaṃtaṃ tvā kave prayasvaṃta upa śikṣema*  
> *dhītibhiḥ ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तृतीये । धानाः । सवने । पुरुस्तुत । पुरोळाशं । आहुतम् । ममहस्व । नः ।  
> यभुमंतं । वाजवंतं । त्वा । कवे । प्रयस्वंतः । उप । शिक्षेम । धीतिभिः ॥ ६ ॥

> *tṛtīye | dhānāḥ | savane | purustuta | puroḷāśaṃ | āhutam | mamahasva | naḥ |*  
> *yabhumaṃtaṃ | vājavaṃtaṃ | tvā | kave | prayasvaṃtaḥ | upa | śikṣema | dhītibhiḥ ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तृतीयसवने सवनीयपुरोडाशस्य तृतीये धाना इत्यनुवाक्य । सूत्रितं
> च । तृतीये धानाः सवने पुरुष्टुतेति तृतीयसवने । आ. ५-४ । इति ॥
> हे पुरुष्टुत पुरुभिर्बहुभिः स्तोतृभिः स्तुत हे इंद्र तृतीये सवने नोऽस्मदीयान्थाना हुतान् भृष्टयवानाहुतं पुरोडाशं च मामहस्व । भक्षणेन
> महय । संभावय । हे कवे इंद्र प्रयस्वंतः संभृतहविष्का वयमृभुमंतं ।
> ऋभवो ये केचन देवास्तद्वंतं । वाजवंतं । वाजो नाम सुधन्वनः पुत्रस्तद्वंतं त्वां धीतिभिः स्तुतिभिरुप शिक्षेम । समीपे परिचरेम ॥ आहुतं: । जुहोतेः
> कर्मणि क्तः । गतिरनंतर इति गतेः स्वरः । मामहस्व । मह पूजायामित्यस्य यज्लुगंतस्य लोट व्यत्ययेनात्मनेपदं । शपो लुगभावश्चांदसः । शिक्षेम । शिक्षतेराशीर्लिजि लिज्याशिष्यज्ञ त्यज् प्रत्ययः। निघातः । धीतिभिः । हिष्ठवत्सरतिशत्थांतानामित्यंतोदात्तत्वं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Viniyoga: the anuvākyā तृतीये धानाः (*tṛtīye dhānāḥ*) is for the savanīya cake at the third pressing. It is prescribed in the sūtra तृतीये धानाः सवने पुरुष्टुतेति तृतीयसवने (*tṛtīye dhānāḥ savane puruṣṭuteti tṛtīyasavane*) (Ā. 5-4).

"O Indra, praised (purustuta) by many praisers, at the third pressing honour (māmahasva) our roasted barley, that is, the parched grains, and the cake that has been offered; honour them by eating, show them regard. O sage (kavi) Indra, we, who have gathered the oblations (prayasvantaḥ), serve you, who are accompanied by the Ṛbhus (ṛbhumantam), that is, by whatever gods are Ṛbhus, and by Vāja (vājavantam), the son of Sudhanvan, with our hymns (dhītibhiḥ), that is, praises; we attend upon you near." The remainder of the block is grammar (āhutam, māmahasva, śikṣema, dhītibhiḥ).
### Printed p. 165

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरुष्टुत (*puruṣṭuta*)—O Indra, who are praised by many
- तृतीये सवने (*tṛtīye savane*)—at the time of the third pressing
- नः (*naḥ*)—offered by us
- धानाः (*dhānāḥ*)—the barley grains
- आहुतं (*āhutaṃ*)—offered in the form of an oblation
- पुरोळाशं (*puroḷāśaṃ*)—the cake
- मामहस्व (*māmahasva*)—eat and be satisfied
- कवे (*kave*)—O Indra, of far-reaching wisdom
- प्रयस्वंतः (*prayasvaṃtaḥ*)—we who have prepared the oblation
- ऋभुमंतं (*ṛbhumaṃtaṃ*)—you who are joined with Ṛbhu
- वाजवंतं (*vājavaṃtaṃ*)—and joined with Vāja
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- धीतिभिः (*dhītibhiḥ*)—with praises
- उप शिक्षॆम (*upa śikṣèma*)—we approach and serve

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, praised by many, at the time of the third pressing eat the barley grains offered by us and the cake offered as an oblation, and be satisfied. O Indra, of far-reaching wisdom, we who have prepared the oblation approach you, who are joined with Ṛbhu and with Vāja, by means of praises, and serve you.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra, you are praised of many, accept readily from our friend of barley, and cakes, and butter, offered at the third (or evening) sacrifice : laden with sacrificial viands, we approach with praises to you, sage Indra, who are accompanied by the Ribhus and by Vaja.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra explains, in the sūtra तृतीये धानाः सवने पुरुष्टुतेति तृतीयसवने (*tṛtīye dhānāḥ savane puruṣṭuteti tṛtīyasavane*), that the Ṛk तृतीये धानाः (*tṛtīye dhānāḥ*) is to be used as the puronuvākyā mantra in the cake-offering belonging to the third pressing. (Ā. 5-4)

प्रयस्वंतः (*prayasvaṃtaḥ*)—संभृतहविष्काः (*saṃbhṛtahaviṣkāḥ*). The word prayas is read among the names of food (Ni. 3-9). Prayasvantaḥ means: "we who stand with the oblation in the form of food, prepared for offering to you, Indra, held in our hands."

ऋभुमंतं वाजवंतं (*ṛbhumaṃtaṃ vājavaṃtaṃ*)—In this Ṛk Indra is described as one who is with Vibhu **NOTE** and with one called Vāja. An explanation is necessary as to who the Ṛbhu and Vāja are.

It is said in many places in the Ṛgveda that the Ṛbhus were men in former times and attained divinity by their excellent conduct and the performance of good rites. In former times
### Printed p. 166
the seer Aṅgiras had a son named Nītimañjari. The seer Sudhanvā, born in his line, had three sons, Ṛbhu, Vibhu and Vāja. It is believed that through good works, such as performing good rites, helping the gods and making sacrificial vessels and other implements, they obtained the birth of gods, or became gods. It is the custom to call these three sons of Sudhanvā, Ṛbhu and the others, together "the Ṛbhus"; all three sons are called by the name of the eldest. Since they were pupils of Tvaṣṭṛ **NOTE**, they are also called the craftsmen of the gods. It is said here and there that they made for Indra and the other gods the implements they needed, that is, a chariot for Indra and the camasa vessel for Agni. There are descriptions here and there that they have wonderful power and are able to do any work. In Ṛ. Saṃ. sūkta 1-20 an account is given of some of the works they did. Since they have the same power and capacity as the gods, the seers regard them as belonging to the company of the gods and describe them and their deeds in many ways. A detailed account of the Ṛbhus is given in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 5, pp. 658-662; the derivation of the form of the word Ṛbhu, Part 5-156 and 8-687; the description of how the Ṛbhus received the hospitality of Āditya and became worthy of drinking soma in the sacrifice, Part 8-666; the matter of the Ṛbhus making the camasa and other sacrificial vessels, Part 8-671; the matter of the Ṛbhus protecting the calf that had no mother, Part 8-693; Indra's companionship with the Ṛbhus, Part 8-699; and an account of the Ṛbhus' artistic skill, Part 8-704. All of this is explained in detail on those pages.
#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पू॒ष॒ण्वते॑ ते चक्रमा करं॒भं हरि॑वते॒ हर्य॑श्वाय धा॒नाः ।  
> अ॒पू॒पम॑द्धि॒ सग॑णो म॒रुद्भिः॒ सोमं॑ पिब वृत्र॒हा शू॑र  ॒
> विद्वान् ॥ ७ ॥

> *pūṣaṇvate te cakramā karaṃbhaṃ harivate haryaśvāya dhānāḥ |*  
> *apūpamaddhi sagaṇo marudbhiḥ somaṃ piba vṛtrahā śūra*  
> *vidvān ‖ 7 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पूषण्वते । ते । चक्रम । करंभं । हरिऽवते । हरिऽअश्वाय । धानाः ।  
> अपूपं । अद्धि । सगणः । मरुत्भिः । सोमं । पिब । वृत्रहा । शूर । विद्वान् ॥७॥

> *pūṣaṇvate | te | cakrama | karaṃbhaṃ | hari 'vate | hari 'aśvāya | dhānāḥ |*  
> *apūpaṃ | addhi | sagaṇaḥ | marutbhiḥ | somaṃ | piba | vṛtrahā | śūra | vidvān ‖7‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र पूषण्वते । पूषा नाम कश्चिद्देवोदंतकः । तद्वते ते तुभ्यं
> करंभं दधिमिश्रं सक्तुं चक्रम । भक्षणार्थं कुर्मः । तथा च श्रुतिः । पूषा प्रसिष्ट-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, you are Pūṣaṇvat. Pūṣan is a certain toothless god; to you, who are with him, we have made karambha, barley meal mixed with curds, for eating." A śruti confirms this: Pūṣan's share is a pounded one, for he is toothless (Tai. Saṃ. 2-6-8).
### Printed p. 167

> भागोऽदंतको हि । तै सं, २-६-८ । इति । हरिवते । हरिनामकावश्वा । तद्वते
> पर्यश्चाय हरितवर्णाश्वयुक्तायेंद्राय तुभ्यं धाना भक्षणार्थं कुर्मः । तथा
> च मंत्रवर्णः । ग्रसेतामश्वा वि मुचेह शोणा दिवेदिवे सदृशीरद्दि धानाः । ऋग्वे.
> ३-३५ ३ । इति । मरुद्भिः सगणस्त्वमपूपं पुरोडाशमद्दि । भक्षय । हे शूर असहायॆ
> हे इंद्र वृत्रहा वृत्रस्य हंता विद्वान् सर्वं जानानस्त्वं सोमं पिब ॥ पूषण्वते ।
> पूषशब्दस्य मतुप् । अनो नुडिति नुडागमः । रषाभ्यामिति णत्वं । चक्रम । करोतेश्चांदसे वर्तमाने लिटि क्रादित्वादिदभावः । निघातः । हरिवते । छंदसीर इति मतुपो
> वत्वं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Harivat: having horses called Hari; to you, Indra, who are Haryaśva, joined with horses of tawny colour, we have made roasted barley for eating." The mantra text says so too: ग्रसेतामश्वा वि मुचेह शोणा दिवेदिवे सदृशीरद्दि धानाः । (*grasetāmaśvā vi muceha śoṇā divedive sadṛśīraddi dhānāḥ |*) (Ṛgve. 3-35 3 **NOTE**). "You, accompanied by the Maruts, eat the apūpa, the cake. O hero, that is, unaided, O Indra, slayer of Vṛtra, knowing all, drink the soma." The rest of the block is grammar (pūṣaṇvate, cakrama, harivate).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- पूषण्वते (*pūṣaṇvate*)—you who are joined with Pūṣan
- हरिवते (*harivate*)—you who have horses named Hari
- हरश्वाय (*haraśvāya*)—you who have horses of tawny colour
- ते (*te*)—for you
- धानाः (*dhānāḥ*)—the barley grains
- करंभं (*karaṃbhaṃ*)—the cake mixed with curds
- चक्रम (*cakrama*)—we have prepared
- मरुद्भिः (*marudbhiḥ*)—with the Maruts
- सगणः (*sagaṇaḥ*)—you who are in company
- अपूपं (*apūpaṃ*)—the cake
- अद्दि (*addi*)—eat
- शूर (*śūra*)—you who are valiant
- वृत्रहा (*vṛtrahā*)—slayer of Vṛtra
- विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—omniscient
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the soma
- पिब (*piba*)—drink

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, for you, who are joined with Pūṣan, who have horses named Hari, who have horses of tawny colour, we have prepared the barley grains and the cake mixed with curds. You, who are in company with the Maruts, eat the cake. You, who are valiant, the slayer of Vṛtra, omniscient, drink the soma.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
We have prepared the parched grain and curds for you, associated with Pushan ; the fried barley for you, lord of the tawny horses, associated with your steeds : attended by the troop of Maruts, eat the cakes; hero, you are wise, the slayer of Vritra, drink the libation.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

पूषण्वते (*pūṣaṇvate*)—पूषानाम कश्चिद्देवोऽदंतकः । तद्वते (*pūṣānāma kaściddevo 'daṃtakaḥ | tadvate*). Pūṣan is a certain toothless god; the commentator explains it here as "to Indra, who is with him". Pūṣan is an important deity in the Ṛgveda; there are eight sūktas in his praise in the Ṛgveda. Besides this, in Ṛ. Saṃ. sūkta 6-57 with Indra, and in Ṛ. Saṃ. sūkta 2-40

### Printed p. 168
…in the hymn he is praised together with Soma. His chief functions are to show the way to those who have lost it, to recover lost things, and to bring strayed cattle safely home. There are descriptions such as: "Scatter the enemies; may our path lead us to the things we desire" (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-53-4); "Make the road good, and may the road reach a place of good pasture" (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-42-7); "May he remove the troubles that may arise on the way and make the path auspicious" (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-54-9; 10-59-7); "He is the protector of every path" (6-49-8). Therefore everyone setting out on a journey should worship Pūṣan, the builder of roads, reciting Ṛ. Saṃ. Sūkta 6-53, and all who have lost their way should take refuge in Pūṣan alone; this is stated in the Āśvalāyana Gṛhya Sūtra (Ā. Gṛ. Sū. 3-6-8, 9) and in the Sāṃkhyāyana Śrauta Sūtra (Sāṃ. Śrau. Sū. 3-4-9).
For a detailed account of the deity Pūṣan see Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 5, pp. 547-550.

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रति॑ धा॒ना भ॑रत॒ तूय॑मस्मै पुरो॒ळाशं॑ वी॒रत॑माय नृ॒णां।  
> दि॒वेदि॑वे स॒दृशी॑रिंद्र॒ तुभ्यं॒ वर्धं॑तु त्वा सोम॒पेया॑य  
> धृष्टो ॥ ८ ॥

> *prati dhānā bharata tūyamasmai puroḷāśaṃ vīratamāya nṛṇāṃ|*  
> *divedive sadṛśīriṃdra tubhyaṃ vardhaṃtu tvā somapeyāya*  
> *dhṛṣṭo ‖ 8 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रति । धानाः । भरत । तूयम् । अस्मै । पुरोळाशं । वीरतमाय । नृणां ।  
> दिवेऽदिवे । सद्यशीः । इंद्र । तुभ्यं । वर्धंतु । त्वा । सोमपेयाय । धृष्णोइति ॥ ८ ॥

> *prati | dhānāḥ | bharata | tūyam | asmai | puroḷāśaṃ | vīratamāya | nṛṇāṃ |*  
> *dive 'dive | sadyaśīḥ | iṃdra | tubhyaṃ | vardhaṃtu | tvā | somapeyāya | dhṛṣṇoiti ‖ 8 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यजमानोऽध्वय्यादीन्प्रति ब्रूते । हे अध्वय्यादयः अस्मा इंद्राय
> धानास्तूयं क्षिप्रं प्रति भरत । संपादयत । नृणां वीरतमायास्मा इंद्राय
> पुरोडाशं च संपादयत । अथ प्रत्यक्षकृतः । हे धृष्टो शत्रूणामभिभवनशील हे
> इंद्र सदृशीगेकविधाः स्तुतयस्तुभ्यं त्वदर्थं दिवे दिवे प्रतिदिवसमस्माभिः क्रियंते ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sacrificer addresses the Adhvaryu and the other priests: "Priests, quickly bring fried barley (धानाः (*dhānāḥ*)) for this Indra, and prepare the cake (पुरोडाशं (*puroḍāśaṃ*)) for Indra, the most heroic of men." Then, in the direct address of the second half: "O bold one, who overcomes enemies, Indra, praises of one like kind are made to you, for your sake, day by day, by us."

### Printed p. 169

> ताः स्तुतयः सोमपेयाय सोमपानार्थं त्वां वर्धंतु ! उत्साहेन प्रवृद्धं
> कुर्वंतु ॥ सदृशीः । समानान्ययोश्चेति कञ्त्ययः । टिड्डाणइत्यादिना जीप्
> दृगृशवतुष्टिति समानस्य सभावः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"May those praises make you grow, for the drinking of Soma; may they make you increase with zeal." (The grammatical remainder on सदृशीः (*sadṛśīḥ*) is left to the Grammar.)

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्मै (*asmai*)—addressing Indra
- धानाः (*dhānāḥ*)—barley grains
- तूयं (*tūyaṃ*)—quickly
- प्रति भरत (*prati bharata*)—procure (O Adhvaryus)
- नृणां (*nṛṇāṃ*)—among all leaders
- वीरतमाय (*vīratamāya*)—for you, who are exceedingly mighty
- पुरोडाशं (*puroḍāśaṃ*)—procure the cake
- धृष्टो (*dhṛṣṭo*)—capable of defeating enemies
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- सदृशीः (*sadṛśīḥ*)—praises of one kind
- तुभ्यं (*tubhyaṃ*)—for your praise
- दिवेदिवे (*divedive*)—are made every day (those same praises)
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- सोमपेयाय (*somapeyāya*)—for the drinking of Soma
- वर्धंतु (*vardhaṃtu*)—may they make (you) grow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Adhvaryus, quickly procure barley for this Indra. Procure the cake for Indra, who is exceedingly heroic among leaders. O Indra, capable of defeating enemies, praises of equal beauty are made for you every day. May those praises make you grow for the drinking of Soma.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Offer to him quickly (priests) the fried barley : offer to the most heroic of leaders the cakes and butter : may the like offerings, victorious Indra, presented to you daily, augment your vigour for the drinking of the Soma juice.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The bhāṣya explains that the first half of this Ṛk is a description in the indirect manner (parokṣakṛta) and the second half a description in the direct manner (pratyakṣakṛta). We explain here in detail what parokṣakṛta and pratyakṣakṛta mean. On this subject Yāska says in his Nirukta:
तद्यानि नामानि प्राधान्यस्तु तीनां देवतानां तद्वैवतमित्याचक्षते । यत्काम ऋषिर्यस्यां देवतायामार्थपत्यमिच्छन् स्तुतिं प्रयुंक्ते तद्वैवतः स मंत्रो भवति । शास्त्रिविधा ऋचः परोक्षकृताः प्रत्यक्षकृता आध्यात्मिकाश्च । तत्रपरोक्षकृताः सर्वाभिर्नामविभक्तिभिर्युज्यंते प्रथमपुरुषैश्चाख्यातस्य । अथ प्रत्यक्षकृता मध्यमपुरु षोपयोगास्त्वमिति चैतेन सर्वनाम्ना । अथाध्यात्मिक्य उत्तमपुरुषयोगा अहमिति चैतेन सर्वनाम्ना । परोक्षकृताः प्रत्यक्षकृताश्च मंत्रा भूयिष्ठा अल्पश आध्यात्मिकाः ॥ (*tadyāni nāmāni prādhānyastu tīnāṃ devatānāṃ tadvaivatamityācakṣate | yatkāma ṛṣiryasyāṃ devatāyāmārthapatyamicchan stutiṃ prayuṃkte tadvaivataḥ sa maṃtro bhavati | śāstrividhā ṛcaḥ parokṣakṛtāḥ pratyakṣakṛtā ādhyātmikāśca | tatraparokṣakṛtāḥ sarvābhirnāmavibhaktibhiryujyaṃte prathamapuruṣaiścākhyātasya | atha pratyakṣakṛtā madhyamapuru ṣopayogāstvamiti caitena sarvanāmnā | athādhyātmikya uttamapuruṣayogā ahamiti caitena sarvanāmnā | parokṣakṛtāḥ pratyakṣakṛtāśca maṃtrā bhūyiṣṭhā alpaśa ādhyātmikāḥ ‖*) (Ni. 7-1 to 3)
That is, among mantras there are many kinds: mantras of the deity, praise-mantras, benedictions, oaths, curses, a statement of some matter, lament, censure and praise. A deity-mantra describes the greatness of deities; a praise is a mantra containing hymns and prayers; a benediction is a blessing, a wish for good; an oath is a vow; a curse is an evil wish; a statement of some matter is the explanation of a subject or principle; lament is grief and distress; censure is blame; praise is eulogy, praise of gifts and so on.
Among the many kinds mentioned, the Ṛks of the deity are of three kinds: parokṣakṛta, pratyakṣakṛta and ādhyātmika. Parokṣakṛta: because the deity praised is not present, mantras that use third-person nouns and verbs (third person both in subject and predicate) about that deity. Pratyakṣakṛta: Ṛks that imagine the deity praised as present and praise it by addressing it with second-person words such as "you", "your". Ādhyātmika: mantras that praise by using the first person. Of these three kinds, the parokṣakṛta and pratyakṣakṛta mantras are the most numerous; the ādhyātmika mantras are very few.

## Sūkta 53 (Maṇḍala 3)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रापर्वतेति चतुर्विंशत्यृचं पंचदशं सूक्तं । अत्रेयमनुक्रमणिका ।
> इंद्रापर्वता चतुर्विंशतिराद्यैंद्रापार्वती पंचदश्यादिद्दे वाचे ससर्पर्यॆ
> चतस्रो रथांगस्तुतयोऽंत्या अभिशापास्ता वसिष्ठद्वेषिण्यो न वसिष्ठा शृण्वंति ।
> दशमी षोडशौ जगत्त्रयोदशी गायत्रि द्वादशीविंशीद्वाविंश्योऽनुष्टु
> भोऽष्टादशी बृहतीति । विश्वामित्र ऋषिः । दशमी षोडशी च द्वे जगत्त्रयोदरी
> गायत्रि द्वादशीविंशीद्वाविंश्यस्तिस्रोऽनुष्टु भोऽष्टादशी बृहती शिष्टास्त्रिष्टु भः ।
> आद्येंद्रापर्वतदेवताका पंचदशीषोडशौ वाग्गेवत्ये तॆदुत्तराश्चतस्रो रथांग
> देवताकाः शिष्टा ऐंद्रैः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The hymn beginning इंद्रापर्वता (*iṃdrāparvatā*) has twenty-four Ṛks and is the fifteenth sūkta of the group. Sāyaṇa cites the Anukramaṇikā: the first Ṛk is Aindrāpārvatī; the fifteenth and sixteenth are addressed to Vāc, Sasarparī; four (17-20) praise the parts of a chariot; the last ones are curses against the enemies of Vasiṣṭha, which the Vasiṣṭhas do not hear. It gives the metres: the tenth and sixteenth Jagatī; the thirteenth Gāyatrī; the twelfth, twentieth and twenty-second Anuṣṭubh; the eighteenth Bṛhatī; the rest Triṣṭubh. The seer is Viśvāmitra. The first has Indra and Parvata as deity, the fifteenth and sixteenth Vāc, the next four the chariot parts, the rest Indra.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
(Anuvāda in Kannada, not English.) The Kannada Anuvāda renders the above: Viśvāmitra is the seer of this hymn of twenty-four Ṛks. The first Ṛk has Indra-Parvata as deity; Ṛks 15 and 16 Vāc; Ṛks 17-20, beginning ससर्परीः (*sasarparīḥ*), the chariot parts; Ṛks 2-14 and 21-24 Indra. The last four (21-24), though addressed to Indra, express curses (the matter of abhiśāpa Ṛks was explained on p. 150). Because these four last Ṛks revile Vasiṣṭha, his descendants and followers, those born in the Vasiṣṭha gotra neither recite nor hear them. Ṛks 10 and 16 are Jagatī; 13 Gāyatrī; 12, 20 and 22 Anuṣṭubh; 18 Bṛhatī; the rest (1-9, 11, 14, 15, 17, 19, 21, 23, 24) Triṣṭubh. **NOTE**

Sūkta 53. Maṇḍala 3; Anuvāka 4; Sūkta 53; Aṣṭaka 3; Adhyāya 3; Vargas 19, 20, 21, 22, 23; number of Ṛks 24. Seer: Viśvāmitra. Deity: 1 Indra-Parvata; 2-14, 21-24 Indra; 15-16 Vāc; 17-20 parts of the chariot. Metre: 1-9, 11, 14, 15, 17, 19, 21, 23, 24 Triṣṭubh; 10, 16 Jagatī; 12, 20, 22 Anuṣṭubh; 13 Gāyatrī; 18 Bṛhatī.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्रा॑पर्वता बृह॒ता रथे॑न वा॒मीरिष॒ आ व॑हतं सु॒वीराः॑ ।  
> वी॒तं ह॒व्यान्य॑ध्व॒रेषु॑ देवा॒ वर्धे॑थां गी॒र्भिरिळ॑या॒ मदं॑ता॥

> *iṃdrāparvatā bṛhatā rathena vāmīriṣa ā vahataṃ suvīrāḥ |*  
> *vītaṃ havyānyadhvareṣu devā vardhethāṃ gīrbhiriḷayā madaṃtā‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रापर्वता । बृहता । रथेन । वामीः । इषः । आ । वहतं । सुवीराः ।  
> वीतं । हव्यानि । अध्वरेषु । देवा । वर्धेथां । गीःभिः । इळया । मदंता ॥ १ ॥

> *iṃdrāparvatā | bṛhatā | rathena | vāmīḥ | iṣaḥ | ā | vahataṃ | suvīrāḥ |*  
> *vītaṃ | havyāni | adhvareṣu | devā | vardhethāṃ | gīḥbhiḥ | iḷayā | madaṃtā ‖ 1 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रापर्वता इंद्रश्च पर्वतश्च । हे इंद्रापर्वता बृहता रथेनागत्य वामिार्वननीयाः सुवीराः शोभनपुत्रोपेता इषोऽन्नान्या वहतं । अस्मदर्थ० धारयतं! प्रयच्छतमित्यर्थः। किंच हे देवौ द्योतमानावध्वरेष्वस्मत्संबंधिषु यज्ञेषु हव्यानि हवनयोग्यानि पुरोडाशादीनि हवींषि वीतं। भक्षयतं। तथेळयास्माभिर्दत्तेन हविषा मदंता हृष्यंतौ युवां गीर्भिः स्तुतिलक्षणाभिरस्मदीयाभिर्वाग्भिर्वर्धेथां । समृद्धौ भवतं ॥ इंद्रापर्वता । आमंत्रितस्य पादादित्वात्पाष्ठिकमाद्युदात्तत्वं । सुवीराः । बहुव्रीहौ वीरवीयौ चेत्युत्तरपदाद्युदात्तत्वं । वीतं । वी
> कांत्यादिष्ठित्यस्य लोट्यदादित्वाच्छपो लुक् । प्रत्ययस्वरः । वर्धेथां । वृधु वृद्धावित्यस्य लोट्याथामो लसार्वधातुकस्वरे धातुस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

इंद्रापर्वता (*iṃdrāparvatā*) means Indra and Parvata. "O Indra and Parvata, coming in a great chariot, bring desirable food (इषः (*iṣaḥ*)) accompanied by excellent sons; hold it for us, that is, give it. Further, O shining deities, partake of the oblations such as cakes offered at our sacrifices. Gladdened by the offering given by us, may you grow, be prosperous, by our praises of the nature of hymns."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रापर्वता (*iṃdrāparvatā*)—O Indra and Parvata
- बृहता (*bṛhatā*)—of great form
- रथेन (*rathena*)—in (your) chariot
- वामीः (*vāmīḥ*)—praiseworthy
- सुवीराः (*suvīrāḥ*)—accompanied by excellent sons
- इषः (*iṣaḥ*)—food
- आ वहतं (*ā vahataṃ*)—bring
- देवा (*devā*)—you shining ones
- अध्वरेषु (*adhvareṣu*)—in the sacrifices
- हव्यानि (*havyāni*)—cakes and such, fit for worship
- वीतं (*vītaṃ*)—partake
- इळया (*iḷayā*)—with the oblation
- मदंता (*madaṃtā*)—you who are gratified
- गीर्भिः (*gīrbhiḥ*)—by our words in the form of praise
- वर्धेथां (*vardhethāṃ*)—obtain prosperity

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Parvata, bring in your great chariot food that is praiseworthy and accompanied by excellent sons. Shining ones, partake in the sacrifices of the cakes and other oblations fit for worship.

### Printed p. 173
Gratified by the oblation, obtain prosperity through our words of praise.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra and Parvata, bring hither, in a spacious car, delightful viands (generative of) good progeny : partake, deities, of the oblations (offered) at (our) sacrifices, and, gratified by the (sacrificial) food, be elevated by our praises.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

इंद्रापर्वता (*iṃdrāparvatā*)—by this word Indra and a cloud, or the deity presiding over clouds, that is Parjanya, are addressed. The word occurs in the Ṛgveda only in this Ṛk and in Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-122-3 and 1-132-6, nowhere else. In explaining the word, the bhāṣyakāra says:
इंद्रापर्वता । इंद्रः प्रसिद्धः । पर्वतः पर्ववान् वृष्ट्यादिपूरणवान् पर्जन्यः ॥ (*iṃdrāparvatā | iṃdraḥ prasiddhaḥ | parvataḥ parvavān vṛṣṭyādipūraṇavān parjanyaḥ ‖*)
That is, Indra is the well-known deity; Parvata is the deity Parjanya, presiding over rain, who fills the earth with rain and the like. Bhaṭṭa Bhāskara says that the word parvata also means year (पर्वतःसंवत्सरः (*parvataḥsaṃvatsaraḥ*)). Whatever meaning is given to parvata, it is clear that here the word is a synonym for a deity to be worshipped. The word is also used as a synonym of enemy: since parvata is listed among the names of clouds (Ni. 2-21), in many places it is said that Indra caused rain by splitting the parvata; therefore an inquiry into the use of this word is proper.
The word parvata is used most in hymns to Indra and the Maruts. Its common meaning is mountain, but it is customary as a synonym of cloud. The Nirukta says: पर्ववान् पर्वतः । पर्व पुनः पृणातेः प्रीणातीर्वा मेघोऽपि गिरिरेतस्मादेव ! (*parvavān parvataḥ | parva punaḥ pṛṇāteḥ prīṇātīrvā megho 'pi giriretasmādeva !*) (Ni. 1-20). Showing that the word parva derives either from the root pṛ "to fill" or from the root prī "to worship, to satisfy", it explains parvata as mountain or cloud. The etymology gives the sense of filling a barren region, or of satisfying beings (with rain and the like). One must notice which parvata is the obstructing one and which the beneficial one.

### Printed p. 174
In general, Indra's fight with the Asuras and with the mountains may be called a fight between darkness and light.
In the Ṛk वि चिद्वृतस्य दोधतो वज्रेण शतपर्वणा । शिरो बिभेद वृष्टिना ॥ (*vi cidvṛtasya dodhato vajreṇa śataparvaṇā | śiro bibheda vṛṣṭinā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-6-6), the sense is that with the thunderbolt whose joints (parvas) are a hundred in number and are of the nature of rays, he destroyed the Asura who is of the nature of darkness and who is a coverer. From such examples it can be said that, just as words like Vṛtra show the embodied form of the darkness that covers the world, so the word *parvata* also denotes darkness in the form of a mountain. Moreover, if one simply gives the ordinary meaning "hill" or "cloud", it does not yield the full sense that suits the contexts. For four kinds of achievement are described: releasing the cows and the waters that had been hidden away by the slaying of the *parvata*, and causing Uṣas and Sūrya to become manifest. Therefore, if the word *parvata* is explained only as "cloud", it does not suit the contexts.
In the Ṛk आदर्दरुत्समसृजो वि खानि त्वमर्णवान्बद्बधाना अरम्णाः । महांतमिंद्र पर्वतं वि यद्वः सृजो वि धारा अव दानवं हन् । (*ādardarutsamasṛjo vi khāni tvamarṇavānbadbadhānā aramṇāḥ | mahāṃtamiṃdra parvataṃ vi yadvaḥ sṛjo vi dhārā ava dānavaṃ han |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-32-1), and in the Brāhmaṇa sentence शिशुं नदीनां हरिमद्रिबुध्नमिति गिरिर्वा अद्रिर्गिरिबुध्ना ॥ (*śiśuṃ nadīnāṃ harimadribudhnamiti girirvā adrirgiribudhnā ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 7-5-2-18), it is understood that the words *parvata* and *megha* have an exalted, supernatural meaning. This is described as a mass of darkness in the form of a mountain, situated in the heavenly world, which hides the waters and the cow. Having split this darkness, Indra and the Maruts cause the water and the light to spread.
In Ṛks such as अहन्नहिं पर्वते शिश्रियाणं त्वष्टास्मै वज्रं स्वर्यं ततक्ष । (*ahannahiṃ parvate śiśriyāṇaṃ tvaṣṭāsmai vajraṃ svaryaṃ tatakṣa |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-32-2) it is said that Indra, who destroys Asuras such as Ahi, Śañjara **NOTE** and Vṛtra, who hide in this mass of darkness in the form of a mountain and obstruct the flowing water, is worthy of praise. In the same way, in many places the word *parvata* is described as a synonym of the enemy or as the enemies' refuge. Therefore in Ṛks such as आरंस्त पर्वतश्चित्सरिष्यन् (*āraṃsta parvataścitsariṣyan*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-11-7) and शुष्माचिदस्य पर्वता भयंते (*śuṣmācidasya parvatā bhayaṃte*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-12-13), it is said that as soon as Indra, who is of the nature of light, appeared, the masses of darkness in the form of mountains trembled with fear, and that Indra hid them away in the nether world—

### Printed p. 175
Contrary to these descriptions, in many places it is also said that the *parvata* is the thunderbolt, which is of the nature of light, and Parjanya, the presiding deity of rain, water and the like.
In Ṛks such as प्राक्तादपाक्तादधरादुदक्तादभि जहि रक्षसः पर्वतेन (*prāktādapāktādadharādudaktādabhi jahi rakṣasaḥ parvatena*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-104-19) and अस्मे रुद्रा मेहना पर्वतासो वृत्रहत्ये भरहूता सजोषाः । (*asme rudrā mehanā parvatāso vṛtrahatye bharahūtā sajoṣāḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-63-12), the mountains are instruments that help Indra in the work of destroying enemies.
In Ṛks such as प्र पूषणं विष्णुमग्निं पुरंधिं सवितारमोषधीः पर्वतांश्च । (*pra pūṣaṇaṃ viṣṇumagniṃ puraṃdhiṃ savitāramoṣadhīḥ parvatāṃśca |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-21-9) and शं नः पर्वता ध्रुवयो भवंतु शं नः सिंधवः शमु सन्त्वापः । (*śaṃ naḥ parvatā dhruvayo bhavaṃtu śaṃ naḥ siṃdhavaḥ śamu santvāpaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-25-8), the *parvata* is invoked together with deities such as Mitra, Varuṇa and Pūṣan.
In the places युवं तमिंद्रापर्वता पुरोयधा यो नः पृतन्यादप तंतमुद्धतं वज्रेण तंतमुद्धतं । (*yuvaṃ tamiṃdrāparvatā puroyadhā yo naḥ pṛtanyādapa taṃtamuddhataṃ vajreṇa taṃtamuddhataṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-132-6) and इंद्रापर्वता बृहता रथेन वामीरिष आ वहतं सुवीराः । (*iṃdrāparvatā bṛhatā rathena vāmīriṣa ā vahataṃ suvīrāḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-53-1), and in the present Ṛk as well, Indra and the *parvata* are invoked and praised together. Sāyaṇa has said that here *parvata* means Parjanya. Here the *parvata* is a deity to be worshipped **NOTE**
वामीः सुवीराः इषः (*vāmīḥ suvīrāḥ iṣaḥ*)—the word *iṣa* is read among the names of food (Ni. 3-9). Here the words *vāmīḥ* and *suvīrāḥ* are qualifiers of the word *iṣaḥ*. The meaning is: bring food together with sons who are to be obtained and who are heroes; that is, "bring the heroic sons and the food that may come to us through your grace."

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तिष्ठा॒ सु कं॑ मघव॒न्मा परा॑ गाः॒ सोम॑स्य॒ नु त्वा॒ सुषु॑तस्य  
> यक्षि ।  
> पि॒तुर्न पु॒त्रः सिच॒मा र॑भे त॒ इंद्र॒ स्वादि॑ष्टया गि॒रा श॑चीवः ॥

> *tiṣṭhā su kaṃ maghavanmā parā gāḥ somasya nu tvā suṣutasya*  
> *yakṣi |*  
> *piturna putraḥ sicamā rabhe ta iṃdra svādiṣṭayā girā śacīvaḥ ‖*

### Printed p. 176

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तिष्ठ । सु । कं । मघवन् । मा । परा । गाः । सोमस्य । नु । त्वा । सुसुतस्य ।  
> यक्षि ।  
> पितुः । नः । पुत्रः । सिचं । आ । रभे । ते । इंद्र । स्वादिष्टया । गिरा । शचीवः ॥ २ ॥

> *tiṣṭha | su | kaṃ | maghavan | mā | parā | gāḥ | somasya | nu | tvā | susutasya |*  
> *yakṣi |*  
> *pituḥ | naḥ | putraḥ | sicaṃ | ā | rabhe | te | iṃdra | svādiṣṭayā | girā | śacīvaḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अहर्गणेष्वंत्यवर्जितेष्वहःसु हारियोजनस्य तिष्ठा सु कमित्यनुवाक्य ।
> सूत्रितं च। तिष्ठा सु कं मघवन्मा परा गा आयं यज्ञो देवया अयं मियेधः
> । आ. ६-११ । इति ॥
> हे मघवन्मनवन्निंद्र कं सुखेनास्मिन्यज्ञे सु तिष्ठ । सुष्टु कंचित्कालमत
> तिष्ठ । मा परा गाः । अस्मदीयाद्यज्ञाना गच्छ ॥ तत्र कारणमाह । सुषुतस्य सुष्ठभिषुतेन सोमस्य सोमेन नु क्षिप्रं त्वां यक्षि । यजे । हे शचीवः शक्तिमन्निंद्र
> ते तव सिचं वस्त्रप्रांतं स्वादिष्टया स्वादुतरया स्तुतिलक्षणया गिरा आ रभे । अलभे!
> गृह्णामीत्यर्थः । तत्र दृष्टांतः । पितुर्न । यथा पुत्रो मधुरया वाचा पितुर्जनकस्य चेलांचलं गृह्णाति तद्वत् ॥ सु कमिति निपातद्वयं । प्रथमस्य निपाता आद्युदात्ता इत्याद्युदात्तत्वं । द्वितीयस्य चादयोऽनुदात्ता इत्यनुदात्तत्वं । सोमस्य
> यक्षि । यज देवपूजासंगतिकरणदानेषु । स्वरितञ्चित इत्यात्मनेपदं । तस्य ल:जि
> सिचेकाच इतीट् प्रतिषेधः । व्रश्चादिना षत्वं । षढोः कः सीति कत्वं । सिचः सकारस्य
> षत्वं । निघातः । रभे । डुलभष् प्राप्तौ । लट्युत्तम इट रूपं । रलयोरभेदः ।
> निघातः । स्वादिष्टया । स्वादुशब्दादिष्ठनि रूपं । नित्त्वादाद्युदात्तः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The anuvākya "tiṣṭhā su kam" is employed in the Hāriyojana rite on the days of the ahargaṇas other than the last. It is also given in the sūtra (Ā. 6-11), which quotes the verse together with the formula beginning "ayaṃ yajño devayā". "O Maghavan, Indra, the wealthy one, stay happily (*kam*) in this sacrifice; stay well here for some time; do not go away from our sacrifice." He gives the reason: "I worship you quickly with the well-pressed Soma." "O Śacīvan, mighty Indra, I take hold of the hem (*sica*) of your garment, the edge of your cloth, with a sweeter (*svādiṣṭha*) speech in the form of praise." The example is "as a father's...": as a son with sweet speech takes hold of the hem of the cloth of his father, the begetter, so (I take hold of yours). The rest is grammar: *su* and *kam* are two particles, the first accented on the first syllable and the second unaccented; the derivations of *yakṣi*, *rabhe* and *svādiṣṭayā* follow.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—Indra, the wealthy one
- कं (*kaṃ*)—happily
- सु तिष्ठ (*su tiṣṭha*)—stay in this very sacrificial place
- मा पर्यागाः (*mā paryāgāḥ*)—do not go elsewhere from this place (because)
- सुषुतस्य (*suṣutasya*)—well pressed
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—with Soma
- नु (*nu*)—quickly
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- यक्षि (*yakṣi*)—I worship
- शचीवः (*śacīvaḥ*)—mighty
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—Indra
- पुत्रः (*putraḥ*)—a son
- पितुः न (*pituḥ na*)—as (a son), taking hold of the hem of his father's garment, speaks to him (with sweet speech)
- ते (*te*)—your
- सिचं (*sicaṃ*)—the hem of the garment
- स्वादिष्टया (*svādiṣṭayā*)—most heart-pleasing
- गिरा (*girā*)—with speech in the form of praise
- आ रभे (*ā rabhe*)—I keep hold

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O wealthy Indra, stay happily in this very sacrificial place. I shall worship you quickly with well-pressed Soma; therefore do not leave this place and go elsewhere.

### Printed p. 177
O mighty Indra, as a son holds the hem of his father's garment while speaking sweetly, so I too hold the hem of your garment with most heart-pleasing speech, and I praise you.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Tarry awhile contentedly, Maghavan, (at our rite): go not away ; for I offer to you (the libation) of the copiously-effused Soma : powerful Indra. I lay hold of the skirts (of your robe) with sweet flavoured commen- dations, as a son (clings to the garment) of a father.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

On the day before the last day of the sacrifice called Ahargaṇa, in the rite called Hāriyojana, this Ṛk "tiṣṭhā su kam" is to be recited as the puro'nuvākyā mantra; this is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra तिष्ठा सु कं मघवन्ना परा गा अयं यज्ञो देवया वयं मियेधः (*tiṣṭhā su kaṃ maghavannā parā gā ayaṃ yajño devayā vayaṃ miyedhaḥ*) (Ā. Sū. 6-11).
शचिवः इंद्र पितुः न पुत्रः स्वादिष्टया गिरा ते ३ चं आ रभे (*śacivaḥ iṃdra pituḥ na putraḥ svādiṣṭayā girā te 3 caṃ ā rabhe*) **NOTE**—हे शक्तिमन्निंद्र तव वस्त्रप्रांतं यथा जनकस्य चेलांचलं पुत्रो मधुरया वाचा गृह्णाति तद्वत् स्वादुतरया स्तुतिलक्षणया गिरा तव वस्त्रप्रांतं आलभे । (*he śaktimanniṃdra tava vastraprāṃtaṃ yathā janakasya celāṃcalaṃ putro madhurayā vācā gṛhṇāti tadvat svādutarayā stutilakṣaṇayā girā tava vastraprāṃtaṃ ālabhe |*). Here the word *śacī* is read among the names of speech (Ni. 2-23), among the names of action (Ni. 3-1) and among the names of wisdom (Ni. 3-13). Here the commentator explains it as "strength" and interprets it as "Indra who has power". In the sentence quoted above, the seer describes with an illustration: "O Indra, as a son, when speaking with affection and respect to his father, keeps hold of the end of the garment his father wears, so I too, holding the end of your garment, will speak loving, sweet words."

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> शंसा॑वाध्वयो॒ प्रति॑ मे गृणी॒हींद्रा॑य॒ वाहः॑ कृणवाव॒  ॑
> जुष्टं ।  
> एदं ब॒र्हियर्ज॑मानस्य सी॒दाथा॑ च भूदु॒क्थमिंद्रा॑य  ॒
> शस्तं ॥ ३ ॥

> *śaṃsāvādhvayo prati me gṛṇīhīṃdrāya vāhaḥ kṛṇavāva*  
> *juṣṭaṃ |*  
> *edaṃ barhiyarjamānasya sīdāthā ca bhūdukthamiṃdrāya*  
> *śastaṃ ‖ 3 ‖*

### Printed p. 178

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शंसाव । अध्वयो इति । प्रति । मे । गृणीहि । इंद्राय । वाहः । कृणवाव । जुष्टं  
> आ । इदं । बर्हिः । यजमानस्य । सीद । अथ । च । भूत् । उक्तं । इंद्राय । शस्तं ॥

> *śaṃsāva | adhvayo iti | prati | me | gṛṇīhi | iṃdrāya | vāhaḥ | kṛṇavāva | juṣṭaṃ*  
> *ā | idaṃ | barhiḥ | yajamānasya | sīda | atha | ca | bhūt | uktaṃ | iṃdrāya | śastaṃ ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> शंसिष्यन् होताध्वर्युं ब्रूते । हे अध्वर्यो आवां शंसाव । होतस्त्वमेव शंससि । कथमहं शंसानि । आत आह । प्रति मे गृणीहीति । त्वं मे प्रतिगृणीहि ।
> ओथामोदैवेत्यादिको होतुरुत्साहजननः प्रतिगरः । आ. ७-११ । तं दत्स्व । तथेंद्राय जुष्टं प्रीतियुक्तं वाहः स्तोत्रं कृणवाव । त्वं यजमानस्येदं बर्हिरस्मिन्बर्हिष्या सीद । उपनिश । अथ चानंतरमिंद्रायेंद्रार्थमावाभ्यां क्रियमाणमुक्थं शस्त्रं शस्तं प्रशस्तं भूत् । भवतु ॥ शंसाव । शन्नु स्तुतावित्यस्य लोट्युत्तमद्विवचने रूपं । आडुत्तमस्यॆत्याडागमः। उत्तमस्य पिद्वद्भावादनुदात्तत्वे धातुस्वरः
> मे । अनुप्रतिगृणश्च । पा. १-४-४१ । इति संप्रदानसंज्ञा । जुष्टं । नित्यं मंत्र
> इत्याद्युदात्तत्वं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The hotā, about to recite, says to the adhvaryu: "O adhvaryu, let us two recite." The adhvaryu says: "Hotā, you alone recite; how shall I recite?" Thereupon the hotā says "prati me gṛṇīhi": "You respond to me." The pratigara that begins with "oṭhāmodaiva" and encourages the hotā is quoted from the sūtra (Ā. 7-11): "give it." Likewise, "let us two make a pleasing (*juṣṭa*), loving *vāhaḥ*, that is, a praise-hymn, for Indra. Sit on this sacred grass of the yajamāna; settle down here. And after that let the uktha, the śastra performed by us two for Indra, be *śasta*, that is, excellent." The remainder is grammar (the derivation of *śaṃsāva*, the designation *sampradāna* for *me* by Pā. 1-4-41, and the accent of *juṣṭam*).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अध्वर्यो (*adhvaryo*)—O adhvaryu
- शंसाव (*śaṃsāva*)—let us two praise (Indra)
- प्रति मे गृणीहि (*prati me gṛṇīhi*)—you concur with me
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—addressing Indra
- जुष्टं (*juṣṭaṃ*)—affectionate
- वाहः (*vāhaḥ*)—a hymn of praise
- कृणवाव (*kṛṇavāva*)—let us two compose
- यजमानस्य (*yajamānasya*)—of the yajamāna
- इदं बर्हिः (*idaṃ barhiḥ*)—on this seat of darbha grass
- आ सीद (*ā sīda*)—sit down
- अथ च (*atha ca*)—and after that
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—made for Indra
- उक्तं (*uktaṃ*)—the recitation of the śastra mantras
- शस्त्रं भूत् (*śastraṃ bhūt*)—may it be excellent

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O adhvaryu, let us two praise Indra. You concur with me. Let us compose an affectionate hymn of praise addressed to Indra. Sit on this darbha seat of the yajamāna. After that, may the recitation of the śastra mantras made for Indra be excellent.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Adhwaryu, let us two offer praise : do you concur with me : let us address pleasing praise to Indra : sit down, Indra, on the sacred grass (prepared by) the institutor of the rite : and may our commendations be most acceptable to Indra.

### Printed p. 179

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the hotā addresses the priest called adhvaryu, and the seer describes him as saying: "Let us two, sitting on the darbha seat in this sacrifice performed by the yajamāna, praise Indra with excellent śastra mantras."

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> जा॒येदस्तं॑ मघव॒न्तेदु॒ योनि॒स्तदित्वा॑ यु॒क्ता हर॑यो  
> वहंतु ।  
> य॒दा क॒दा च॑ सु॒नवा॑म॒ सोम॑म॒ग्निष्ट्वा॑ दू॒तो ध॑न्वा॒तृच्छ॑ ॥

> *jāyedastaṃ maghavantedu yonistaditvā yuktā harayo*  
> *vahaṃtu |*  
> *yadā kadā ca sunavāma somamagniṣṭvā dūto dhanvātṛccha ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> जाया । इत् । अस्तं । मघवन् । सा । इत् । ऊं इति । योनिः । तत् । इत् । त्वा ।  
> युक्ताः । हरयः । वहंतु ।  
> यदा । कदा । च । सुनवाम । सोमं । अग्निः । त्वा । दूतः । धन्वाति । अच्छ ॥ ४ ॥

> *jāyā | it | astaṃ | maghavan | sā | it | ūṃ iti | yoniḥ | tat | it | tvā |*  
> *yuktāḥ | harayaḥ | vahaṃtu |*  
> *yadā | kadā | ca | sunavāma | somaṃ | agniḥ | tvā | dūtaḥ | dhanvāti | accha ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मघवन्दनवन्निंद्र अस्तं । अस्यंते क्षिप्यंते पदार्था अत्रेत्यस्तं गृहं ।
> जायेज्जाय्तैव गृहं भवति । न गृहं गृहमित्याहुर्गृहिणी गृहमुच्यत इति
> स्मृतेः । तथा सेत् सा जायैव योनिः पुरुषस्य मिश्रणस्थानं । उ प्रसिद्ध्यर्थः ।
> तस्मात्तदित् तत्र गृह एव युक्ता रथे योजिता हरयोऽश्वास्त्वा त्वां वहंतु ।
> वयं तु यदा कदा च त्वदर्थं सोमं सुनवाम अभिषुतं करवाम तदास्माभिः
> प्रहितो दूतोऽग्निस्ता त्वामच्छाभिमुख्येन धन्वाति । गच्छेत् ॥ अस्तं। असु क्षेपणे ।
> असिहसिमृग्रिण्वामित्यादिना तन्नत्ययः। नित्वादाद्युदात्तः । सुनवाम । षुञ् अभिषव
> इत्यस्य लोटि रूपं । यद्वृत्तयोगादनिघातः । त्वा । युष्मत्तत्ततक्षुःस्वंतःपादमिति
> संहितायां षत्वं । धन्वाति । धवि गत्यर्थः । लेट्याडागमे रूपं । निघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Maghavan, wealthy Indra: *astam* is the house, because things are thrown (*asyante*) and placed there. Only the wife (*jāyā*) is the house," as the smṛti says: न गृहं गृहमित्याहुर्गृहिणी गृहमुच्यत (*na gṛhaṃ gṛhamityāhurgṛhiṇī gṛhamucyata*). Likewise, "she, indeed, is the *yoni* of a man, his place of mixing (*miśraṇasthāna*)"; *u* has the sense of "well known". "Therefore, in that very house, let the bay horses (*harayaḥ*) yoked to the chariot carry you. And whenever we press Soma for you, then Agni, the messenger sent by us, goes (*dhanvāti*) toward you, facing you." The remainder is grammar (the derivations of *astam*, *sunavāma*, *tvā* and *dhanvāti*).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O wealthy Indra
- जायेत् (*jāyet*)—the wife herself
- अस्तं (*astaṃ*)—is the house (for a man)
- सेत् (*set*)—she herself
- योनिः (*yoniḥ*)—the place of origin (with him)
- तदित् (*tadit*)—that very (sacrifice)

### Printed p. 180
- गृहक्कॆ (*gṛhakkè*)—to the house
- युक्ताः (*yuktāḥ*)—yoked (to your) chariot
- हरयः (*harayaḥ*)—horses
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- वहंतु (*vahaṃtu*)—let them bring
- यदा कदा च (*yadā kadā ca*)—in whatever circumstances
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—Soma
- सुनवाम (*sunavāma*)—we prepare (for you), (at those times)
- दूतः (*dūtaḥ*)—the divine messenger
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- त्वा (*tvā*)—to you
- अच्छ (*accha*)—facing
- धन्वति (*dhanvati*)—let him come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O wealthy Indra, for a man the wife is the house. She herself is the place of origin. To that very sacrificial house, which for you is as a wife, let the horses yoked to your chariot bring you. Whenever we prepare Soma for you, at all those times let Agni, the divine messenger, come facing you.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
A man's wife, Maghavan, is his dwelling ; verily she is his place of birth : thither let your horses, harnessed (to your car), convey you : we pre- pare the soma juice at the fit season : may Agni come as our messenger before you.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

अस्तं (*astaṃ*)—अस्यंते क्षिप्यंते पदार्था अत्रेत्यस्तं गृहं । पदार्थगळु याव स्थळगळल्लि इडल्पडुत्तवॆयो आ स्थळक्कॆ गृहवु ऎंदरॆ अस्तं ऎंब शब्दक्कॆ मनॆयॆंदु भाष्यकाररु अर्थविवरणॆ माडिद्दारॆ. जाया इत्—जायैव गृहं (*asyaṃte kṣipyaṃte padārthā atretyastaṃ gṛhaṃ | padārthagaḷu yāva sthaḷagaḷalli iḍalpaḍuttavèyo ā sthaḷakkè gṛhavu èṃdarè astaṃ èṃba śabdakkè manèyèṃdu bhāṣyakāraru arthavivaraṇè māḍiddārè. jāyā it—jāyaiva gṛhaṃ*) is not used here; I give the sense only: the commentator has explained the word *astam* as "house": the place where things are put is called a house (*gṛha*).
जाया इत् (*jāyā it*)—जायैव गृहं भवति (*jāyaiva gṛhaṃ bhavati*). *Jāyā* means the wife or the housewife. Explaining the word *gṛha* to mean that for a man his own wife, or the place where his wife is, is regarded as the house, he gives in support of it the smṛti sentence न गृहं गृहमित्याहुर्गृहिणी गृहमुच्यते (*na gṛhaṃ gṛhamityāhurgṛhiṇī gṛhamucyate*) as an example. *Gṛha* does not mean a house or building built for dwelling. For a householder his own wife, or the place where the wife is, is called the *gṛha*. The sense of this sentence is that a house without a wife is not called a house. Therefore it is indicated that the role of the wife in the householder's home is of the greatest importance.
सा इत् योनिः उ (*sā it yoniḥ u*)—सा जायेव पुरुषस्य योनिः मिश्रणस्थानं (*sā jāyeva puruṣasya yoniḥ miśraṇasthānaṃ*). That wife is for the man the place of origin of pleasure and pain; "this is well known" means that a man obtains happiness only from his wife, and that in many circumstances, while carrying out household duties and so on, sorrow also arises. It is for this reason that the commentator has explained the word *yoni* as *miśraṇasthāna*, "a place together with happiness and sorrow". The sense is that this matter is well known, that is, known to everyone.

### Printed p. 181
तत् इत् त्वा युक्ताः हरयः वहंतु (*tat it tvā yuktāḥ harayaḥ vahaṃtu*)—therefore let the yoked bay horses carry you there. तस्मात्तत्र गृह एव रथे योजयिता हरयोऽश्वास्तां वहंतु । (*tasmāttatra gṛha eva rathe yojayitā harayo 'śvāstāṃ vahaṃtu |*). For that very reason, may your horses bring you in the chariot to such a house. "House" here should be understood as the sacrificial house of the sacrificer (yajamāna). Just as a man comes eagerly to the house where his housewife or wife is, so the sacrificer's sacrificial house, where the sacrifice or Soma juice dear to Indra like a wife stands ready, is welcome to him. The ṛṣi prays that Indra's horses bring Indra in the chariot to such a sacrificial house.
यदा कदा च सोमं सुनवाम अग्निः त्वा दूतः अच्छ धन्वति । (*yadā kadā ca somaṃ sunavāma agniḥ tvā dūtaḥ accha dhanvati |*)—O Indra, whenever we prepare Soma juice for you, you must come toward our sacrificial house; for that Agni, as messenger, comes toward you (to you). This is the main purport of this sentence.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> परा॑ याहि मघव॒न्ना च॑ या॒हींद्र॑ भ्रातरुभ॒यत्रा॑ते॒ अर्थं॑।  
> यत्रा॒ रथ॑स्य बृह॒तो नि॒धानं॑ वि॒मोच॑िनं वा॒जिनो॒ रास॑भस्य ॥

> *parā yāhi maghavannā ca yāhīṃdra bhrātarubhayatrāte arthaṃ|*  
> *yatrā rathasya bṛhato nidhānaṃ vimocinaṃ vājino rāsabhasya ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> परा । याहि । मघवन् । आ । च । याहि । इंद्र । भ्रातः । उभयत्र । ते । अर्थं ।  
> यत्र । रथस्य । बृहतः । निधानं । विमोचनं । वाजिनः । रासभस्य ॥ ५ ॥

> *parā | yāhi | maghavan | ā | ca | yāhi | iṃdra | bhrātaḥ | ubhayatra | te | arthaṃ |*  
> *yatra | rathasya | bṛhataḥ | nidhānaṃ | vimocanaṃ | vājinaḥ | rāsabhasya ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अहर्गणेऽंत्यवर्जितेष्वहःसु हारियोजनस्य वैकल्पिकी परा याहीत्यनुवाक्या । परा याहि मघवन्ना च याहीति वानुवाक्येति सूत्रितं । आ. ६-११ ॥
> हे मघवन्थनवन्निंद्र त्वं परा याहि । अस्माद्वेदयजनात्पराज्मुखो गृहं
> प्रति गच्छ । अथवा देवयजनं प्रत्यायाहि । हे भ्रातः पोषकोभयत्रापि ते
> तवार्थं अर्यते प्राप्यत इत्यर्थः प्रयोजनं । अस्ति तत्र जायात्र सोमः !
> प्रयोजनांतरमाह । यत्र गृहं प्रति गमने बृहतो महतो रथस्योपरि निधानमवस्थानं प्रयोजनं । अत्र स्थितौ रासभस्य हेषारवं कुर्वतो वाजिनोऽश्वस्य

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

On the ahargaṇa, on the days other than the last, the verse परा याहि (*parā yāhi*) is an optional anuvākyā of the Hāriyojana; it is prescribed by the sūtra "परा याहि मघवन्ना च याहीति वानुवाक्या (*parā yāhi maghavannā ca yāhīti vānuvākyā*)" (Ā. Sū. 6-11). "O wealthy Indra, go away: turn your back on this place of the Veda-sacrifice and go to your house; or else come to the divine sacrificial place. O brother, nourisher: in both cases there is a purpose (artha: what is reached, a use) for you. There (at home) is the wife; here is the Soma." He then states the other purpose: in going home, the purpose is the standing (nidhāna, remaining) upon the great chariot; and if he stays here, the purpose is the unyoking from the chariot of the neighing horse (vājin), which makes a braying/neighing sound (rāsabha)…

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O wealthy one
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- परा (*parā*)—away from the sacrificial house
- याहि (*yāhi*)—you may go (or)
- आ याहि (*ā yāhi*)—come to this sacrificial house
- भ्रातः (*bhrātaḥ*)—O Indra, the nourisher
- ते (*te*)—your
- अर्थं (*arthaṃ*)—purpose
- उभयत्र (*ubhayatra*)—is served in both ways
- यत्र (*yatra*)—by your act of going
- बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)—great
- रथस्य (*rathasya*)—upon the chariot
- निधानं (*nidhānaṃ*)—your placing (becomes the purpose) (if you go home)
- रासभस्य (*rāsabhasya*)—your neighing
- वाजिनः (*vājinaḥ*)—of the horse
- विमोचनं (*vimocanaṃ*)—release (from the chariot) (becomes the second purpose)

### Printed p. 182

> रथाद्विमोचनं प्रयोजनं ॥ अर्थं । ऋ गतौ । उषिकुषिगार्तिभ्यस्थन्निति थन् ।
> नित्स्वरः । निधानं । दधातेर्भावे ल्युट् । लितीति प्रत्ययात्पूर्वस्योदात्तत्वं ।
> रासभस्य । रासृ शब्दे । रासिवल्लिभ्यां च । उ. ३-१२५ । इति कर्तर्यभच्‌प्रत्ययः ।
> ग्रामादित्वादाद्युदात्तः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The release from the chariot is the purpose. (The rest of the block gives the grammar of अर्थं (*arthaṃ*), निधानं (*nidhānaṃ*) and रासभस्य (*rāsabhasya*); रासभस्य (*rāsabhasya*) is derived from the root रासृ शब्दे (*rāsṛ śabde*).)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O wealthy Indra, whether you depart from this sacrificial house or come here, there is a twofold purpose in both courses of action. By departing from here, your being seated in your chariot is one purpose. Or, if you stay here, the release of your neighing horse is another purpose.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Depart, Maghavan ; come Indra : both ways, protector, there is a motive for you, whether it be standing in your vast chariot, or liberating your neighing steed.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

On the day before the last day of the sacrifice called ahargaṇa, in the rite called Hāriyojana, this ṛk "Parā yāhi" may optionally be recited as the anuvākyā mantra; this is stated by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, "परा याहि मघवन्ना च याहीति वानुवाक्या (*parā yāhi maghavannā ca yāhīti vānuvākyā*)" (Ā. Sū. 6-11).
भ्रातः (*bhrātaḥ*)—For this word, derived from the root भृञ् भरणे (*bhṛñ bharaṇe*), the commentator gives the meaning "Indra the nourisher". Or the meaning "brother" belonging to the word भ्रातृ (*bhrātṛ*) may also be taken. It should be understood that out of great regard Indra is addressed as brother.

### Printed p. 183
रासभस्य (*rāsabhasya*)—रासृ शब्दे (*rāsṛ śabde*). हेषारवं कुर्वतः वाजिनः (*heṣāravaṃ kurvataḥ vājinaḥ*). The commentator says that this word is derived from the root रासृ (*rāsṛ*), meaning "to make a sound", and therefore explains the word as "of the horse that makes a sound, or that neighs".
In this ṛk it is explained that Indra (after coming to the sacrificial ground and drinking Soma) may stay right here, that is, on the sacrificial ground, if he wishes; otherwise he may depart to his own abode. In both these courses there is a purpose. How? If Indra goes back toward his own house, he will sit in his great chariot; our seeing this is the purpose. Or, if Indra stays here, then the horse harnessed to the chariot is unyoked and let loose, not yoked to the chariot; it will neigh with joy. Thus the ṛṣi has explained the purpose that lies in Indra's coming and returning.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अपाः॒ सोम॒मस्त॑मिंद्र॒ प्र या॑हि कल्या॒णीर्जा॒या सु॒रणं॑ ॒ ॑
> गृहे ते ।  
> यत्रा॒ रथ॑स्य बृह॒तो नि॒धानं॑ वि॒मोच॑नं वा॒जिनो॒ दक्षि॑णावत् ॥ ६ ॥

> *apāḥ somamastamiṃdra pra yāhi kalyāṇīrjāyā suraṇaṃ*  
> *gṛhe te |*  
> *yatrā rathasya bṛhato nidhānaṃ vimocanaṃ vājino dakṣiṇāvat ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अपाः । सोमं । अस्तं । इंद्र । प्र । याहि । कल्याणीः । जाया । सुरणं । गृहे ।  
> ते ।  
> यत्र । रथस्य । बृहतः । निधानं । विमोचनं । वाजिनः । दक्षिणावत् ॥ ६ ॥

> *apāḥ | somaṃ | astaṃ | iṃdra | pra | yāhi | kalyāṇīḥ | jāyā | suraṇaṃ | gṛhe |*  
> *te |*  
> *yatra | rathasya | bṛhataḥ | nidhānaṃ | vimocanaṃ | vājinaḥ | dakṣiṇāvat ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अंत्येष्वहःसु हारियोजनस्यापाः सोममित्यनुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च । हारियोजनोऽपाः सोममस्तमिंद्र प्र याहि । आ. ६-११ । इति ॥
> हे इंद्र सोममपाः । अत्रैव स्थित्वा सोमं पिब । पीत्वास्तं गृहं प्र
> याहि । प्रकर्षेण गच्छ । ते तव गृहे कल्याणीर्मंगलकारिणी जाया शची विद्यते ।
> सुरणं शोभनगीतादिध्वनिश्चास्ति । यद्वा ।सुरणं सुरमणीयं यथा भवति तथा गृहे

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

On the last days, अपाः सोमं (*apāḥ somaṃ*) is the anuvākyā of the Hāriyojana; it is prescribed by the sūtra "हारियोजनोऽपाः सोममस्तमिंद्र प्र याहि (*hāriyojano 'pāḥ somamastamiṃdra pra yāhi*)" (Ā. Sū. 6-11). "O Indra, drink the Soma: staying right here, drink Soma. Having drunk, go to your house (astam), go forth. In your house there is your auspicious wife Śacī, and there is also the sound of fine songs and the like. Or else: your wife stays in the house in such a way that it is delightful to the gods (surana)…
### Printed p. 184

> जाया तिष्ठति । यत्रेत्यादि गतं । दक्षिणावत् । अत्र स्थितौ सत्यां रथे योजितानां
> वाजिनां विमोचनं खादनपानादिना दक्षिणावत् प्रयोजनवद्भवति । विमुक्तेभ्यो
> वाजिभ्यो खादनं पानं चोपहरंति ॥ आपाः । पा पाने । लुजि गातिस्थेति सिचो
> लुक् । अडागमस्वरः । कल्याणेः । कल्याणमस्यास्तीति मत्वर्थे छंदसीवनिपाविति
> ईप्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । सुरणं । रण शब्दार्थः । आस्मादकृच्छार्थे खल् ।
> लित्स्वरः । प्रत्ययस्य पित्तादनुदात्तत्वे धातुस्वरः । समासे कृदुत्तरपदस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

…The rest of the sentence is as above ("yatra" etc., already treated). दक्षिणावत् (*dakṣiṇāvat*): if you stay here, the unyoking of the horses yoked to the chariot becomes fruitful (dakṣiṇāvat, "with a reward") through their eating, drinking and so on; for to the released horses they bring food and drink. (The rest of the block is grammar of आपाः (*āpāḥ*), कल्याणेः (*kalyāṇeḥ*) and सुरणं (*suraṇaṃ*).)

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma
- अपाः (*apāḥ*)—drink (afterwards) to your house (astam)
- प्र याहि (*pra yāhi*)—go
- ते (*te*)—your
- गृहे (*gṛhe*)—in the house
- कल्याणीः (*kalyāṇīḥ*)—auspicious
- जाया (*jāyā*)—your wife
- सुरणं (*suraṇaṃ*)—is delightful to you
- बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)—great
- रथस्य (*rathasya*)—upon the chariot
- निधानं (*nidhānaṃ*)—your placing
- दक्षिणावत् (*dakṣiṇāvat*)—becomes beneficial (or, if you stay here)
- वाजिनः (*vājinaḥ*)—of the horse
- विमोचनं (*vimocanaṃ*)—there is release (from the chariot)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, first drink the Soma. Afterwards go to your house. In your house your auspicious wife is a delight to you. Your being placed upon the great chariot is beneficial. Or, if you stay here, the horse is released from your chariot.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
When you have drunk the Soma, then, Indra, go home : an auspicious life (abides) pleasantly in your dwelling : in either (case) there is the standing in your car or liberating the steeds for provender.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

On the last day of the sacrifice called ahargaṇa this ṛk "Āpāḥ somaṃ" is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra; this is stated by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, "हारियोजनापाः सोममस्तमिंद्र (*hāriyojanāpāḥ somamastamiṃdra*)" (Ā. Sū. 6-11).
In this ṛk too the matter stated in the previous ṛk is set out in detail. And there is also a description that in Indra's house Indra's wife welcomes him with the sound of auspicious songs.
इंद्र सोममपाः अस्तं प्र याहि (*iṃdra somamapāḥ astaṃ pra yāhi*)—O Indra, drink Soma on our sacrificial ground; after drinking, set out toward your own house. As was said in the 4th ṛk, here too the word अस्तं (*astaṃ*) is explained as "house".

### Printed p. 185
कल्याणी जाया ते गृहे सुरणं (*kalyāṇī jāyā te gṛhe suraṇaṃ*)—तव गृहे मंगलकारिणी तव जाया (शची) विद्यते । शोभनगीतादिध्वनिश्चास्ति । (*tava gṛhe maṃgalakāriṇī tava jāyā (śacī) vidyate | śobhanagītādidhvaniścāsti |*) In your house there is your wife (Śacī), shining delightfully, that is, an ornament to the house; and there is the sound of the sweet songs and the like that she sings to welcome you; that is, it is heard. This is the purport.
यत्र रथस्य........दक्षिणावत् (*yatra rathasya........dakṣiṇāvat*)—What was said in the latter half of the previous ṛk is said again here. If Indra departs, he sits in his great chariot and gives darśana to all; if he does not depart but stays here, he has the horses of his chariot unyoked and sees that food and drink are provided for them: these two kinds of purpose are there, the ṛṣi says once more.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मे भो॒जा अंगि॑रसो॒ विरू॑पा दि॒वस्पु॒त्रासो॒ असु॑रस्य  ॒
> वीराः ।  
> वि॒श्वामि॑त्राय॒ दद॑तो म॒घानि॑ सहस्रसा॒वे प्र ति॑रंत॒ आयुः॑॥

> *ime bhojā aṃgiraso virūpā divasputrāso asurasya*  
> *vīrāḥ |*  
> *viśvāmitrāya dadato maghāni sahasrasāve pra tiraṃta āyuḥ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमे । भोजाः । अंगिरसः । विरूपाः । दिवः । पुत्रासः । असुरस्य । वीराः ।  
> विश्वामित्राय । ददतः । मघानि । सहस्रसावे । प्र । तिरंते । आयुः ॥ ७ ॥

> *ime | bhojāḥ | aṃgirasaḥ | virūpāḥ | divaḥ | putrāsaḥ | asurasya | vīrāḥ |*  
> *viśvāmitrāya | dadataḥ | maghāni | sahasrasāve | pra | tiraṃte | āyuḥ ‖ 7 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र इमे यागं कुर्वाणा भोजाः सौदासाः क्षत्रियाः तेषां याजका
> विरूपा नानारूपा मेधातिथिप्रभृतयोंगिरसश्च दिवोऽसुरस्य देवेभ्योऽपि बलवतो रुद्रस्य पुत्रासः पुत्रा वीरा बलवंतो मरुतः ते सर्वे विश्वामित्राय मह्यं
> सहस्रसावे । सहस्रं सूयतेऽत्रेति सहस्रसावोऽश्वमेधः । तस्मिन्नश्वमेधे मघानि
> महनीयानि धनानि वदतः प्रयच्छंतः संत आयुर्जीवनमन्नं ना प्र तिरंते ।
> प्रकर्षण वर्धयंति ॥ दिवस्पुत्रास इति संहितायां षष्ठ्याः पतिपुत्रेति विसर्जनीयस्य सत्वं । दवतः । डुदाण् दान इत्यस्य शतरि रूपं । अभ्यस्तानामादिरित्याद्युदात्तत्वं । तिरंते । तरतेर्व्यत्ययेन शः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, these Bhojas who perform the sacrifice are Saudāsa kṣatriyas; their priests are the Aṅgirases of diverse forms, Medhātithi and others; and the sons (putrāsaḥ) of the asura Rudra, who is stronger even than the gods, are the heroic, mighty Maruts. All these, giving to me, Viśvāmitra, at the sahasrasāva (the Aśvamedha, in which a thousand are pressed), magnificent riches (maghāni), prolong life, that is, living and food, and make them increase." (The rest of the block is grammar: the visarjanīya of दिवस्पुत्रास (*divasputrāsa*) in the Saṃhitā, ददतः (*dadataḥ*), तिरंते (*tiraṃte*).)

### Printed p. 186

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) इमे (*ime*)—the sacrificers who perform the sacrifice here
- भोजाः (*bhojāḥ*)—sons of the kṣatriya named Sudāsa
- विरूपाः (*virūpāḥ*)—of diverse forms, Medhātithi and others
- अंगिरसः (*aṃgirasaḥ*)—the Aṅgirases (are the priests of these sacrificers)
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—than the gods
- असुरस्य (*asurasya*)—of Rudra, who has greater strength
- पुत्रासः (*putrāsaḥ*)—sons
- वीराः (*vīrāḥ*)—the Maruts, endowed with strength
- विश्वामित्राय (*viśvāmitrāya*)—to me, Viśvāmitra
- सहस्रसावे (*sahasrasāve*)—in this Aśvamedha sacrifice
- मघानि (*maghāni*)—riches
- ददतः (*dadataḥ*)—giving
- आयुः (*āyuḥ*)—life
- प्रति तिरंते (*prati tiraṃte*)—they increase

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, the sacrificers who perform the sacrifice here are the sons of the kṣatriya named Sudāsa. The Aṅgirases of diverse forms, Medhātithi and others, are priests (ṛtvik) of this sacrificer. The Maruts, sons of Rudra, who has greater strength than the gods, and endowed with strength, giving wealth and riches to me, Viśvāmitra, in this Aśvamedha sacrifice, increase my life.**NOTE**

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
These (sacrificers) are the Bhojas, of whom the diversified Angirasas (are the priests) : and the heroic sons of the expeller (of the foes of the gods) from heaven, bestowing riches upon Vishwamitra at the sacrifice of a thousand (victims), prolong (his) life.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

भोजाः (*bhojāḥ*)—सौदासः क्षत्रियाः (*saudāsaḥ kṣatriyāḥ*). Sāyaṇa explains this as "sons of the king named Sudāsa". Sudāsa is the son of a well-known king named Pijavana. In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa it is explained that there was a king named Sudāsa in the Solar dynasty, that another king of the same name was in the Lunar dynasty, and that another king of the same name was in the Lunar dynasty as son of the king Divodāsa.**NOTE**. The name of Divodāsa is mentioned in many places in the Ṛgveda.
Concerning the king Sudāsa, the commentator, in explaining Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-112-9, has told an earlier story:
अत्रापीतिहासमाचक्षते । सुदासनामा पैजवनो राजा यस्य विश्वामित्रः पुरोहितो बभूव । स यष्टु कामः सारवत्प्रभूतं धनमश्विन् ययाचे। तस्मै रथेनानीयाश्विस्सौ ददुतुरिति ॥ (*atrāpītihāsamācakṣate | sudāsanāmā paijavano rājā yasya viśvāmitraḥ purohito babhūva | sa yaṣṭu kāmaḥ sāravatprabhūtaṃ dhanamaśvin yayāce| tasmai rathenānīyāśvissau daduturiti ‖*)
